# Functional-Parity Feature Checklist — Clean-Room Reimplementation of the Claude Code Observable Experience

**Document type:** implementation planning specification (planning documentation only).
**Status:** baseline for a multi-year engineering programme. Every row is a testable contract.
**Reference product observed:** Claude Code CLI v2.1.306 (commit `01ae8327ca99`, win32-x64) — observed from the outside only.
**Target:** an independent, clean-room reimplementation whose *observable* behaviour (CLI surface, event protocol, file effects, safety refusals, extension points) matches the reference.

---

## 1. Scope

### 1.1 What is in scope

This checklist covers the complete **externally observable** surface of a terminal coding agent:

* the command-line entrypoint surface, argument validation, exit codes and output formats;
* the interactive terminal experience (input contexts, key bindings, prompt queue, panels, footer, transcript viewer);
* the project-context layer (directory anchoring, instruction files, repository state, memory);
* the file subsystem (read, search, edit, patch, diff review) and its refusal behaviour;
* the planning / task-decomposition / multi-step execution loop;
* controlled terminal execution (shell tools, background execution, output-size ladders);
* the permission engine (six modes, rule syntax, approval surface, critical-path circuit breakers, sandbox);
* error diagnosis and the model-driven repair loop;
* MCP integration and deferred tool discovery;
* skills, slash commands, plugins and reusable instruction bundles;
* delegated work (subagents, agent teams, dynamic workflows, worktree isolation);
* context management, compaction, persistence, resumption;
* progress reporting, the status/statusline surfaces and the general UX;
* model selection, inference configuration and provider abstraction;
* the audit, test-harness and reproducibility requirements of the above;
* REA-based reverse-engineering workflows as a first-class investigation capability.

### 1.2 What is out of scope

* The reference implementation's source code, its internal architecture, or any decompilation of it.
* Any reproduction of Anthropic-authored *prompt prose*. This document describes contracts, not wording.
* Cloud-only surfaces that cannot be reproduced without a vendor account (hosted IDE bridges, vendor-hosted review services, account-scoped connectors) are described only as *capability gates*.
* Credential and keychain mechanics were deliberately not investigated and are represented here only as an interface boundary.

### 1.3 How to read this document

| Convention | Meaning |
| :- | :- |
| `S3-FILE-014` | Unique checklist ID. Prefix = subsystem; word = feature family; digits = sequence within the family. IDs are stable and are the reference keys used by the dependency columns. |
| `agent-evidence.md §4 Area 3 F3-4` | Evidence pointer. Every row that is not an original design decision cites a specific source file and section, line, or finding ID. |
| `ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION` | The literal label used when the reference behaviour was **not** observed or could only be inferred. Such rows are proposals by this document, not claims about the reference. Rows marked `INFERRED` are additionally flagged inside the evidence cell. |
| P0 / P1 / P2 | Priority band: **P0** = parity-core (a reimplementation without it is not recognisably the same product); **P1** = extensibility (the surface exists and is coherent, but a missing implementation degrades rather than breaks); **P2** = safety-tier (controls whose absence is a security or correctness risk, and which may be implemented after the parity core but must ship before unattended operation). |
| Phase 1..5 | Implementation phase. Phase 1 = foundations and the read-only loop; Phase 2 = the mutation loop and permissions; Phase 3 = extensibility (MCP, skills, plugins); Phase 4 = delegation and scale; Phase 5 = polish, parity hardening and the remaining breadth. |
| "Depends on" | Other checklist IDs, or `none`. A dependency is a *build-order* dependency, not a runtime call dependency. |
| Module + interface | A proposed module name and a public interface shape at the level of a function, class or tool signature. The intent is to make the contract implementable without re-deciding its shape. |
| Verification test | The automated test that proves the row. Named so it can be written directly into a test suite. |
| Risk + mitigation | The security or operational failure mode and the shape of the mitigation. |

Cells are kept terse. Where a cell would be unreadable as a table cell, the feature is expanded as a **definition-list block** immediately beneath the table, using the same eight attributes.

**Total: 398 feature rows** across 15 subsystems.

**Reading order for a team:** S1 → S2 → S3 → S5 → S6 → S7 form the single-thread parity core (Phase 1–2). S4, S11 and S13 are the model-loop spine (Phase 1–2). S8, S9, S10 and S12 are the extensibility tier (Phase 3–4). S14 and S15 are the assurance tier (Phase 4–5).
