## S1 — Interactive CLI & session handling

**Priority band:** P0 parity-core.

**Evidence base.** `agent-evidence.md` §4 Area 1 (session identity, launch modes, print-mode envelopes, stream-json protocol, turn loop, resumption) and §4 X1 (headless findings H1–H23); `agent-reference-shots/areas/area6-tests-errors.md` §7b.3 (H1–H23 headless findings); `agent-reference-shots/area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md` §2 (F-I1…F-I18, the interactive/TUI surface); `agent-reference-shots/PROTOCOL-NOTES.md` (init-event field table and tool-set mutation matrix); `agent-reference-shots/print-mode-json-result.json` (the result envelope); `agent-reference-shots/cli-reference/claude-help-full.txt` (flag surface).

**Scope note.** S1 owns the *shell* of the product: how a session starts, how input is captured, how the harness emits events, and how a session ends. The content of a turn (tools, permissions, files) belongs to S3, S5 and S6.
| ID | Evidence | Acceptance criterion (testable) | Depends on | Proposed module + interface shape | Automated verification test | Security / operational risk + mitigation | Priority / Phase |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| S1-CLI-001 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F1; docs-cache/headless.md (H1); area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H1 | The CLI exposes a fixed set of launch modes (interactive, `--print`/`-p`, `--output-format`, `--input-format`, `--session-id`, `--resume`, `--continue`, `--bare`, `--restricted`, `--add-dir`, `--mcp-config`, `--settings`, `--permission-mode`, `--model`, `--agents`, `--allowed-tools`/`--tools`) and rejects an invalid combination with exit code 1 before any model request is issued. | none | `cli/launcher.py :: parse_argv(argv) -> LaunchPlan`; `LaunchPlan` carries `mode`, `output_format`, `input_format`, `session_id`, `resume_key`, `extra_dirs`, `settings_sources`, `tool_allowlist`, `permission_mode`, `model`, `agents`. `cli/validate.py :: validate(plan) -> list[ValidationError]` raising `CliValidationError`. | `test_cli_launch_matrix.py::test_invalid_flag_combination_exits_1_without_model_call` — builds each documented invalid pair, asserts exit code 1, asserts a stdout marker `error:` and asserts the injected fake provider recorded zero requests. | Rejecting late (after a request) wastes a paid call and can execute a tool before the operator learns the invocation was invalid. Mitigation: validation is a pure function over the parsed plan, run before provider construction. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S1-CLI-002 | area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H1; agent-evidence.md §4 X1 | `-p`/`--print` is the single switch from interactive to non-interactive: with it the process reads the prompt from stdin or the argument, emits no interactive chrome, and terminates when the turn loop ends; without it the process enters the interactive renderer. | S1-CLI-001 | `cli/launcher.py :: resolve_mode(plan) -> Literal['interactive','print']`; `runtime/session.py :: Session.run(mode)` branching once at entry. | `test_launch_modes.py::test_print_mode_never_opens_interactive_renderer` — run with a pty-less stdin and assert the renderer factory was not constructed and the transcript contains no `system/init` interactive-only fields beyond the documented set. | A half-interactive headless run can hang waiting for a keypress in CI. Mitigation: the print path never reads the TTY; stdin EOF terminates the turn. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S1-CLI-003 | area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H2, H3; agent-evidence.md §4 X1 | `--output-format` accepts exactly `text`, `json`, `stream-json`; any other value is a validation error (exit 1). The flag is only meaningful in print mode and is rejected in interactive mode. | S1-CLI-001, S1-CLI-002 | `cli/validate.py :: validate_output_format(value, mode) -> None` raising `CliValidationError`; `output/format.py :: OutputFormat` enum with `TEXT`, `JSON`, `STREAM_JSON`. | `test_output_format.py::test_invalid_output_format_rejected` and `::test_output_format_rejected_in_interactive_mode` — assert exit 1 and a message naming the accepted set. | A silently-defaulted format makes a CI pipeline parse the wrong thing. Mitigation: hard validation, never a default fallback for an unrecognised value. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S1-CLI-004 | area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H3; agent-reference-shots/print-mode-json-result.json | With `--output-format json` the process writes exactly one JSON object to stdout — the result envelope — and nothing else; the object parses with a standard JSON parser with no trailing bytes. | S1-CLI-003, S1-RESULT-011 | `output/writer.py :: write_json(envelope: ResultEnvelope) -> None`; `protocol/envelope.py :: ResultEnvelope` dataclass with `to_dict()` that emits keys in a fixed order and omits unset optional fields rather than emitting null. | `test_json_output.py::test_json_format_emits_single_parseable_object` — capture stdout, assert `json.loads(stdout)` succeeds, assert exactly one top-level value, assert the set of present keys equals the documented set. | Extra bytes on stdout break `ConvertFrom-Json`/jq pipelines (observed limitation L5). Mitigation: the writer owns stdout exclusively in this mode; diagnostics go to stderr. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S1-CLI-005 | area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H4; agent-evidence.md §4 X1 | With `--output-format stream-json` every event is one JSON object per line (newline-delimited), in emission order, and the final line is the result envelope; a consumer that reads lines sees no embedded newline inside any event. | S1-CLI-003, S1-RESULT-011 | `output/writer.py :: write_stream(event: StreamEvent) -> None` — serialises with `json.dumps(..., separators=(',',':'))` and appends a single `\n`; `runtime/loop.py` guarantees the envelope is emitted last via a `finally` block. | `test_stream_json.py::test_each_line_is_one_json_object` and `::test_final_line_is_result_envelope` — split stdout on newlines, parse each line, assert the last parsed object has `type == 'result'`. | A non-atomic write can interleave two events on one line under concurrency. Mitigation: writes are serialised through a single writer with an internal lock and are flushed per line. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S1-CLI-006 | PROTOCOL-NOTES.md (init event field table); area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H5; agent-reference-shots/area-headless-programmatic/stream-json-events.jsonl | The first event of a `stream-json` run is `{"type":"system","subtype":"init",...}` and carries the whole session manifest: `cwd`, `original_cwd`, `session_id`, `tools`, `mcp_servers`, `model`, `permissionMode`, `slash_commands`, `terminal_slash_commands`, `apiKeySource`, `claude_code_version`, `output_style`, `agents`, `skills`, `plugins`, `capabilities`, `analytics_disabled`, `product_feedback_disabled`, `uuid`, `memory_paths`, `additional_directories`, `messaging_socket_path`, `fast_mode_state`, `fast_mode_disabled_reason`, `per_turn_effort_active`, `view_mode`, `powershell_path`. | S1-CLI-005 | `protocol/events.py :: InitEvent` dataclass; `runtime/session.py :: build_init_event(ctx) -> InitEvent`. | `test_init_event.py::test_init_event_field_set` — assert the emitted key set equals the documented set exactly; `::test_init_is_first_event` — assert index 0 of the event log is the init event. | A client that depends on a field that silently disappears breaks on upgrade. Mitigation: the field set is versioned and asserted by a golden test; additions are additive only. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S1-CLI-007 | area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H6 | `--input-format stream-json` accepts newline-delimited user messages on stdin for the lifetime of the process; each line is one user turn and the session keeps running across lines until EOF. | S1-CLI-005 | `input/reader.py :: InputReader.read() -> Iterable[UserTurn]` with a `TextInputReader` and a `StreamJsonInputReader` implementation; the loop pulls from the iterator. | `test_input_stream.py::test_multiple_user_turns_in_one_process` — feed three lines, assert three assistant responses and a final envelope with `num_turns == 3`. | Unbounded stdin can exhaust memory. Mitigation: the reader is a generator and the loop processes one turn at a time; stdin size is capped per line. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S1-CLI-008 | area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H7 | Flag-combination validation is strict, occurs before any model call, and exits 1 with a message naming the offending combination. | S1-CLI-001 | `cli/validate.py :: validate(plan) -> None` — collects *all* errors and reports them together rather than failing on the first. | `test_cli_validation.py::test_validation_precedes_model_call` — assert provider.request_count == 0 on a failing combination; `::test_multiple_errors_reported_together` — assert two distinct offending flags are both named in stderr. | Reporting only the first error costs a round-trip of operator time. Mitigation: error collection is a list, rendered in a stable order. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S1-CLI-009 | area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H8 | `--json-schema` supplied with `--output-format json` produces a `structured_output` field on the result envelope that validates against the supplied schema; the field is optional and may be absent. | S1-CLI-003, S1-RESULT-011 | `protocol/envelope.py :: ResultEnvelope.structured_output: Optional[dict]`; `runtime/structured.py :: parse_structured(text, schema) -> dict` with JSON-schema validation delegated to a `SchemaValidator` protocol. | `test_structured_output.py::test_structured_output_matches_supplied_schema` — validate the emitted object against the schema with a JSON-schema validator; `::test_absent_when_no_schema` — assert the key is absent (not null) when no schema was supplied. | Emitting a null instead of an absent key breaks strict consumers. Mitigation: unset optional fields are omitted, never null. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S1-RESULT-011 | agent-reference-shots/print-mode-json-result.json; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F6 | The result envelope exposes the documented field set: `type:'result'`, `subtype`, `is_error`, `result`, `num_turns`, `session_id`, `uuid`, `duration_ms`, `duration_api_ms`, `ttft_ms`, `ttft_stream_ms`, `time_to_request_ms`, `first_content_frame_ms`, `stop_reason`, `api_error_status`, `total_cost_usd`, `usage` (with `service_tier`, `cache_creation.ephemeral_1h`, `cache_creation.5m`), `modelUsage` (per model: `inputTokens`, `outputTokens`, `cacheReadInputTokens`, `cacheCreationInputTokens`, `webSearchRequests`, `costUSD`, `contextWindow`, `maxOutputTokens`, `thinkingTokens`, `canonicalModel`, `provider`, `costBasis`), `permission_denials`, `terminal_reason`, `fast_mode_state`, `fast_mode_disabled_reason`, `per_turn_effort_active`, `subagent_stats`, `safety_stops`, `queued_turn_count`, `result_index`. | S1-CLI-005 | `protocol/envelope.py :: ResultEnvelope` (frozen dataclass) + `to_dict()`; `protocol/envelope.py :: ModelUsage` and `SubagentStats` dataclasses. | `test_result_envelope.py::test_field_set_matches_contract` — assert the emitted key set equals the documented set; `::test_model_usage_is_per_model` — assert `modelUsage` has one entry per model used with the documented sub-keys. | A cost-accounting field that is present but wrong is worse than absent. Mitigation: cost fields are computed by a single `CostModel` used by both the envelope and the statusline, with a unit test against known prices. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S1-RESULT-012 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F6, §4 X1; area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H9 | Semantic failures are reported *in-band* on the envelope (`is_error`, `subtype`, `terminal_reason`) while the process exit code stays 0 for a set of in-band conditions; the exit code and the envelope never disagree about success without the envelope carrying the reason. | S1-RESULT-011, S1-CLI-011 | `protocol/envelope.py :: ResultEnvelope.is_error` and `terminal_reason`; `runtime/exit.py :: exit_code_for(envelope) -> int` with an explicit table of in-band conditions that map to 0. | `test_inband_failures.py::test_inband_failure_has_zero_exit` and `::test_exit_code_matches_envelope_terminal_reason` — table-driven over every documented terminal reason. | A pipeline that keys on the exit code alone silently treats a failed run as a success. Mitigation: the exit-code table is single-sourced and the envelope always carries the authoritative reason. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S1-CLI-013 | area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H9, H10; agent-evidence.md §6 L5 | Diagnostics that describe machine-readable state (unrecognised model, invalid settings, permission denials) are written to stderr, not stdout, so that stdout remains parseable in every output format. | S1-CLI-004, S1-CLI-005 | `output/writer.py :: write_diagnostic(msg) -> None` writing to stderr with a `[claude-code:<tag>]` prefix; stdout writers never emit diagnostics. | `test_stream_separation.py::test_stdout_is_pure_json_in_json_mode` — trigger a known diagnostic (unrecognised model id) and assert stdout still parses as one JSON object while stderr is non-empty. | Mixing streams breaks `ConvertFrom-Json` (observed L5). Mitigation: a single diagnostic sink with a hard rule that only result/stream writers touch stdout. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S1-SESSION-014 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F3; area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H11 | A session id is a UUIDv4; when the caller supplies `--session-id` the supplied value is used verbatim and is also the transcript file name, so two runs with the same id address the same session. | S1-SESSION-016 | `session/identity.py :: SessionId` (value type wrapping a UUID) with `SessionId.generate()` and `SessionId.from_str(raw)`; `session/identity.py :: SessionIdentity(session_id, project_key, resume_key)`. | `test_session_identity.py::test_supplied_session_id_is_used_verbatim` and `::test_two_handles_differ` — assert the session id and the project key are distinct values and that a supplied id survives into the transcript path. | A colliding or predictable session id allows one caller to address another's transcript. Mitigation: generated ids are UUIDv4; a supplied id is validated as a well-formed UUID before use. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S1-SESSION-015 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F2, F4; area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H11 | Project identity is derived from the working-directory path (sanitised into a directory name), never chosen explicitly; two different working directories map to different projects, and the same path always maps to the same project. | S1-SESSION-014, S2-PROJ-001 | `session/project.py :: project_key_for(cwd: Path) -> str` implementing the sanitisation rule (path separators and drive letters replaced, absolute path retained); `session/project.py :: ProjectKey` value type. | `test_project_key.py::test_same_path_same_key` and `::test_different_paths_differ` and `::test_key_is_path_derived` — assert no flag exists to set the project key and that the key contains no raw path separator. | Path-derived identity avoids a registry that could be poisoned to point at another project. Mitigation: the sanitisation is a pure function; no user-supplied alias may redirect the project key. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S1-SESSION-016 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F4, F5 | The transcript is an append-only JSONL file under a configurable config root (`~/.claude/projects/<sanitised-cwd>/<session-id>.jsonl` by default); every entry carries a typed discriminator (`mode`, `permission-mode`, `last-prompt`, `ai-title`, `cost-state`, `queue-operation`, `attachment`, `user`, `assistant`, `system`) and the file is never rewritten in place. | S1-SESSION-014, S1-SESSION-015 | `transcript/writer.py :: TranscriptWriter` opened in append mode; `transcript/entries.py :: TranscriptEntry` discriminated union keyed by `type`; `transcript/reader.py :: read(path) -> Iterator[TranscriptEntry]`. | `test_transcript.py::test_entries_are_typed_and_append_only` — run a session, assert the file only grows and every line parses to a known entry type; `::test_transcript_is_a_tree` — assert a fork produces two branches with a `logicalParentUuid`/parent link, not a linear list. | Rewriting a transcript in place destroys forensic history. Mitigation: append-only writer, no truncation path; a corrupted line is skipped with a diagnostic rather than aborting the read. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S1-SESSION-017 | area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H13 | stdin is the prompt channel in print mode, with a 10 MB cap; a stdin that cannot be read produces a warning and the run continues with an empty prompt rather than hanging. | S1-CLI-002 | `input/reader.py :: read_stdin(max_bytes=10*1024*1024) -> str` raising `StdinTooLarge`; `runtime/session.py` catching it and emitting a warning diagnostic. | `test_stdin.py::test_stdin_over_cap_is_rejected_with_warning` and `::test_unreadable_stdin_does_not_hang` — feed a non-blocking closed pipe and assert the process exits with a diagnostic, under a 5 s timeout. | Unbounded stdin is a denial-of-service vector for a service host. Mitigation: hard byte cap enforced while reading, not after. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S1-SESSION-018 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F9, §6 L19 | When stdin is not a TTY the interactive workspace-trust dialog is skipped; when stdin is a TTY the dialog is shown before the first turn. | S6-TRUST-030, S1-SESSION-017 | `trust/workspace.py :: requires_trust_dialog(stdin_is_tty: bool) -> bool`; `runtime/session.py` consults it before the first turn. | `test_trust_dialog.py::test_non_tty_skips_trust_dialog` — run with a pipe and assert no dialog event is emitted; `::test_tty_shows_dialog` — with a pty, assert the dialog event precedes the first model request. | A skipped trust prompt on a TTY would grant a hostile repository unattended access. Mitigation: the TTY test is on the stdin handle, not on an env var. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S1-SESSION-019 | PROTOCOL-NOTES.md (tool-set mutation matrix); area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H12 | `--bare` produces a deterministic, credential-free, project-content-free run: the tool set is exactly `Bash, Edit, PowerShell, Read`, `mcp_servers` is empty, and no project file content is injected. | S1-CLI-006, S1-TOOLSET-022 | `cli/flags.py :: BARE_TOOLSET = ('Bash','Edit','PowerShell','Read')`; `runtime/session.py :: apply_bare(plan)` clearing `mcp_servers`, `skills`, `agents`, `plugins` and instruction attachments. | `test_bare.py::test_bare_toolset_exact` and `::test_bare_injects_no_project_content` — assert the init event tool list equals the four names, `mcp_servers == []`, and that no instruction attachment appears in the transcript. | `--bare` is a foot-gun if it silently keeps credentials. Mitigation: assert at startup that no credential source is consulted in bare mode. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S1-TOOLSET-020 | PROTOCOL-NOTES.md (tool-set mutation matrix); agent-evidence.md §5.1 | `--tools ""` yields an empty tool set in the init event; `--restricted` yields the 21-tool reduced set (the default 28 minus `Bash`, `PowerShell`, `WebFetch`, `Monitor`) with `mcp_servers: []` and a pruned slash-command list. | S1-SESSION-019 | `tools/registry.py :: ToolRegistry` with `apply_flags(tools_spec, restricted: bool) -> list[str]`; `config/registry.py :: DEFAULT_TOOLS` and `RESTRICTED_TOOLS`. | `test_toolset.py::test_empty_tools_spec_yields_empty_set` and `::test_restricted_removes_exactly_four` and `::test_restricted_prunes_slash_commands` — assert the removed names and that the slash-command list is a strict subset. | An over-broad tool set in restricted mode defeats the restriction. Mitigation: the restricted set is computed by subtraction from a single source of truth, asserted by an exact-set test. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S1-TOOLSET-022 | PROTOCOL-NOTES.md; agent-reference-shots/print-mode-json-result.json | The full default tool set is 27–28 tools: `Task, Bash, CronCreate, CronDelete, CronList, DesignSync, Edit, EnterWorktree, ExitWorktree, Glob, Grep, ListAgents, Monitor, NotebookEdit, PowerShell, PushNotification, Read, ReportFindings, ScheduleWakeup, SendMessage, Skill, TaskStop, ToolSearch, WebFetch, WebSearch, Workflow, Write`; the init event publishes the set actually available to the session. | S1-TOOLSET-020 | `tools/registry.py :: ALL_TOOLS: tuple[ToolSpec, ...]` — a single ordered tuple; `runtime/session.py` filters it by mode, plan, model capability and permission rules before emitting the init event. | `test_tool_registry.py::test_default_tool_set_matches_contract` — assert the tuple equals the documented set; `::test_init_publishes_filtered_set` — under plan mode assert mutation tools are absent from the published list. | Publishing a tool the harness cannot dispatch causes a tool-not-found loop. Mitigation: the init event is generated from the same filtered list the dispatcher uses. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S1-SESSION-023 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F12; area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H11 | Background mode (`--bg`) is a supervisor-mediated, addressable session: the process can be resumed, its state inspected and its messages delivered, independently of an interactive terminal. | S1-SESSION-016, S1-SESSION-024 | `session/background.py :: BackgroundSession(id, state_path, socket_path)` with `start()`, `status()`, `send(text)`, `stop()`; `runtime/daemon.py :: Supervisor` owning session lifecycles. | `test_background.py::test_background_session_is_addressable` — start, exit the client, reconnect by id and assert pending messages are delivered; `::test_background_state_survives_restart`. | An orphan background session holds credentials and file handles. Mitigation: the supervisor reaps sessions without a live client after a documented idle window and records the reap in the audit log. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S1-SESSION-024 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F11; area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H11 | Resume, continue and fork are three distinct history operations: `--resume <id>` opens a named past session, `--continue` opens the most recent session for the current project, and fork creates a new session whose parent is the resumed one. | S1-SESSION-016, S1-SESSION-025 | `session/history.py :: list_sessions(project_key) -> list[SessionSummary]`, `:: resume(session_id)`, `:: continue_latest(project_key)`, `:: fork(session_id) -> SessionId`. | `test_history_ops.py::test_resume_continue_fork_are_distinct` — assert three different resulting session ids/parents for the three operations; `::test_fork_records_parent`. | A fork that shares a transcript file with its parent corrupts both. Mitigation: a fork writes a new transcript whose entries reference the parent id; the parent file is opened read-only. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S1-SESSION-025 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F13, F14 | The session's instruction set is snapshotted per conversation rather than rebuilt per request, and the *result* of startup discovery is published (as the init event) rather than re-discovered on each turn. | S1-CLI-006, S2-INSTR-004 | `runtime/session.py :: SessionContext` frozen at construction; `discovery/pipeline.py :: discover(config) -> DiscoveryResult` run once and published. | `test_snapshot.py::test_context_is_immutable_after_start` — attempt to mutate the context after the first turn and assert a frozen-dataclass error; `::test_discovery_runs_once` — count discovery calls across three turns. | Rebuilding per request causes the injected instruction set to drift mid-conversation, which is both a correctness and a prompt-stability problem. Mitigation: frozen context, explicit re-discovery entry points. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S1-INPUT-026 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I1; docs-cache/keybindings.md §Contexts | The input system is context-dispatched: a keystroke resolves to an action *within the active context*. Exactly 27 contexts are defined (`Global`, `Chat`, `Autocomplete`, `Settings`, `Confirmation`, `Tabs`, `Help`, `Transcript`, `HistorySearch`, `Task`, `ThemePicker`, `Attachments`, `Footer`, `MessageSelector`, `DiffDialog`, `DiffPanel`, `ModelPicker`, `EffortSlider`, `Select`, `Plugin`, `AbovePrompt`, `AbovePromptInput`, `AbovePromptSelect`, `Pane`, `PaneField`, `Agents`, `Scroll`) and the same physical key can have different meanings per context. | S1-INPUT-028 | `ui/keymap.py :: Keymap` with `resolve(context: str, key: KeyEvent) -> Optional[Action]`; `ui/contexts.py :: CONTEXTS: frozenset[str]`; `ui/actions.py :: Action` namespace of `context:action` string identifiers. | `test_keymap.py::test_context_resolution_is_ordered` — for each of a set of keys assert the action differs across at least two contexts; `::test_unknown_context_resolves_to_none`. | A flat single keymap cannot express the observed behaviour (Esc in a footer panel vs Esc interrupting a turn). Mitigation: the keymap is context-first by construction; a flat fallback is not provided. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S1-INPUT-027 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I1; docs-cache/keybindings.md §Configuration file | The keybinding vocabulary is user-editable in `~/.claude/keybindings.json` (an object with a `bindings` array of `{context, bindings}`), a binding value of `null` unbinds a default, a binding naming an unimplemented action stays valid rather than erroring, and changes are applied without a restart. | S1-INPUT-026 | `ui/keymap.py :: Keymap.load(path) -> Keymap` merging user bindings over defaults; `ui/keymap.py :: watch(path, on_change)` for hot reload. | `test_keybindings_file.py::test_null_binding_unbinds_default` and `::test_unknown_action_is_tolerated` and `::test_reload_without_restart` — write the file, assert the new mapping takes effect in the live session. | A malformed user keymap could break the input loop entirely. Mitigation: parse errors fall back to defaults with a warning; the file is never trusted for anything but key names. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S1-INPUT-028 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I4 | Text editing follows readline conventions with an exact word-boundary rule: word motions treat a word as a run of letters and digits (so `_`, `.`, `/` separate words), while `Ctrl+W` deletes back to the previous whitespace; `Ctrl+K`/`Ctrl+U`/`Ctrl+W` store the deleted text in a kill ring that `Ctrl+Y` pastes and `Alt+Y` cycles. These shortcuts are not remappable. | S1-INPUT-026 | `ui/editor.py :: LineEditor` with `word_start(text, pos) -> int` (Unicode letter/digit class) and `kill_ring: list[str]`; `ui/editor.py :: paste_history()`. | `test_editor.py::test_word_boundary_splits_on_punctuation` — `src/utils/foo.ts` yields the sequence `ts`, `foo`, `utils`, `src`; `::test_ctrl_w_removes_whole_path`; `::test_kill_ring_cycle`. | A non-Unicode-aware word rule deletes the wrong span in CJK text. Mitigation: word classification is by Unicode category, and CJK is handled as a code-point run. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S1-INPUT-029 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I3 | Five multiline input methods are supported — backslash+Enter, Option+Enter, Shift+Enter, `Ctrl+J`, and paste — with `Ctrl+J` as the universal fallback and `Ctrl+X Ctrl+S` as a chord fallback for terminals that do not report extended keys. | S1-INPUT-026 | `ui/editor.py :: MULTILINE_TRIGGERS: frozenset[KeyEvent]` — a *set*, not a single binding; `ui/editor.py :: submit()` returns `SubmitIntent` (submit vs newline) based on the trigger set. | `test_editor.py::test_each_trigger_inserts_newline` and `::test_chord_fallback_submits` — parameterise over the five triggers. | Hard-coding one trigger makes the product unusable on terminals that lack it. Mitigation: the trigger set is configurable and always contains at least one chord-composable member. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S1-QUEUE-030 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I2 | The prompt input is a queue with a per-entry kind (message / command / shell command). Messages queued during a turn are delivered to the model as soon as the current tool calls finish, within the same turn; commands and shell commands are held until the turn ends and then run one at a time in queue order. `Ctrl+Enter` sends queued messages immediately; `Up` from the first line takes queued entries back into the input box. | S1-INPUT-026, S1-INPUT-032 | `ui/queue.py :: PromptQueue` with `enqueue(entry: QueueEntry)`, `take_back() -> list[QueueEntry]`, `policy: DeliveryPolicy`; `ui/queue.py :: DeliveryPolicy.deliver(queue, activity: ActivityState) -> list[Delivery]`. | `test_queue.py::test_messages_deliver_within_turn` and `::test_commands_wait_for_turn_end` and `::test_take_back_restores_entries_in_order` and `::test_queue_is_grey_until_started`. | A take-back that mutates the input buffer and the queue non-atomically can lose a queued message. Mitigation: take-back is a single transactional operation over both structures. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S1-INPUT-031 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I8 | `Esc` has five distinct behaviours selected by state: interrupt the running turn; close an open dialog; deselect a footer item instead of interrupting; decline a permission prompt (equivalent to No without a comment); and, pressed twice with empty input, open the rewind menu. `Ctrl+D` requires two presses within 800 ms to exit, `Ctrl+X Ctrl+K` two presses within 3 s. | S1-INPUT-026, S6-APPROVE-020 | `ui/escape.py :: EscapeArbiter` with `press(now) -> EscapeAction`; `ui/escape.py :: DOUBLE_PRESS_WINDOWS = {'ctrl+d': 800, 'ctrl+x ctrl+k': 3000}` in milliseconds. | `test_escape.py::test_five_escape_behaviours_by_state` — parameterise over (dialog open, footer item selected, permission prompt open, turn running, input non-empty); `::test_double_press_window_expiry`. | An Esc that always interrupts can abandon a half-completed dialog; an Esc that never interrupts cannot stop a runaway turn. Mitigation: the arbiter consults an explicit state vector, never a boolean. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S1-INPUT-032 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I9 | Command history is keyed by working directory, spans sessions, collapses consecutive duplicates to one entry, and is separate from a 10-entry cleared-draft ring that survives within a session but not across a restart. | S1-SESSION-015, S1-QUEUE-030 | `history/store.py :: HistoryStore` keyed by `project_key`; `history/store.py :: record(prompt)` de-duplicating against the last entry; `history/drafts.py :: DraftRing(capacity=10)`. | `test_history.py::test_history_is_per_directory` and `::test_consecutive_duplicates_collapse` and `::test_draft_ring_capacity` and `::test_draft_ring_does_not_survive_restart`. | History keyed only by content would leak one project's prompts into another's suggestion list. Mitigation: the key is the project key, and the store path is under the config root. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |


---

## S2 — Project discovery & repository context

**Priority band:** P0 parity-core.

**Evidence base.** `agent-evidence.md` §4 Area 2 (F1–F21) and §4 Area 2b; `agent-reference-shots/areas/area2-project-context.md` (the per-finding detail for F1–F21); `agent-evidence.md` §6 L6/L21 (AGENTS.md suppression) and §4 Area 3 F10-16/F10-17/F10-25 (instructions, rules, auto memory); `docs-cache/common-workflows.md` § "Reference files and directories" (`@`-mention expansion); `docs-cache/env-vars.md` (attachment and mention switches).

**Scope note.** S2 is the layer that decides *what the model is told about the project*. It owns the cwd anchor, the instruction files, the repository snapshot, the memory store, and the mention expansion. It does not own file reading (S3) or prompt construction policy (S4).
| ID | Evidence | Acceptance criterion (testable) | Depends on | Proposed module + interface shape | Automated verification test | Security / operational risk + mitigation | Priority / Phase |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| S2-PROJ-001 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F1; area2-project-context.md F1 | The launch directory (`cwd`) is the single anchor for project context: project identity, instruction discovery, default working directory for tools and additional-directory comparisons are all derived from it, and it is reported in the init event as both `cwd` and `original_cwd` when they differ. | S1-SESSION-015 | `context/project.py :: ProjectContext(cwd: Path, original_cwd: Path, project_key: str, git_root: Optional[Path])` built by `context/project.py :: discover(cwd) -> ProjectContext`. | `test_project_context.py::test_cwd_is_the_anchor` — assert project key, instruction roots and additional-dir comparison all reference `cwd`; `::test_original_cwd_reported_when_changed`. | A drifting anchor lets a tool act outside the directory the operator approved. Mitigation: `cwd` is captured once at session start and every path check uses the captured value. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S2-INSTR-002 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F2; area2-project-context.md F2 | CLAUDE.md discovery walks from the filesystem root down to `cwd`, collecting every CLAUDE.md on the path, and **concatenates** them; a nearer file never overrides a farther one. | S2-PROJ-001 | `instructions/resolver.py :: discover_instruction_files(cwd) -> list[Path]` returning root-to-cwd order; `instructions/resolver.py :: concatenate(paths) -> str` joined in that order. | `test_instructions.py::test_root_to_cwd_order` — build a temp tree with three CLAUDE.md files and assert the assembled content order; `::test_no_override` — assert a nearer file's content is additive, not replacing. | An override rule would let a repository subdirectory silently replace the operator's root instructions. Mitigation: concatenation only; the resolver exposes no override parameter. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S2-INSTR-003 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F2, F3; area2-project-context.md F2, F3 | Each discovered instruction file contributes a separate named entry; the assembled instruction text is injected as a **user-role attachment**, not into the system prompt. | S2-INSTR-002, S1-SESSION-025 | `instructions/attachment.py :: build_attachments(files) -> list[Attachment]` with `Attachment(role='user', kind='instruction', source=path, text=...)`; `runtime/loop.py` places attachments in the user turn, not the system prompt. | `test_attachments.py::test_instructions_are_user_role_attachments` — assert the transcript's message role for instruction content is `user` and that no system-role message contains the file text. | Injecting untrusted repository text into the system prompt is a privilege-escalation vector. Mitigation: instructions are always user-role; the injection point is a single function that forbids system role. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S2-INSTR-004 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F4; area2-project-context.md F4 | A CLAUDE.md in a subdirectory is loaded **on demand**, triggered when a file in that directory is accessed, not at launch. | S2-INSTR-002, S3-FILE-001 | `instructions/lazy.py :: LazyInstructionLoader` with `on_file_accessed(path) -> list[Attachment]`; the loader keeps a `loaded_dirs: set[Path]` so each directory loads at most once. | `test_lazy_instructions.py::test_subdir_instructions_not_loaded_at_start` and `::test_loaded_on_file_access` and `::test_loaded_only_once`. | Eager loading of every subdirectory file bloats the context and increases the injected-attack surface. Mitigation: lazy, once-per-directory loading; the trigger is file access only. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S2-INSTR-005 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F5, §6 L6/L21; area2-project-context.md F5 | `AGENTS.md` is suppressed by the mere **existence** of a CLAUDE.md or CLAUDE.local.md above `cwd`; the suppression is silent — no warning is emitted. `AGENTS.md` is implemented as a built-in plugin whose switch is readable only from user, `--settings` and managed scopes, not from project settings. | S2-INSTR-002 | `instructions/resolver.py :: select_instruction_sources(cwd, plugin_state) -> list[Path]` implementing existence-based suppression; `plugins/builtin.py :: BUILTIN_PLUGINS` with the AGENTS.md plugin and its scope restriction. | `test_agents_md.py::test_agents_md_suppressed_by_claude_md` and `::test_suppression_is_silent` and `::test_project_scope_cannot_enable_agents_md`. | Silent suppression can hide operator instructions the operator expects to be honoured. Mitigation: the behaviour is specified as silent, but a debug-level diagnostic records it; the test pins the silence so a future change is deliberate. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S2-INSTR-006 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F6; area2-project-context.md F6 | `@path` imports inside an instruction file expand at launch into separate entries, with a maximum expansion depth of 4 hops, and an external path (outside the project tree) requires an approval gate before its content is included. | S2-INSTR-002, S6-APPROVE-021 | `instructions/importer.py :: expand_imports(text, base_dir, depth=0, max_depth=4) -> list[ImportedEntry]`; `instructions/importer.py :: ImportedEntry(path, text, is_external)`. | `test_imports.py::test_import_expands_at_depth_1` and `::test_depth_4_is_maximum` and `::test_external_import_requires_approval`. | Unbounded import chains permit a repository to pull arbitrary external content into context. Mitigation: hard depth cap plus an explicit approval gate for out-of-tree paths. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S2-RULES-007 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F7, §4 Area 3 F10-17; area2-project-context.md F7 | `.claude/rules/` files are path-scoped: a rule with a `paths:` frontmatter glob is injected only when a file matching the glob is accessed, not at launch. | S2-INSTR-004, S3-FILE-001 | `instructions/rules.py :: RuleStore.load(dir) -> list[Rule]` with `Rule(name, paths_glob, body)`; `instructions/rules.py :: rules_for(path) -> list[Rule]` matching the glob against the accessed path. | `test_rules.py::test_rule_injected_on_matching_path_only` and `::test_non_matching_path_not_injected`. | A glob that is not anchored could match a path outside the project and inject content. Mitigation: glob matching is performed against paths that have already passed the working-directory check. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S2-GIT-008 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F8; area2-project-context.md F8 | `gitStatus` is captured **once** at session start and injected as a static snapshot; later changes to the working tree are not reflected in that snapshot. | S2-PROJ-001 | `context/git.py :: capture_status(repo_root) -> GitStatus` invoked once during discovery and stored on the frozen session context. | `test_git_snapshot.py::test_status_is_captured_once` — mutate the working tree mid-session and assert the injected snapshot is unchanged; `::test_snapshot_empty_outside_repo`. | A live git status would mean an unexpected network or subprocess call per turn. Mitigation: capture is one-time and its result is frozen. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S2-ENV-009 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F15; area2-project-context.md F15 | An environment-context attachment enumerates the platform, the active shell, and the policy on untrusted data, and is injected at session start. | S2-PROJ-001 | `context/environment.py :: build_environment_attachment(env: Env) -> Attachment` with fields `platform`, `shell`, `untrusted_data_policy`; `context/env.py :: Env` a snapshot of the relevant variables taken at start. | `test_environment_attachment.py::test_attachment_names_platform_and_shell` and `::test_env_snapshot_is_taken_once`. | Environment variables can carry secrets; the attachment must enumerate categories, not values. Mitigation: the attachment builder has a deny-list of sensitive variable names and never includes arbitrary variables. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S2-MEM-010 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F16, §4 Area 3 F10-25; area2-project-context.md F16 | Auto memory is per-repository, shared across worktrees of that repository, stored as an index plus topic files, and the index is capped at 200 lines / 25 KB. | S2-PROJ-001, S10-WORKTREE-028 | `memory/store.py :: MemoryStore(root, repo_id)` with `read_index() -> list[MemoryEntry]`, `write_topic(name, text)`, and a `MAX_INDEX_LINES = 200` / `MAX_INDEX_BYTES = 25*1024` enforcement. | `test_memory.py::test_shared_across_worktrees` and `::test_index_cap_enforced` and `::test_repo_scoping`. | A shared-across-worktree memory file can leak one branch's notes into another. Mitigation: scope is the repository identity, not the worktree; the index is a bounded, auditable file. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S2-SETTINGS-011 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F18; area2-project-context.md F18 | Project settings are **not** inherited from parent directories: a `.claude/settings.json` applies only to the project rooted at its own directory; a parent project's settings do not leak into a child project. | S1-SESSION-015 | `config/loader.py :: load_settings_chain(project_root) -> list[SettingsSource]` returning only the project's own file plus the user/managed layers; `config/loader.py :: SettingsSource(scope, path, data)`. | `test_settings_scope.py::test_parent_project_settings_not_inherited` — create a parent project with a deny rule and a child project; assert the child's effective rules exclude it. | Inheritance would let an outer repository impose policy on an inner one (or vice versa) unexpectedly. Mitigation: the chain is explicitly enumerated; no directory walk upward is performed for settings. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S2-MENTION-012 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F21; docs-cache/common-workflows.md § "Reference files and directories" | An `@` mention in a user message is expanded at submission: a file mention includes the file's content when it fits the Read token limit (25,000 tokens by default) and is refused above 256 KB; a directory mention contributes a file *listing*, not contents; an MCP resource mention is fetched as `@server:resource`. | S3-READ-003, S8-RESOURCE-018 | `mentions/resolver.py :: resolve(text, ctx) -> Expansion` with `Expansion(kind=Literal['file','directory','resource','agent','session'], payload)`; `mentions/resolver.py :: FILE_TOKEN_LIMIT = 25_000`, `FILE_BYTE_LIMIT = 256*1024`. | `test_mentions.py::test_file_mention_includes_content` and `::test_oversize_file_not_expanded` and `::test_directory_mention_is_listing_only`. | Unbounded mention expansion is a context-flooding vector. Mitigation: hard byte and token caps; over-limit files contribute only their path. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S2-MENTION-013 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F21; docs-cache/env-vars.md (`CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_FILE_MENTIONS`, `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ATTACHMENTS`) | `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_FILE_MENTIONS=1` stops `@` mention expansion and instead gives the model the mentioned paths with a note to read them with the file tools; the variable is ignored in project and local settings. `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ATTACHMENTS=1` disables attachment processing so mentions are sent as plain text. | S2-MENTION-012 | `mentions/resolver.py :: mention_policy(env, settings_scopes) -> MentionPolicy` — the env var is honoured only from the user/managed/env scope, never from project or local settings. | `test_mentions.py::test_disable_file_mentions_replaces_with_paths` and `::test_project_settings_cannot_set_the_switch`. | If a repository could set the switch it could suppress content the operator expected to be included. Mitigation: the scope restriction is enforced at the policy resolution point and asserted by a test. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S2-MENTION-014 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I6 | The `@` typeahead is a multi-kind namespace — files, directories, MCP resources, subagents and other live sessions — and the typeahead source and the resolution semantics are separate: typing `@agent-` shows file matches but the mention still resolves as an agent. | S2-MENTION-012, S10-AGENT-006 | `mentions/typeahead.py :: suggest(prefix, ctx) -> list[Suggestion]` with `Suggestion(kind, display, payload)`; resolution uses the same `resolve()` entry point regardless of how the mention was typed. | `test_typeahead.py::test_kinds_are_disambiguated` and `::test_agent_mention_resolves_despite_file_suggestions`. | A typeahead that leaks other projects' file names into the suggestion list is an information disclosure. Mitigation: suggestions are filtered to the project's working directories before display. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S2-ENV-015 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 12 F2, F17; area12-dependencies.md F2 | `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` is a **capability gate**, not merely an endpoint override: four features are disabled outright when it is set. | S13-PROVIDER-020 | `config/env.py :: EnvFlags` exposing `base_url_set: bool`; `capabilities/gate.py :: apply_base_url_gate(features, flags) -> set[Feature]` removing the four gated features. | `test_capability_gate.py::test_base_url_disables_gated_features` — set the variable and assert the four features are absent from the published capability set. | A feature that silently half-works against a non-vendor endpoint is worse than one that is off. Mitigation: the gate removes the feature from the registry entirely, not just from a menu. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S2-SELF-016 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F17; area2-project-context.md F17 | The product exposes self-inspection commands: `/context`, `/memory`, `/skills`, `/hooks`, `/mcp`, `/permissions`, `/status`, `/doctor`, each of which reports state without contacting the model. | S11-CONTEXT-022, S9-SKILL-006 | `cli/selfinspect.py :: SELF_INSPECT_COMMANDS: dict[str, Callable[[SessionContext], str]]` — each entry a pure function over the frozen context. | `test_selfinspect.py::test_each_command_renders_without_model_call` — invoke each and assert the provider recorded zero requests and the output is non-empty. | A self-inspection command that contacts the model costs money and leaks state. Mitigation: the self-inspection registry is disjoint from the model-dispatch path by construction. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S2-ENV-016A | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F19; area2-project-context.md F19 | The prompt-caching strategy shapes the injection surface: content injected into the first turn is positioned so that it remains cacheable across subsequent turns, and volatile content (timestamps, status) is excluded from the cached prefix. | S2-INSTR-003, S11-CACHE-018 | `context/cacheplan.py :: plan_cacheable_prefix(ctx) -> PrefixPlan` listing the ordered segments eligible for caching and the volatile segments excluded. | `test_cache_plan.py::test_volatile_segments_excluded` and `::test_prefix_order_stable_across_turns`. | A volatile segment inside the cached prefix invalidates the cache on every turn, multiplying cost. Mitigation: the cache plan is computed once and asserted stable; volatile data is appended after the cache boundary. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S2-FILE-017 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F20; area2-project-context.md F20 | The file-type handling matrix is explicit: text files are returned as text, images and PDFs are handled by dedicated paths, `.ipynb` is treated as a cell-addressable structure, and binary formats (e.g. `.xlsx`, `.parquet`, `.heic`, `.zip`) are refused by the read tool. | S3-READ-010, S3-NOTEBOOK-020 | `files/filetypes.py :: classify(path) -> FileKind` with `TEXT/IMAGE/PDF/NOTEBOOK/BINARY/UNSUPPORTED`; `files/filetypes.py :: REFUSED_EXTENSIONS`. | `test_filetypes.py::test_binary_extensions_refused` and `::test_notebook_classified` — assert each representative extension maps to the expected kind. | Attempting to read a binary as text produces mojibake that then enters the model's reasoning. Mitigation: classification is by extension plus content sniffing, and unknown types are refused rather than guessed. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S2-ENV-018 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F15, §4 Area 12 F19; docs-cache/env-vars.md | The environment-variable surface is a first-class configuration surface: recognised variables are enumerated, their scope is documented (some are ignored in project and local settings), and an unrecognised variable is ignored rather than fatal. | S1-CLI-013, S14-REDACT-004 | `config/env.py :: RECOGNISED_VARS: dict[str, EnvVarSpec]` with `EnvVarSpec(value_parser, scopes_allowed, applies_to)`; `config/env.py :: read_env(os.environ) -> EnvSnapshot` ignoring unknown names. | `test_env_surface.py::test_unknown_var_ignored` and `::test_scope_restricted_var_ignored_in_project_settings` and `::test_documented_set_matches_contract`. | An unrecognised variable silently ignored can mask a typo in an operator's setup. Mitigation: recognised variables are documented and tested; unknown ones are ignored by design but are reported at debug level. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S2-DETECT-019 | ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION (no direct observation of the reference's repository-detection algorithm beyond the `gitStatus` capture in agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F8) | Repository detection resolves the enclosing repository root by walking upward from `cwd` and stopping at the first directory containing a VCS marker, and reports the resolved root in the session context; if no marker is found the root is `cwd` itself. | S2-PROJ-001 | `context/repo.py :: find_repo_root(cwd) -> Optional[Path]` walking upward for `.git` (file or directory), `.hg`, or `.jj`; `context/repo.py :: RepoKind` enum. | `test_repo_root.py::test_walks_upward_to_marker` and `::test_no_marker_means_cwd` and `::test_bare_git_file_is_recognised`. | Resolving the root too high would place the project boundary outside what the operator intended, widening tool scope. Mitigation: the walk stops at the first marker and never above the filesystem root. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S2-ENV-020 | ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION (derived from the observed additional-directories surface in agent-reference-shots/PROTOCOL-NOTES.md `additional_directories` and agent-evidence.md §4 Area 8 §3.6) | Additional working directories supplied via `--add-dir` are added to the session's path-allowance set, appear in the init event, and are reported to the user; they widen the set of paths that file tools and permission checks treat as inside the project. | S6-PATH-024, S1-CLI-006 | `context/dirs.py :: add_directories(base_dirs, extra) -> list[Path]`; `context/dirs.py :: is_within_working_dirs(path, dirs) -> bool` used by the permission engine. | `test_add_dir.py::test_add_dir_widens_allowance` and `::test_add_dir_is_reported_in_init` and `::test_path_outside_all_dirs_is_not_within`. | An added directory is a widening of the tool's reach; if it were not reported the operator would not know. Mitigation: every added directory is echoed in the init event and in a status surface. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S2-INSTR-021 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F16, §4 Area 3 F10-25; area2-project-context.md F16 | Auto memory has an index-plus-topics layout: the index lists topic names with a short pointer, and the body lives in separate files, so that reading memory costs a bounded amount of context. | S2-MEM-010 | `memory/store.py :: read_topic(name) -> str`; `memory/store.py :: write_topic(name, body)` maintaining the index line count. | `test_memory.py::test_index_and_topics_separated` and `::test_topic_read_does_not_load_all_topics`. | A single concatenated memory file would consume the whole context budget. Mitigation: index/topic split with an enforced index cap. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S2-ENV-022 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F5, §4 Area 3 F10-18; area2-project-context.md F5 | Instruction loading is a plugin-driven capability: the set of instruction *sources* is determined by which instruction-providing plugins are enabled, and the AGENTS.md provider is a built-in plugin that a project cannot enable. | S2-INSTR-005, S9-PLUGIN-026 | `plugins/registry.py :: enabled_instruction_providers() -> list[Provider]`; `instructions/resolver.py :: select_instruction_sources(cwd, providers) -> list[Path]`. | `test_instruction_providers.py::test_project_cannot_enable_agents_provider` and `::test_disabled_provider_contributes_nothing`. | If a repository could enable a provider, it could inject instructions that claim operator authority. Mitigation: provider enablement is restricted to user/managed/CLI scopes, enforced in the plugin registry. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S2-ENV-023 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F8; area2-project-context.md F8 | Repository state injected at start is limited to the status snapshot; no other repository metadata (branches, remotes, commit history) is injected automatically. | S2-GIT-008 | `context/git.py :: GitStatus` with fields only for the working-tree status; the injection builder has no other git source. | `test_git_snapshot.py::test_only_status_is_injected` — assert the injected text contains branch/remotes-free status only. | Remote URL injection could leak an internal host name or a credential embedded in a URL. Mitigation: the injected snapshot excludes remotes. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S2-ENV-024 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F9; area2-project-context.md F9 | A read performed through the shell counts as a read for the purposes of the read-before-edit precondition, so a file edited after a shell-based read does not raise a stale-read error. | S3-EDIT-016, S5-CMD-008 | `files/readtracker.py :: ReadTracker.record(path, kind)` where `kind` is `tool/shell`; `files/readtracker.py :: was_read(path) -> bool` treats both kinds as a read. | `test_readtracker.py::test_shell_read_counts_as_read` — perform a shell `cat` through the shell tool then an edit; assert no stale-read refusal. | Conversely, an edit that is *not* preceded by any read must refuse. Mitigation: the tracker records the read kind explicitly and the edit precondition consults it. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S2-ENV-025 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F21; docs-cache/common-workflows.md | A directory mention contributes a file listing that respects the same ignore semantics as the search tools, and never contributes file contents. | S2-MENTION-012, S3-GLOB-008 | `mentions/resolver.py :: directory_listing(dir, ignore_policy) -> list[str]` reusing the search layer's ignore filter. | `test_mentions.py::test_directory_listing_excludes_ignored_paths` and `::test_listing_has_no_contents`. | A listing that bypasses ignore rules would expose build output and vendored trees. Mitigation: the listing reuses the search layer's ignore policy rather than a separate walk. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S2-ENV-026 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F16; area2-project-context.md F16 | Auto memory is scoped per repository but its *topic bodies* are loaded lazily: the session injects the index and loads a topic body only when a topic is referenced or read. | S2-MEM-010, S2-INSTR-021 | `memory/store.py :: LazyMemory` with `topics() -> list[str]` and `body(topic) -> str` called on demand by the memory tool. | `test_memory.py::test_topic_body_not_injected_at_start` — assert the transcript contains only topic names, not bodies, until a topic is read. | Eagerly injecting memory bodies wastes context and may surface stale facts. Mitigation: index-only injection at start; bodies are tool-read. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S2-ENV-027 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F2, F3; area2-project-context.md F3 | Instruction content that is injected is marked as a user-role attachment with an identifiable source, so that the model can distinguish operator-authored instructions from user-typed text and from tool output. | S2-INSTR-003 | `instructions/attachment.py :: Attachment` carrying `source_path` and `kind='instruction'`; the transcript entry type `attachment` preserves it. | `test_attachments.py::test_instruction_attachment_is_distinguishable` — assert a transcript reader can classify each user-role message as instruction, typed prompt or tool result. | Conflating operator instructions with user text would let a user message impersonate policy. Mitigation: attachment kinds are a closed enum, and the transcript preserves the distinction. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S2-ENV-028 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F15; area2-project-context.md F15 | The environment attachment states the untrusted-data policy explicitly, so the model is told which inputs are untrusted (file contents, command output, tool results) rather than being left to infer it. | S2-ENV-009, S12-UX-020 | `context/environment.py :: UNTRUSTED_SOURCES` enumerating the categories; the attachment text names each category. | `test_environment_attachment.py::test_untrusted_sources_enumerated` — assert each category name appears in the attachment. | Absent an explicit policy, tool output can be treated as instruction. Mitigation: the categories are enumerated in the attachment and asserted by a test so the list cannot silently shrink. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |


---

## S3 — File read / search / edit / patch / diff review

**Priority band:** P0 parity-core.

**Evidence base.** `agent-evidence.md` §4 Area 2 F9–F14, F20 (read contract, notebooks, Glob/Grep asymmetry, file-type matrix) and §5.8 (output-size handling); `agent-reference-shots/areas/area2-project-context.md` F9–F13, F20; `agent-reference-shots/areas/area6-tests-errors.md` F20 (Read/Edit refusal behaviour); `docs-cache/tools-reference.md` (read/glob/grep/edit/notebook tool contracts); `docs-cache/common-workflows.md` (mention and diff workflows); `area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md` §2 F-I13 (the two diff surfaces and per-turn attribution).

**Scope note.** S3 owns the file *data plane*: what bytes the model can see, what it can change, and how changes are reviewed. Tool *permission* is S6; *shell* execution is S5.
| ID | Evidence | Acceptance criterion (testable) | Depends on | Proposed module + interface shape | Automated verification test | Security / operational risk + mitigation | Priority / Phase |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| S3-READ-001 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F9; area2-project-context.md F9 | The read tool takes an absolute path and returns the file's lines numbered from 1, in a stable text format; a request for a relative path is resolved against the session cwd before use. | S2-PROJ-001 | `tools/read.py :: read(path: str, offset: int/None, limit: int/None) -> ReadResult`; `ReadResult` carries `text`, `lines`, `total_lines`, `truncated`, `kind`. | `test_read.py::test_line_numbering_starts_at_one` and `::test_relative_path_resolved_against_cwd` and `::test_offset_limit_paging`. | Absolute-path resolution must reject traversal outside the working directories. Mitigation: path normalisation happens before the read, and the result of the check is enforced by the permission layer (S6). | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S3-READ-003 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F9, §5.8; area2-project-context.md F9 | A text file larger than 256 KB or larger than 25,000 tokens is **not** refused; it is returned as a page together with a `PARTIAL view` notice that states what was omitted and how to obtain the rest. A read whose `offset`/`limit` window itself exceeds the token limit **is** an error telling the model to narrow the range or use Grep. | S3-READ-001 | `tools/read.py :: ReadLimits(byte_soft_limit=256*1024, token_soft_limit=25_000)`; `tools/read.py :: partial_view(omitted_ranges) -> str` producing the notice. | `test_read.py::test_oversize_file_returns_partial_view_not_error` and `::test_over_token_window_is_an_error` — assert one returns content plus notice, the other raises `ReadWindowTooLarge`. | Silently truncating without a notice would make the model believe it read the whole file. Mitigation: the notice is mandatory whenever content is omitted and is asserted by the test. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S3-READ-004 | area6-tests-errors.md F20; docs-cache/tools-reference.md | A read of a file that is not valid UTF-8 is **refused** with a message naming the likely legacy encodings and stating that nothing was written; a file with even one invalid byte sequence is refused because the check covers the file's bytes as a whole. | S3-EDIT-005 | `files/encoding.py :: validate_utf8(path) -> EncodingCheck` with `EncodingCheck(ok, kind=Literal['utf8','invalid','binary'])`; `tools/read.py` raising `InvalidEncoding` with the refusal text. | `test_read.py::test_invalid_utf8_refused` — write a Windows-1252 file with a lone 0x92 byte; assert the read raises and the file is byte-identical afterwards. | Reading a legacy-encoded file as UTF-8 and then writing it back would replace every undecodable byte with U+FFFD — silent corruption. Mitigation: the encoding check precedes any write path and refuses rather than coercing. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S3-READ-005 | docs-cache/tools-reference.md; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F20 | Binary extensions (e.g. `.xlsx`, `.parquet`, `.heic`, `.zip`) are refused by the read tool; an image over 128 MB is refused with a message naming the limit; a pipe or device that streams more than 128 MB without ending is refused with a distinct message. | S2-FILE-017 | `tools/read.py :: read_image(path) -> ImageResult` with `MAX_IMAGE_BYTES = 128*1024*1024`; distinct exception types `ImageTooLarge` and `NotAFile`. | `test_read.py::test_binary_extension_refused` and `::test_image_over_128mb_refused` and `::test_device_streaming_over_limit_refused`. | A device/pipe read can block forever and exhaust memory. Mitigation: a hard byte ceiling is enforced while streaming, and the refusal distinguishes a size limit from a non-file. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S3-READ-006 | docs-cache/tools-reference.md; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F9 | Since the reference version that added it, a single read call may be permitted to return a whole file sized against the remaining context (`allow_large: true`), and such a read is explicitly treated as a full read for edit purposes. | S3-READ-003, S11-CONTEXT-010 | `tools/read.py :: read(..., allow_large: bool = False)`; the read tracker records the call as complete when `allow_large` was honoured. | `test_read.py::test_allow_large_marks_file_fully_read` — read with `allow_large` and then edit without a stale-read refusal. | Allowing an unbounded read can overflow the context window. Mitigation: the allowance is checked against the remaining context budget, not a constant. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S3-EDIT-005 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F9; docs-cache/tools-reference.md | The edit tool replaces an exact string in a file: the old string must appear exactly once unless a replace-all flag is set, and the file is re-read immediately before the write so a concurrent change is detected. | S3-READ-001, S3-EDIT-016 | `tools/edit.py :: edit(path, old_str, new_str, replace_all=False) -> EditResult` raising `OldStringNotFound` / `OldStringNotUnique` / `StaleFile`. | `test_edit.py::test_old_string_not_found_is_an_error` and `::test_non_unique_old_string_requires_flag` and `::test_concurrent_modification_detected`. | A non-unique replacement can corrupt an unrelated region of the file. Mitigation: uniqueness is enforced unless explicitly overridden, and the pre-write re-read detects concurrent modification. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S3-EDIT-016 | docs-cache/tools-reference.md; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F9, §6 L14 | Editing a file that has not been read in this session is refused with a message asking for a read first; a *partial* read (one that returned a `PARTIAL view`) does not count as a read for this purpose. | S3-READ-003, S2-ENV-024 | `files/readtracker.py :: require_full_read(path) -> None` raising `NotReadYet`; the tracker distinguishes full from partial reads. | `test_edit.py::test_edit_without_read_refused` and `::test_partial_read_does_not_satisfy_precondition`. | The precondition is the main defence against a blind overwrite of a file whose current content the model has never seen. Mitigation: the tracker records read completeness, and a shell read also satisfies it (S2-ENV-024). | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S3-EDIT-017 | docs-cache/tools-reference.md; area6-tests-errors.md F20 | An edit to a file whose content is not valid UTF-8 is refused with a model-directed message offering two remedies (use a shell command that preserves the encoding, or ask the user about conversion); nothing is written. | S3-READ-004, S3-EDIT-005 | `tools/edit.py :: edit(...)` performing the same `validate_utf8` check as the read path before writing; refusal message enumerates the two remedies. | `test_edit.py::test_edit_of_non_utf8_refused_and_file_unchanged` — assert the refusal text names both remedies and the file hash is unchanged. | This is the specific corruption path the refusal exists to prevent. Mitigation: the check is shared with the read path so the two cannot diverge. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S3-EDIT-018 | docs-cache/tools-reference.md; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F9 | A write creates or fully replaces a file; creation of a file that already exists is refused unless an overwrite flag is supplied, and the write is atomic (temp file plus rename) so a failure cannot leave a half-written file. | S3-EDIT-005 | `tools/write.py :: write(path, content, overwrite=False) -> WriteResult`; `tools/write.py :: atomic_write(path, data)` using a temp file in the same directory plus `os.replace`. | `test_write.py::test_create_existing_refused_without_flag` and `::test_write_is_atomic_on_failure` — inject a failure mid-write and assert the original file is intact. | A non-atomic write can truncate a source file on a disk-full error. Mitigation: same-directory temp file plus rename; the temp file is removed on failure. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S3-GLOB-008 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F11; area2-project-context.md F11 | Glob and Grep have deliberately different ignore semantics: Glob traverses `.git/` while Grep does not, and the two tools apply different sets of ignored directories. The asymmetry is a property of the tools, not a bug. | S3-GREP-009 | `search/glob.py :: glob(pattern, path) -> list[Path]` with `GLOB_IGNORES`; `search/grep.py :: grep(pattern, path) -> list[Match]` with `GREP_IGNORES`; both share `search/ignore.py :: base_ignores()` but add their own. | `test_glob_grep.py::test_grep_ignores_git_dir_but_glob_does_not` — create `.git/x` with a match and assert the two tools return different result sets. | An accidental alignment of the two ignore sets would silently change results the operator relies on. Mitigation: the two ignore sets are separate constants, and the difference is pinned by a test. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S3-GLOB-009 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F12; area2-project-context.md F12 | The search tools publish their search paths and output modes: Glob returns a list of paths, Grep returns content lines with a file/line prefix, and both accept a path argument that limits the search root. | S3-GLOB-008 | `search/api.py :: GlobResult(paths, truncated)` and `:: GrepResult(matches=[GrepMatch(file, line, text)], truncated)`; `search/api.py :: SearchOutputMode` enum. | `test_search.py::test_glob_returns_paths_and_grep_returns_lines` and `::test_path_argument_limits_scope`. | Result truncation must be visible; a silently truncated result set leads the model to conclude a symbol does not exist. Mitigation: the result carries a `truncated` flag and a count note. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S3-SEARCH-010 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F13; area2-project-context.md F13 | Tool availability is platform-dependent: the `PowerShell` tool exists only on Windows, and `Glob`/`Grep` availability is likewise platform-conditional, with the available set published in the init event. | S1-TOOLSET-022 | `tools/platform.py :: available_tools(platform) -> tuple[str, ...]`; the registry filters `ALL_TOOLS` by platform before publishing. | `test_tool_availability.py::test_powershell_only_on_windows` and `::test_init_publishes_platform_subset`. | Publishing an unavailable tool leads to a tool-not-found loop; hiding an available one loses capability. Mitigation: a single platform filter feeds both the registry and the dispatcher. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S3-NOTEBOOK-020 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F10; area2-project-context.md F10 | `.ipynb` files are treated as a cell-addressable structure with stable cell ids: reads return cell sources keyed by id, and notebook edits target a cell by id, preserving the file's JSON structure and any other cell's content. | S3-EDIT-005, S3-EDIT-018 | `tools/notebook.py :: read_notebook(path) -> list[NotebookCell(id, source, cell_type, metadata)]`; `tools/notebook.py :: edit_cell(path, cell_id, new_source) -> None` rewriting the JSON with `json.dump` and preserving unrelated keys. | `test_notebook.py::test_cell_ids_stable_across_reads` and `::test_edit_preserves_other_cells_and_metadata` — assert an untouched cell's source and the notebook-level metadata are byte-equivalent after the edit. | A notebook write that rebuilds the JSON can drop cell outputs and metadata, silently destroying information. Mitigation: the editor mutates the parsed structure rather than re-serialising from a lossy model. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S3-NOTEBOOK-021 | docs-cache/tools-reference.md | A notebook over 100 MB is refused by the read tool; the refusal names the size limit. | S3-READ-005 | `tools/notebook.py :: read_notebook(path)` refusing above `MAX_NOTEBOOK_BYTES = 100*1024*1024`. | `test_notebook.py::test_oversize_notebook_refused`. | Parsing an unbounded notebook is a memory-exhaustion vector. Mitigation: a hard size ceiling checked before parsing. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S3-DIFF-022 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I13; docs-cache/common-workflows.md | The diff surface shows the changes Claude has made alongside the operator's uncommitted changes, per file, with added and removed line counts. | S3-EDIT-005, S3-EDIT-018 | `diff/panel.py :: DiffSnapshot` built from `vcs/status.py :: working_tree_changes(repo) -> list[FileDiff]` plus `session/edits.py :: SessionEditLog`. | `test_diff.py::test_diff_lists_files_with_counts` — create a tracked change and assert the entry carries `added` and `removed` counts. | The diff must not include content from outside the working directories. Mitigation: the change collector filters by path before rendering. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S3-DIFF-023 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I13 | There are two sources of truth for 'what changed': git (working tree) and the agent's own per-turn edit log. The UI exposes both, labelled `Current` and `T1`/`T2`, and a change made through a shell command appears **only** under `Current`, never under a turn view. | S3-DIFF-022, S5-CMD-010 | `session/edits.py :: SessionEditLog` with `record(turn_id, path, change)` and `turns() -> list[TurnId]`; `diff/panel.py :: diff_for(source: Literal['current', 'turn']) -> list[FileDiff]`. | `test_diff.py::test_turn_view_excludes_shell_made_changes` — edit via the shell tool and assert the turn view is empty while `Current` shows it. | Mis-attributing a shell-made change to a turn would misreport what the agent did. Mitigation: only the edit tool records turn attribution. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S3-DIFF-024 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I13 | The diff panel's comparison baseline is switchable — this session's changes, the uncommitted changes as one list, or everything since the branch split from the default branch — and the choice is remembered per repository. | S3-DIFF-022 | `diff/baseline.py :: BaselineMode` enum (`session`, `uncommitted`, `branch-split`); `diff/baseline.py :: remember(repo_key, mode)` persisted under the config root. | `test_diff_baseline.py::test_baseline_cycles` and `::test_choice_persisted_per_repository`. | A branch-split baseline requires network or full history and can be slow. Mitigation: the mode is explicit and the remembered value is per repository so it cannot leak between projects. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S3-DIFF-025 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I13 | The diff panel requires a fullscreen renderer, a git repository, and a terminal at least 110 columns wide; it auto-opens when Claude starts editing files in a terminal at least 144 columns wide, and once closed it stays closed in that session and later ones until `/diff` is run again. | S3-DIFF-022, S12-UX-014 | `ui/panels.py :: DiffPanel` with `should_auto_open(terminal_width, is_fullscreen, is_repo) -> bool`; the closed-state is persisted under the config root keyed by repository. | `test_diff_panel.py::test_width_thresholds` — assert 109 columns does not open and 110 does; `::test_auto_open_threshold_is_144`; `::test_closed_state_persists`. | Opening a panel in a narrow terminal corrupts the layout. Mitigation: width checks are exact and tested at the boundary. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S3-PATCH-026 | ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION (the reference's patch-application surface was not directly characterised in agent-evidence.md; only the diff review surfaces in area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I13 were observed) | A patch-application tool accepts a unified diff, applies it against the current file content, and reports per-hunk results (applied / rejected with a reason / failed context match); it never applies a partially-matching hunk silently. | S3-DIFF-022, S3-EDIT-005 | `tools/patch.py :: apply_patch(path, unified_diff) -> PatchResult` with `PatchResult(hunks=[HunkResult(index, status, reason)])`; `tools/patch.py :: apply_hunks(text, hunks) -> tuple[str, list[HunkResult]]`. | `test_patch.py::test_hunk_with_stale_context_is_rejected_with_reason` and `::test_partial_application_is_reported` and `::test_patch_is_atomic`. | A partial patch application leaves the file in a state the model did not intend. Mitigation: apply to an in-memory copy and commit only when every hunk applies, or report each hunk's status explicitly. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S3-PATCH-027 | ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION (large-file diff semantics are listed as an open question in agent-evidence.md §7/§11) | When a file's diff is too large to render, the diff view paginates and states the total size and the current window rather than truncating silently. | S3-DIFF-022 | `diff/render.py :: paginate(diff, max_lines) -> DiffPage(page, total_pages, total_lines)`. | `test_diff.py::test_large_diff_paginates_with_totals`. | Silent truncation makes a reviewer believe they saw the whole change. Mitigation: pagination is explicit and the totals are always shown. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S3-READ-028 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F9, §5.8; area6-tests-errors.md F6 | Read output that exceeds the inline limit is persisted to a file and replaced by a wrapper that names the file path, so the model can retrieve the rest on demand rather than losing it. | S3-READ-003, S7-OUTPUT-006 | `output/persist.py :: persist(text, session_dir) -> PersistedRef(path, preview)`; `tools/read.py` embedding the ref in the returned text. | `test_read.py::test_oversize_output_persisted_with_path` — assert the returned text contains the persisted path and that the file exists with the full content. | A persisted file inside the session directory is agent-written content; it must not be treated as project source. Mitigation: persisted files live under the session dir with a reserved prefix and are excluded from project searches. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S3-EDIT-029 | ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION (derived from the observed Edit/Write refusal behaviour in area6-tests-errors.md F20 and the read-before-edit precondition in docs-cache/tools-reference.md) | An edit whose `old_str` is present but whose surrounding context differs from the last read is refused as a stale-file condition, and the refusal states that the file changed since the last read. | S3-EDIT-005, S3-EDIT-016 | `tools/edit.py :: EditGuard` with `check(path, expected_hash) -> None` raising `StaleFile`. | `test_edit.py::test_stale_file_refused` — read, modify the file externally, then edit; assert the refusal and that the file is unchanged. | Applying an edit to a changed file can clobber the other writer's work. Mitigation: hash comparison at write time, with a refusal that names the condition. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S3-EDIT-030 | docs-cache/tools-reference.md; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F20 | A file with no trailing newline is edited without introducing one unexpectedly, and a replacement that spans a line boundary preserves the line structure of the surrounding text; the edited file's line count changes by exactly the expected delta. | S3-EDIT-005 | `tools/edit.py :: apply_edit(text, old_str, new_str, replace_all) -> str` operating on the text with explicit newline handling. | `test_edit.py::test_line_count_delta_exact` and `::test_no_trailing_newline_added`. | Newline mangling creates noisy diffs that obscure the real change. Mitigation: newline handling is explicit and covered by an exact-delta assertion. | P1 extensibility / Phase 2 |
| S3-SEARCH-031 | docs-cache/tools-reference.md; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F12 | Grep supports regular expressions and reports the match line with its file and line number; the output is capped and the cap is stated in the result rather than applied silently. | S3-GLOB-009 | `search/grep.py :: grep(pattern, path, output_mode) -> GrepResult` with `MAX_MATCHES` and a `truncated` marker plus a count of omitted matches. | `test_search.py::test_match_cap_reported` and `::test_regex_and_literal_patterns`. | An uncapped grep over a large repository can flood the context. Mitigation: a hard cap with an explicit truncation notice. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S3-SEARCH-032 | ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION (the reference's search-path and ignore-file precedence was observed only at the level recorded in agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F11–F12) | Ignore sources are applied in a fixed precedence order — version-control ignore files, then the product's own default ignore list, then explicit exclusions — and the order is asserted so results are reproducible across machines. | S3-GLOB-008, S3-SEARCH-031 | `search/ignore.py :: IgnoreStack` with `sources: tuple[IgnoreSource, ...]` applied in a fixed order; `search/ignore.py :: IgnoreSource` enum (`vcs`, `default`, `explicit`). | `test_ignore.py::test_precedence_order_fixed` and `::test_same_tree_same_results_on_two_machines` — run the same fixture with and without a VCS ignore file and assert the documented ordering. | Ignore-file precedence determines whether secrets in an ignored file reach the model. Mitigation: the order is a fixed constant and is tested. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
