| S6-AUDIT-051 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 8 F-21; area8-permissions.md §11 F-21 | Permission rules reach the **model** as a tool-set change and the **operator** as a per-file rule inventory; both views are produced from the same rule store, so they cannot disagree. | S6-RULE-011, S2-SELF-016 | `permissions/inventory.py :: rule_inventory() -> list[RuleRecord(source_file, kind, tool, specifier, scope)]`; the tool-set filter and the inventory are both derived from the rule store. | `test_rule_views.py::test_tool_set_view_and_inventory_agree` — assert every bare-denied tool appears in the inventory and that the two views are derived from the same source. | An operator who cannot see the effective rules cannot audit them. Mitigation: one store, two derived views, one test asserting consistency. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S6-PS-052 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 8 F-1; area8-permissions.md §11 F-1 | The permission system is deny-first and the deny is scope-absolute: a deny in any scope applies regardless of where an allow comes from, and the mode never grants what a deny forbids. | S6-RULE-010, S6-MODE-001 | `permissions/engine.py :: decide()` structured as `deny → ask → allow → mode_default`, with the deny stage returning unconditionally on a match. | `test_permission_eval.py::test_deny_in_any_scope_wins` — place a deny in the user scope and an allow in the project scope, assert the deny wins. | This is the single most important ordering in the system. Mitigation: the stage order is fixed in one function and asserted by a precedence table test. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S6-RULESET-053 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 8 §9, F-20; area8-permissions.md §9.4 | Managed (organisation) settings are delivered out of band and are applied ahead of every other scope for *restrictions*; a repository cannot observe or override them. | S6-SETTINGS-050 | `config/managed.py :: load_managed_settings() -> Settings` reading the managed delivery location; `config/loader.py` applying it first for restriction keys. | `test_managed.py::test_managed_restriction_applies_over_user_allow` and `::test_managed_settings_not_readable_from_project`. | A managed restriction that a user setting could override is not a restriction. Mitigation: managed restrictions are applied at a separate, earlier stage and the test asserts the override attempt fails. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S6-WARN-054 | agent-evidence.md §6 L4; area8-permissions.md §11 F-18 | In `-p` (print) mode an invalid permission configuration is silently ignored rather than fatal, so an unattended run is not blocked by a malformed settings file — and the behaviour is different from interactive mode. | S6-SETTINGS-050, S1-CLI-002 | `config/loader.py :: load_settings(fail_open: bool)` with `fail_open=True` in print mode; a diagnostic is written to stderr. | `test_settings.py::test_print_mode_ignores_invalid_settings` and `::test_interactive_mode_reports_invalid_settings`. | Fail-open in unattended mode trades strictness for availability; the asymmetry must be deliberate and documented. Mitigation: the mode-dependent behaviour is a single flag with a test for each side. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S6-RECOVER-055 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F11, §4 Area 6 F14; area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H11 | A session that ended with an interrupted or failed turn can be resumed and continued; the transcript retains the partial work so the continuation sees it. | S6-RECOVER-044, S1-SESSION-016 | `session/history.py :: resume(session_id)` restoring the context from the transcript including the partial turn; `runtime/loop.py` continuing from the last assistant content. | `test_recovery.py::test_resume_sees_partial_output` — interrupt mid-turn, resume, assert the partial text is in the restored context. | A resume that drops partial work forces redundant re-execution of side effects. Mitigation: the transcript is the source of truth for restoration. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S6-CANCEL-056 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F10, §6 L16; area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H15 | A cancelled turn leaves the session in a resumable, explicitly unfinished state; the harness reports the turn as unfinished rather than completed, and the operator is given a way to continue. | S6-CANCEL-042, S6-RECOVER-044 | `runtime/loop.py :: TurnOutcome(status=Literal['complete','interrupted','error','limit','unfinished'])`; the `unfinished` status is set when the turn is abandoned by a limit or an error mid-tool. | `test_cancel.py::test_unfinished_status_distinct_from_error` and `::test_session_resumable_after_cancel`. | Reporting an abandoned turn as complete would make a pipeline believe the work was done. Mitigation: an explicit `unfinished` status, asserted. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S6-APPROVE-057 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 8 §5.4, F-7; area8-permissions.md §5.5 | A denial is surfaced to the operator in a machine-readable form (a structured denial record) in addition to the model-facing text, so that automation can react without parsing prose. | S6-DENIAL-038, S1-RESULT-011 | `protocol/envelope.py :: PermissionDenial(tool, specifier, reason, reason_type, decided_by)`; the envelope's `permission_denials` array is populated for every denial. | `test_denial_event.py::test_denials_appear_in_envelope` — assert the envelope's `permission_denials` is non-empty after a denied call and that the run's overall status is still success. | A run that had a denial is still a successful *run*; conflating the two breaks CI. Mitigation: denials are a separate array on the envelope, and a test asserts the run status is unaffected. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S6-MOUNT-058 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 8 §4.3; area8-permissions.md §4.3 | A removal that targets a mounted drive raises a distinct prompt rather than being treated as an ordinary out-of-scope path. | S6-CRIT-021, S6-PATH-045 | `permissions/critical.py :: detect_mounted_target(path) -> Optional[MountInfo]`; the decision distinguishes `mounted` from `out_of_scope`. | `test_mounted.py::test_mounted_target_uses_distinct_prompt`. | A mounted drive can hold data the user did not know was attached. Mitigation: a distinct decision kind with its own prompt. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S6-CONF-059 | ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION (the destructive-command analyser's algorithm is listed as an open question in agent-evidence.md §7/§11; the *inputs* it must consider are observed) | The destructive-command analyser classifies a command into `safe`, `destructive-guardable` or `prohibited`, and a `prohibited` classification is never overridable by a mode, an allow rule or a hook. | S6-CRIT-021, S6-CLS-026 | `permissions/analyser.py :: classify(command) -> Classification` with `Classification(kind, rule_id, overridable: bool)`; `permissions/engine.py` refusing `overridable=False` classifications unconditionally. | `test_analyser.py::test_prohibited_classification_never_overridden` — attempt the same action under every mode with a permissive allow rule and assert refusal. | A class of action that can be overridden by configuration is not prohibited. Mitigation: the `overridable` flag is a field of the classification and the prohibited path is tested against every mode. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S6-CONF-060 | ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION (the `permissions.allow` / `permissions.ask` / `permissions.deny` key names are observed in agent-evidence.md §4 Area 8 §9.2; the merge semantics across files are not directly characterised) | The three rule lists (`permissions.allow`, `permissions.ask`, `permissions.deny`) merge across settings scopes by concatenation, with the scope order determining which rule is encountered first within a list, and the deny-before-ask-before-allow evaluation still applying to the merged list. | S6-RULE-010, S6-SETTINGS-050 | `config/loader.py :: merge_rule_lists(scopes) -> RuleList` concatenating in scope order; `permissions/engine.py` evaluating the merged list. | `test_rule_merge.py::test_lists_concatenate_in_scope_order` and `::test_merged_list_still_deny_first`. | Merge semantics decide whether a project can append to a user's allow list. Mitigation: the merge is one function with a documented order, tested. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |


---

## S7 — Test execution, error diagnosis, iterative repair

**Priority band:** P0 parity-core.

**Evidence base.** `agent-evidence.md` §4 Area 6 (F1–F20) and §6 (L1, L4, L5, L9, L11, L14, L16, L17, L18, L23); `agent-reference-shots/areas/area6-tests-errors.md` §2 (the F1–F20 detail, including F13/F14/F16/F17/F19/F20); `agent-reference-shots/tool-error-contract.json` (the ordered denial event trace); `agent-reference-shots/tool-result-dual-representation.json` (observed result shapes); `docs-cache/errors.md` (the error taxonomy strings).

**Scope note.** S7 owns the *failure contract*: what a failure looks like to the model, to the operator and to automation, and the rules that decide whether a failure is retried, re-sent, or left alone. The repair loop itself is model-driven; the harness supplies text and boundaries.
| ID | Evidence | Acceptance criterion (testable) | Depends on | Proposed module + interface shape | Automated verification test | Security / operational risk + mitigation | Priority / Phase |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| S7-RESULT-001 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F1; area6-tests-errors.md F1 | A successful tool call yields a typed `tool_use_result` object; a failed or denied call yields a **string**. The harness never emits a partially-populated object for a failure. | S5-CMD-002 | `protocol/toolresult.py :: encode(outcome) -> dict / str`; the type of the encoded value is decided by the outcome kind, not by field presence. | `test_tool_result.py::test_failure_never_produces_object` — encode a failure and assert `isinstance(payload, str)`. | A hybrid shape (object with `is_error`) is what many harnesses produce and what parsers mis-handle. Mitigation: the type is decided in one place and pinned by a test. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S7-RESULT-002 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F1; area6-tests-errors.md F1 | A successful shell result object contains exactly `stdout`, `stderr`, `interrupted`, `isImage` and `noOutputExpected`. | S7-RESULT-001 | `protocol/toolresult.py :: ShellToolResult` dataclass with those five fields and no others. | `test_tool_result.py::test_success_object_field_set_exact` — assert the key set equals the five names. | Extra fields invite a consumer to depend on them. Mitigation: exact-set assertion. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S7-RESULT-003 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F2, §4 Area 5 F1; area6-tests-errors.md F2 | The exit code is delivered as a prepended `Exit code N` line in the model-facing text, and a failure text is `Exit code N\n` followed by the output. | S5-CMD-004 | `tools/shell.py :: format_failure(code, text) -> str` with the exact `Exit code {code}\n` prefix. | `test_tool_result.py::test_failure_text_format` — assert the exact prefix and that the output follows on the next line. | Machine readability depends on an exact prefix. Mitigation: the prefix is a constant and is asserted literally. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S7-RESULT-004 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F3, §4 Area 5 F2; area6-tests-errors.md F3 | A non-zero exit is a failure except for the documented benign-exit-1 allowlist; the failure is signalled by the `Error: ` prefix on the machine-facing payload and the `Exit code N` line on the model-facing text. | S5-CMD-005, S7-RESULT-001 | `tools/shell.py :: is_failure(command, exit_code) -> bool`; `protocol/toolresult.py` prefixing `Error: ` when the outcome is a failure. | `test_exit_codes.py::test_benign_and_non_benign_classified` and `::test_failure_payload_prefixed`. | Misclassification in either direction causes repair loops or silent failures. Mitigation: one classifier used by both the prefix decision and the repair-loop trigger. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S7-RESULT-005 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F4; area6-tests-errors.md F4 | A permission denial is delivered as a **failed** tool result, and the overall run is still reported as successful; the denial is recorded separately on the envelope. | S6-DENIAL-057, S7-RESULT-001 | `runtime/loop.py` marking a denied call as `Outcome(kind='failure')` while the run status remains success; `protocol/envelope.py :: permission_denials`. | `test_denial.py::test_denial_is_failed_tool_result_but_successful_run`. | Conflating a denial with a run failure makes every gated run look broken. Mitigation: the separation is asserted. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S7-OUTPUT-006 | agent-evidence.md §5.8, §4 Area 6 F6; area6-tests-errors.md F6 | Large command output is **persisted and replaced by a wrapper**, not truncated inline; the wrapper names the file path so the model can retrieve the rest deliberately. | S5-OUTPUT-006 | `output/persist.py :: persist_output`; the model-facing text is the wrapper, the machine payload carries the reference. | `test_output_ladder.py::test_wrapper_replaces_inline_output` — assert the inline text is absent and the path is present. | A silently truncated output makes the model draw conclusions from a partial result. Mitigation: replacement is explicit and the path is always given. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S7-OUTPUT-007 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F7, §6 L1; area6-tests-errors.md F7 | Failure output gets **less** inline text than success output and no file path; the failure text is a head-and-tail excerpt. | S5-OUTPUT-007 | `output/truncate.py :: excerpt(text, head, tail)` used only on the failure path. | `test_output_ladder.py::test_failure_excerpt_shape` — assert the head and tail are present and the middle is elided with a marker. | The asymmetry is a deliberate reference behaviour (a known limitation). Mitigation: pinned by a test so any improvement is an explicit change. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S7-DENIAL-010 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F9, §4 Area 8 F-7; area6-tests-errors.md F9 | A denial reason is specific, names the offending fragment, and tells the model what would have been required — so the agent can ask a targeted question instead of retrying blindly. | S6-DENIAL-039 | `permissions/engine.py :: denial_text(decision) -> str` with a template that includes the offending fragment and the required-approval statement. | `test_denial_text.py::test_reason_is_actionable` — assert the text contains the fragment, the required-approval clause and no generic-only wording. | A vague denial causes repeated identical attempts and wasted turns. Mitigation: the template is a single function and is shape-tested. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S7-DENIAL-011 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F9, §4 Area 5 F13; area5-bash-permissions.md F13 | A denial names the *part* of a compound command that required approval, e.g. that the trailing `exit 3` of a multi-operation command is the offending fragment. | S5-CMD-012, S7-DENIAL-010 | `permissions/engine.py` reporting the specific `Operation` that failed within a compound command. | `test_denial_text.py::test_denial_names_the_offending_subcommand`. | Blame on the whole command sends the model to rewrite safe parts. Mitigation: the failing operation is carried on the `Decision` and rendered. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S7-DENIAL-012 | agent-evidence.md §6 L9; area6-tests-errors.md F4 | A denial occupies a tool-result slot in the conversation: the model receives a tool result for the denied call, so the turn continues rather than aborting. | S6-DENIAL-038, S4-LOOP-010 | `runtime/loop.py` emitting a `tool_result` for a denied call and continuing the turn. | `test_denial.py::test_turn_continues_after_denial` — assert the assistant produces further content after the denial and the turn terminates normally. | Aborting the turn on a denial wastes the model's remaining reasoning. Mitigation: the denial is a tool result, and the test asserts continuation. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S7-REPAIR-012 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F8; area6-tests-errors.md F8 | The repair loop is **model-driven**: the harness supplies the failure text and the model decides what to do next; the harness does not synthesise a fix, retry the same call, or apply a repair strategy. | S4-LOOP-010, S7-RESULT-003 | `runtime/loop.py :: LoopPolicy` with no automatic-repair strategy; the failure text is placed in the tool result and the loop awaits the model's next turn. | `test_repair.py::test_no_automatic_retry_of_failed_call` — assert the same tool call is not re-issued by the harness after a failure. | An automatic retry can repeat a side effect. Mitigation: the harness has no retry strategy for tool failures; only the model decides. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S7-TEST-013 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F10; area6-tests-errors.md F10 | A failing test suite is a failed tool call whose text is the test output; there is no special-casing of test frameworks, and the exit code follows the ordinary rules. | S5-CMD-004, S7-RESULT-004 | no special module; the ordinary shell path handles it. The test asserts the *absence* of special-casing. | `test_repair.py::test_failing_tests_are_ordinary_failures` — run a failing test command and assert the result shape is the ordinary failure shape with the output as text. | Special-casing test frameworks creates divergent contracts. Mitigation: the parity test asserts the ordinary path is used. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S7-API-014 | area6-tests-errors.md F13; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F13 | A retryable API failure emits a `system`/`api_retry` event before retrying, carrying `attempt` (1-based), `max_retries`, `retry_delay_ms`, `error_status` (nullable), an optional `no_response` object, a provider-independent `error` category, `uuid` and `session_id`. | S1-CLI-005, S13-PROVIDER-018 | `protocol/events.py :: ApiRetryEvent`; `transport/retry.py :: RetryPolicy` with `error` drawn from a closed category enum of thirteen values. | `test_api_retry.py::test_retry_event_shape` and `::test_error_category_is_provider_independent` — assert the category enum and that `error_status` may be null. | A provider-independent category lets a host branch on meaning rather than on HTTP status. Mitigation: the category is a closed enum and the status is a separate field. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S7-API-015 | area6-tests-errors.md F13; docs-cache/errors.md | Transient failures are retried up to 10 times with exponential backoff; the budget and the backoff base and ceiling are configurable, and a watchdog mode retries the capacity class of errors indefinitely up to a per-request wait ceiling. | S7-API-014 | `transport/retry.py :: RetryPolicy(max_retries=10, base_delay_ms, max_delay_ms)`; `transport/retry.py :: RetryCategory = {RATE_LIMIT, OVERLOADED, ...}`; watchdog options read from the documented environment variables. | `test_api_retry.py::test_retry_budget_ten` and `::test_backoff_is_exponential_with_ceiling` and `::test_watchdog_only_for_capacity_class`. | Retrying a terminal class (auth, billing) wastes the budget and delays the error. Mitigation: the retryable/terminal split is explicit and tested. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S7-API-016 | area6-tests-errors.md F13 | For a rejected credential, the first two retries are made quietly with no event emitted, and the event is emitted from the third consecutive retry onward; the quiet retries still count toward the attempt number. | S7-API-014 | `transport/retry.py :: quiet_retry_budget = 2` for the credential-rejection case; `transport/retry.py` suppressing the event for attempts below the threshold while incrementing the counter. | `test_api_retry.py::test_quiet_retries_count_but_do_not_emit` — assert no event for attempts 1–2 and an event from attempt 3, with `attempt` continuing to increment. | Silent retries can hide a broken credential from an operator watching the stream. Mitigation: the behaviour is bounded and documented; a diagnostic is written at the threshold. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S7-MIDRESP-010 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F14, §6 L16; area6-tests-errors.md F14 | A partially-delivered response is **never re-sent**. If a failure occurs after a text or tool-call block has completed, the completed output is preserved and the error states that the response above may be incomplete; the operator resolves it by replying `continue`. | S6-RECOVER-044, S7-API-014 | `transport/stream.py :: ResponseStream` with a `committed: bool` flag set when the first content block completes; `transport/retry.py :: on_failure(committed) -> Action` with `Action.retry()` only when nothing was committed. | `test_midresponse.py::test_committed_response_is_never_resent` — fail the stream after a completed tool-call block and assert the provider is not called again; `::test_error_text_states_may_be_incomplete`. | This is the highest-hazard rule in the document: a naive retry would execute a tool call twice (a second `rm`, a second `git push`, a second deploy). Mitigation: the `committed` flag gates every retry path, and the test asserts the provider is not re-called. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S7-MIDRESP-011 | area6-tests-errors.md F14 | A failure *before* any content block is committed is retried: a drop after thinking but before text or a tool call is retried up to two times in quick succession, and a stalled pre-response stream is aborted and retried at most once under the normal rules. | S7-MIDRESP-010, S7-API-015 | `transport/retry.py :: pre_response_retry_limit = 2` and `stall_retry_limit = 1`, applied only while `committed` is false. | `test_midresponse.py::test_pre_response_failure_is_retried` and `::test_stalled_stream_retried_at_most_once`. | The two retry regimes must not be confused. Mitigation: the branch on `committed` is a single function with tests on both sides. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S7-MIDRESP-012 | area6-tests-errors.md F14 | The mid-response failure is reported with a specific message family that names the cause (server error, connection lost, computer slept, response stopped arriving, part never arrived, malformed stream) and always ends with the incomplete-response warning. | S7-MIDRESP-010 | `transport/errors.py :: mid_response_message(cause) -> str` with a closed cause enum and a fixed suffix. | `test_midresponse.py::test_message_family_cause_specific` — parameterise over the causes and assert the suffix is present in each. | A generic message gives the model no basis for deciding whether to retry or to re-read state. Mitigation: cause-specific messages with a fixed, testable suffix. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S7-DISK-014 | area6-tests-errors.md F16; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F16 | Disk and temp-filesystem failures have a taxonomy of three distinguishable conditions: the command could not start (nothing ran), the command ran but its output could not be captured (side effects may have happened), and the temp directory itself is unsafe to use (refused to start). | S7-RESULT-004 | `output/temp.py :: TempDirError` with `kind=Literal['not_started','output_lost','refused']`; `exec/runner.py` mapping errno (`ENOSPC`, `EDQUOT`, inode exhaustion) to the kinds. | `test_disk.py::test_three_conditions_distinguished` — simulate each and assert the distinct message family. | Telling the user 'disk full' when the command actually ran and wrote files is a material misrepresentation. Mitigation: the three kinds are separate and shape-tested. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S7-DISK-015 | area6-tests-errors.md F16 | A temp directory with unexpected ownership or unreadable permissions is **refused** rather than used, with a message pointing at a configurable override; this fails closed because a pre-created directory could be a symlink or an attack. | S7-DISK-014 | `output/temp.py :: validate_temp_dir(path) -> None` checking readability and ownership; `output/temp.py :: TEMP_DIR_ENV` override. | `test_disk.py::test_wrong_owner_temp_dir_refused` — create a directory with mismatched ownership metadata and assert refusal. | A hostile pre-created temp directory is a classic privilege-escalation vector. Mitigation: refuse, and offer an operator-controlled override rather than using it anyway. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 2 |
| S7-HOOK-018 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F17, §4 Area 8 §9.5; area6-tests-errors.md F17 | A hook failure is surfaced with the hook's name and command in the notice, and the notice distinguishes a blocking error from a non-blocking one; on `PreToolUse` the notice appears in the transcript. | S6-HOOK-047 | `hooks/exec.py :: hook_error_notice(spec, outcome) -> str` including the hook name and the command with `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}` expanded. | `test_hook_verdict.py::test_notice_names_hook_and_command`. | An unlabelled hook error makes it impossible to tell which policy fired. Mitigation: the notice template is fixed and asserted. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S7-HOOK-019 | area6-tests-errors.md F17 | `PostToolUse` and `PostToolUseFailure` are distinct events (success and failure), and a `PostToolUse` hook may replace the tool's result via `updatedToolOutput`; exit 2 on `PostToolUse` does **not** undo the completed action. | S6-HOOK-047, S7-RESULT-001 | `hooks/dispatch.py :: PostToolUseDispatcher` with `updatedToolOutput` applied to the outgoing tool result; `protocol/toolresult.py` accepting a replacement. | `test_hook_post.py::test_post_hook_can_replace_result` and `::test_exit2_does_not_undo_action` and `::test_failure_event_distinct`. | A post hook that rewrites the result is a powerful capability; it must not be able to misrepresent a side effect as undone. Mitigation: the action's completion is tracked independently of the hook verdict. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S7-MCP-020 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F18; area6-tests-errors.md F18 | An MCP server failure is surfaced in three places: a status field in the init event, a startup error field, and a runtime text hint; a failing server does not prevent the session from starting. | S8-TRANSPORT-002, S1-CLI-006 | `protocol/events.py :: InitEvent.mcp_servers: list[McpServerStatus]` with `McpServerStatus(name, status, source)`; `mcp/manager.py` recording the startup error separately. | `test_mcp_failure.py::test_server_failure_does_not_block_start` and `::test_status_and_error_recorded`. | A hard failure at startup would make one broken server unusable for everything. Mitigation: per-server isolation, with the failure recorded rather than swallowed. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S7-AGENT-021 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F19; area6-tests-errors.md F19 | A tool-call failure inside a delegated agent degrades differently from the main loop: a partial text with no tool calls causes the subagent to be asked to continue; a foreground subagent with partial text returns that text with a cut-off note; a subagent with no text or only tool calls fails with `Agent terminated early due to an API error`; a background subagent is marked failed but its last output is included. | S10-AGENT-020, S7-MIDRESP-010 | `agents/runner.py :: degrade(outcome) -> DelegatedOutcome` with `DelegatedOutcome(status, partial_text, note)`; the four cases are distinguished by a single function. | `test_agent_degradation.py::test_four_degradation_cases` — table-driven over (partial text, tool calls, foreground/background). | Partial work must never be lost. Mitigation: the degradation preserves text in every branch, and the test asserts each branch's output. | P0 parity-core / Phase 4 |
| S7-ERRORS-022 | area6-tests-errors.md F20; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F20 | Read and Edit failures refuse rather than corrupt: a non-UTF-8 file is refused with a model-directed message naming two remedies, an image over 128 MB is refused, and a binary extension is refused. | S3-READ-004, S3-EDIT-017 | `files/encoding.py` and `tools/read.py` refusals (see S3); the S7 obligation is that each refusal is *specific* and states that nothing was written. | `test_read_edit_refusals.py::test_each_refusal_states_nothing_written`. | A refusal that does not state the file is unchanged invites the model to report a false failure or to retry destructively. Mitigation: the refusal texts are fixed and shape-tested. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S7-DIAG-023 | agent-evidence.md §6 L5, L12, §4 Area 6 F12 | Diagnostics are written to stderr, are prefixed with a stable tag, and stdout carries only the result; an unrecognised model id produces a `[claude-code:unrecognized_model]` diagnostic on stderr rather than a stdout banner. | S1-CLI-013, S13-MODEL-030 | `output/writer.py :: write_diagnostic(tag, message)` emitting `[claude-code:<tag>] <message>` on stderr. | `test_diagnostics.py::test_diagnostic_prefix_and_stream`. | Diagnostics on stdout break JSON parsing (observed L5). Mitigation: a single diagnostic sink with a hard stream rule. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S7-COMPACT-024 | area6-tests-errors.md F15; agent-evidence.md §6 L11 | A prompt that exceeds the context window triggers a compaction ladder; compaction is a visible pause of tens of seconds and emits a `compact_boundary` event, so the delay is attributable rather than mysterious. | S11-COMPACT-004, S4-LOOP-010 | `context/compaction.py :: compact(context) -> CompactionResult`; `protocol/events.py :: CompactBoundaryEvent`; the ladder is `prompt-too-long → compact → retry`. | `test_compaction.py::test_prompt_too_long_triggers_compaction_and_retry` — assert a compaction event is emitted and the turn completes. | An unexplained multi-second pause looks like a hang. Mitigation: the boundary event is emitted with a duration, and the ladder is tested end to end. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |

## S8 — MCP integration & tool discovery

**Priority band:** P0 parity-core.

**Evidence base.** `agent-evidence.md` §4 Area 9 (F9-1 … F9-20) and §4 Area 6 F18; `agent-reference-shots/areas/area6-tests-errors.md` §"Area 9 Findings" (F9-1 … F9-20 detail); `agent-reference-shots/mcp-debug-log.txt` (an observed HTTP transport lifecycle: init options, connect timeout, version negotiation, reconnect/backoff); `agent-reference-shots/PROTOCOL-NOTES.md` (the `mcp_servers` init field and the `mcp_servers: []` mutation under `--bare`/`--restricted`); `docs-cache/mcp.md` (transports, scopes, OAuth, elicitation); `docs-cache/connectors.md` and `docs-cache/third-party-integrations.md`.

**Scope note.** S8 owns the server lifecycle, the tool namespace, the deferral mechanism, and the enterprise policy layer. The *discovery* of tools through `ToolSearch` is the boundary with the model loop and is specified here.
| ID | Evidence | Acceptance criterion (testable) | Depends on | Proposed module + interface shape | Automated verification test | Security / operational risk + mitigation | Priority / Phase |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| S8-TRANSPORT-001 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-1; area6-tests-errors.md F9-1 | Four MCP transports are supported, discriminated by a `type` field: `http`, `sse`, `stdio` and `webSocket`. A server whose transport type is not recognised is dropped silently rather than failing the session. | none | `mcp/transport.py :: Transport` protocol with `HttpTransport`, `SseTransport`, `StdioTransport`, `WebSocketTransport`; `mcp/transport.py :: make_transport(spec) -> Optional[Transport]` returning `None` for an unknown type. | `test_mcp_transport.py::test_four_transport_types` and `::test_unknown_type_dropped_silently` — assert the drop produces no exception and the server is absent from the init event. | A silent drop hides a configuration error. Mitigation: the drop is silent at the session level but logged at debug level, and the server's absence is visible in the init event. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S8-TRANSPORT-002 | agent-reference-shots/mcp-debug-log.txt; area6-tests-errors.md F9-17 | The HTTP transport initiates with an explicit options object — the URL, the request headers, whether an auth provider is present, and a timeout of 60,000 ms — and the connection timeout is 30,000 ms, with a version-negotiation probe that may fall back to a pinned legacy path. | S8-TRANSPORT-001 | `mcp/http.py :: HttpTransport.connect()` sending the documented init options; `mcp/http.py :: CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000`, `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 60_000`. | `test_mcp_http.py::test_init_options_shape` — assert the options object carries url, headers, hasAuthProvider and timeoutMs. | Timeouts must be bounded or a dead server stalls the session. Mitigation: both the connect and request timeouts are constants, and a timeout is a testable failure. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S8-TRANSPORT-003 | agent-reference-shots/mcp-debug-log.txt | A transport failure is retried with backoff — the log shows consecutive attempts with increasing delay — and the failure modes surfaced are distinguishable (host not found, connection closed). | S8-TRANSPORT-002 | `mcp/transport.py :: connect_with_backoff(transport, attempts, backoff) -> ConnectResult` with `ConnectResult(status, attempts, last_error)`. | `test_mcp_retry.py::test_backoff_between_attempts` and `::test_failure_kinds_distinguished`. | Retrying a permanent failure (host not found) wastes the session's start-up budget. Mitigation: the error kind is captured and permanent kinds short-circuit the retry loop. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S8-SCOPE-004 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-3; area6-tests-errors.md F9-3 | MCP servers live in three scopes — local, project and user — stored in two files, with the project scope keyed per project. The scope determines who can add a server and whether it persists. | S8-CONF-018, S1-SESSION-015 | `mcp/config.py :: McpScope` enum (`local`, `project`, `user`); `mcp/config.py :: load(scope, project_key) -> list[ServerSpec]`; `mcp/config.py :: store(scope, server)` writing to the two storage files. | `test_mcp_scopes.py::test_three_scopes_two_files` and `::test_project_scope_keyed_per_project`. | Scope decides whether a server survives a project change; a mis-scoped write can leak a credential-bearing server into every project. Mitigation: the scope-to-file mapping is a constant, and the project key is always applied. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S8-APPROVE-005 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-4; area6-tests-errors.md F9-4 | A project `.mcp.json` is **inert until a human approves it**; before approval its servers are not started and its tools are not published. | S8-SCOPE-004, S6-APPROVE-036 | `mcp/approval.py :: project_approval_state(project_key) -> ApprovalState`; `mcp/manager.py` skipping unapproved project servers and recording them in the init event as pending. | `test_mcp_project_approval.py::test_unapproved_project_servers_not_started` — assert no connection attempt and that the tools are absent. | Auto-starting a repository-supplied server would let a cloned repository run arbitrary code. Mitigation: the approval gate is checked before the transport is created, and it is tested. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S8-AUTH-006 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-5; area6-tests-errors.md F9-5 | OAuth is a shell-driven flow: the authorisation URL is presented, the user completes it in a browser, and the resulting secret is stored outside the plain configuration file (in the credential store) rather than in the config JSON. | S8-CONF-018 | `mcp/oauth.py :: start_flow(server) -> OAuthFlow(url, state)`; `mcp/oauth.py :: complete_flow(flow, code) -> TokenRef` where `TokenRef` is a handle into the credential store, not the token itself. | `test_mcp_oauth.py::test_token_not_written_to_config` — assert the config file contains no token material after a flow completes. | Writing a token into a config file that is often committed is a credential leak. Mitigation: the token lives in the credential store; the config holds only a reference. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S8-HEADER-007 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-6; area6-tests-errors.md F9-6 | `headersHelper` supplies per-connection credentials dynamically, and the helper is subject to a trust gate: it runs only after the user has trusted the server, and it has a 10-second ceiling. | S8-AUTH-006, S6-TRUST-033 | `mcp/headers.py :: HeadersHelper.run(server) -> dict[str,str]` with `HEADERS_HELPER_TIMEOUT_S = 10`; the helper is gated on the server's trust state. | `test_mcp_headers.py::test_helper_requires_trust` and `::test_helper_timeout_enforced`. | A helper that runs arbitrary code to mint headers is a code-execution surface; running it before trust is a breach. Mitigation: the trust gate precedes invocation and the timeout is a hard ceiling. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S8-ENV-008 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-7; area6-tests-errors.md F9-7 | Environment-variable references in a server configuration are expanded at load time, with a special case for credential variables that are resolved from the credential store rather than from the process environment. | S8-SCOPE-004 | `mcp/config.py :: expand_env(spec, resolver) -> ServerSpec`; `mcp/config.py :: CredentialResolver` interface with `resolve(name) -> str`. | `test_mcp_env.py::test_expansion_applied` and `::test_credential_var_uses_credential_store`. | Expansion happens before the server starts; a credential var must not be echoed in logs or in the init event. Mitigation: the resolver is a separate interface and expanded values are redacted from diagnostics. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S8-NAME-009 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-8; area6-tests-errors.md F9-8 | MCP tools are named `mcp__<server>__<tool>` in the model's tool schema, with a distinct plugin-scoped variant for servers contributed by a plugin. | S8-DEFER-011, S1-TOOLSET-022 | `mcp/naming.py :: tool_name(server, tool, plugin=None) -> str` implementing both variants; `mcp/naming.py :: parse_tool_name(name) -> (server, tool)`. | `test_mcp_naming.py::test_names_and_round_trip` — assert the generated name and that parsing recovers the server and tool. | A name collision between a local and a plugin server would silently shadow a tool. Mitigation: the plugin variant is a distinct form and the round-trip is tested. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S8-RESOURCE-010 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-9; area6-tests-errors.md F9-9 | MCP resources are referenced with `@server:protocol://resource/path`; the reference is resolved against the connected servers and the resource content is fetched into the turn. | S2-MENTION-012 | `mcp/resources.py :: resolve_reference(text, servers) -> Optional[ResourceRef]` with `ResourceRef(server, uri)`; `mcp/resources.py :: read(ref) -> str`. | `test_mcp_resources.py::test_reference_parsed_and_fetched`. | A resource URI may point at a sensitive endpoint on a connected server. Mitigation: the reference must name a configured server, and fetching goes through the tool-permission path. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S8-PROMPT-011 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-10; area6-tests-errors.md F9-10 | MCP prompts become slash commands, so a server-contributed prompt is invocable as a command in the command menu. | S9-CMD-014, S8-NAME-009 | `mcp/prompts.py :: discover_prompts(server) -> list[PromptSpec]`; `commands/registry.py` registering each as a command with the server's namespace. | `test_mcp_prompts.py::test_prompt_becomes_command` — assert the command appears in the menu source list with the server prefix. | A server-contributed command is executable content from a third party. Mitigation: the command's body is treated as a prompt from an untrusted source and is marked as such. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S8-DEFER-012 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-11; area6-tests-errors.md F9-11 | MCP tool definitions are **deferred**: the server's tools are not published in the initial tool schema; they are discovered at run time through a tool-search facility that loads them on demand. | S8-NAME-009, S1-TOOLSET-022 | `tools/toolsearch.py :: ToolSearch` with `search(query) -> list[ToolSpec]` and `load(spec) -> ToolSpec`; the registry keeps deferred tools out of the published schema until loaded. | `test_tool_search.py::test_deferred_tools_absent_until_loaded` and `::test_search_returns_matching_specs`. | Deferral is what makes a large MCP catalogue affordable in context. Mitigation: the published schema and the load path are both driven by the registry, so they cannot diverge. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S8-OUTPUT-013 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-12; area6-tests-errors.md F9-12 | MCP tool output has a ceiling with a spill-to-disk escape hatch, so an oversized result does not flood the context. | S5-OUTPUT-006, S7-OUTPUT-006 | `mcp/limits.py :: MAX_TOOL_OUTPUT_CHARS` with `mcp/output.py :: spill_to_disk(text, session_dir) -> Path`. | `test_mcp_output.py::test_oversize_output_spills_to_disk` — assert the file exists and the returned text references it. | A malicious or careless server can return an enormous payload. Mitigation: the ceiling is enforced by the client regardless of the server's cooperation. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S8-ISOLATE-014 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-13; area6-tests-errors.md F9-13 | Failure isolation: one bad tool call does not take down the server or the session; the failure is reported per call. | S7-MCP-020 | `mcp/session.py :: call_tool(name, args) -> ToolCallResult` with `ToolCallResult(ok, content, error)`; a thrown error is converted into a failed result rather than propagating. | `test_mcp_isolation.py::test_failing_tool_does_not_kill_session` — make one tool throw and assert a subsequent call succeeds. | A server crash must not end the user's session. Mitigation: the error is converted at the client boundary. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S8-REQ-015 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-14; area6-tests-errors.md F9-14 | A tool may declare that it requires user interaction, via the metadata key `anthropic/requiresUserInteraction`; such a tool always requires approval, regardless of the permission rules that would otherwise allow it. | S6-RULE-010, S6-HOOK-046 | `mcp/tools.py :: requires_interaction(meta) -> bool` reading `_meta['anthropic/requiresUserInteraction']`; `permissions/engine.py` forcing an `ask` for such tools. | `test_mcp_interaction.py::test_requires_interaction_forces_approval` — assert the call is prompted even under a permissive allow rule. | The declaration is the server's own claim; a server could omit it. Mitigation: the rule is server-declared but enforced by the client, and the client-side default for an unknown tool is to require approval if the server is not trusted. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S8-ELIC-016 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-15, §6 L20; area6-tests-errors.md F9-15 | MCP elicitation supports two modes — a form the user fills in, and a URL the user visits — and the URL form has a length cap of roughly 8,000 characters (~4,000 escaped) because the URL is carried in-band. | S8-ELIC-017 | `mcp/elicitation.py :: Elicitation(kind=Literal['form','url'], payload)` with `MAX_URL_CHARS = 8_000`; over-long URLs are refused with an explanatory error. | `test_mcp_elicitation.py::test_url_length_cap` — assert a URL at the cap is accepted and one over it is refused. | An unbounded URL is a payload-injection and log-flooding vector. Mitigation: a hard cap with a clear refusal. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S8-ELIC-017 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-15, §6 L20 | An elicitation result is surfaced as a distinct event and the exchange is recorded; the elicitation is a blocking interaction with the user, not a background prompt. | S8-ELIC-016 | `protocol/events.py :: ElicitationEvent` and `ElicitationResultEvent`; `runtime/loop.py` blocking the turn until the result arrives. | `test_mcp_elicitation.py::test_elicitation_blocks_and_records`. | An elicitation that times out silently would apply a default the user never chose. Mitigation: the turn blocks, and a timeout is an explicit failure. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S8-SERVE-018 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-16; area6-tests-errors.md F9-16 | `claude mcp serve` makes the product itself an MCP server, so a host application can drive it over MCP rather than through the CLI. | S1-CLI-001, S8-NAME-009 | `mcp/serve.py :: serve(transport) -> None` exposing the session's tools and the result envelope over the MCP protocol. | `test_mcp_serve.py::test_serve_exposes_result_envelope` — connect a client and assert a tool call returns the documented envelope shape. | Serving the agent to another process widens the attack surface. Mitigation: `mcp serve` runs with the same permission engine and never bypasses it. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S8-POLICY-019 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-19; area6-tests-errors.md F9-19 | An enterprise policy file (`managed-mcp.json`) provides allow and deny lists for servers with a defined evaluation order, and takes exclusive control of which servers may be used. | S6-RULESET-053, S8-SCOPE-004 | `mcp/policy.py :: ManagedMcpPolicy` loaded from the managed location, with `evaluate(server_spec) -> PolicyVerdict`; `mcp/manager.py` consulting the policy before any other source. | `test_mcp_policy.py::test_managed_policy_is_exclusive` and `::test_evaluation_order`. | A managed policy that is merely advisory is not a policy. Mitigation: the policy is consulted first and can refuse a server that another scope would have allowed. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S8-CONN-020 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-20; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 12 F7 | Account-scoped connectors exist as a distinct class of server source, gated by the account and by the network allowlist; a connector that is not available is omitted rather than surfaced as a broken server. | S8-TRANSPORT-001, S13-PROVIDER-020 | `mcp/connectors.py :: ConnectorProvider` interface with `available() -> list[ConnectorSpec]`; the manager merges provider results with configured servers. | `test_mcp_connectors.py::test_unavailable_connector_omitted`. | Account-scoped connectors reach outside the local machine. Mitigation: availability is checked before the server is created and the network allowlist applies. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S8-CLI-021 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-2; area6-tests-errors.md F9-2 | A complete `claude mcp` command surface exists for managing servers — add, remove, list, get, and serve — with a documented flag set, and `claude mcp list` reports each server's name, scope and status. | S8-SCOPE-004 | `cli/mcp.py :: McpCommand` with subcommands `add`, `remove`, `list`, `get`, `serve`; `cli/mcp.py :: render_list(servers) -> str`. | `test_mcp_cli.py::test_list_output_columns` and `::test_add_requires_type_and_command`. | A management command that silently writes to the wrong scope is a configuration hazard. Mitigation: the scope is an explicit argument with a documented default. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S8-CLI-022 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-2; PROTOCOL-NOTES.md | The `mcp_servers` field of the init event lists each server with its name, status and source, and is an empty list when no servers are configured or when the run is `--bare` or `--restricted`. | S8-SCOPE-004, S1-SESSION-019 | `protocol/events.py :: InitEvent.mcp_servers: list[McpServerStatus]`; `McpServerStatus(name, status, source)`. | `test_init_mcp.py::test_mcp_servers_field_shape` and `::test_empty_under_bare_and_restricted`. | The init event is a manifest; a wrong server list misleads a host. Mitigation: the field is generated from the same manager that starts the servers. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S8-PLUGIN-023 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-18; area6-tests-errors.md F9-18 | A plugin may contribute MCP servers, and a plugin's servers are namespaced so they cannot collide with the user's own. | S8-NAME-009, S9-PLUGIN-026 | `mcp/manager.py` accepting `ServerSpec` objects with a `plugin` origin; `mcp/naming.py` applying the plugin-scoped name variant. | `test_mcp_plugin.py::test_plugin_server_is_namespaced`. | A plugin server shadowing a user's server would intercept calls. Mitigation: the namespace is part of the name and the origin is recorded. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S8-REFRESH-024 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-5, F9-6 | An OAuth token that expires mid-session is refreshed once and the call is retried once; a second failure surfaces the error rather than looping. | S8-AUTH-006, S7-API-015 | `mcp/oauth.py :: with_refresh(call) -> ToolCallResult` performing at most one refresh-and-retry. | `test_mcp_oauth.py::test_single_refresh_then_retry` and `::test_second_failure_surfaces`. | An unbounded refresh loop turns an expired credential into a hang. Mitigation: exactly one refresh attempt, then the error. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S8-MANIFEST-025 | agent-reference-shots/PROTOCOL-NOTES.md (capabilities list includes `sdk_mcp_manifests`, `sdk_mcp_tools_list_changed`) | The session publishes MCP capability flags indicating that MCP tool listings can change during the session and that manifests are available, so a host knows the tool list is not static. | S8-DEFER-012, S1-CLI-006 | `protocol/events.py :: InitEvent.capabilities: list[str]` including the MCP-related flags; `mcp/manager.py` emitting a list-changed notification when tools change. | `test_mcp_capabilities.py::test_capability_flags_present` and `::test_list_changed_notification_emitted`. | A host caching the tool list would miss newly available tools or call removed ones. Mitigation: the change notification is emitted by the manager. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S8-DEFER-026 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-11; PROTOCOL-NOTES.md | Deferred tool loading is exposed to the model through a search tool that is itself a registered tool, and a search result is a schema reference rather than the full definition, so the full definition is fetched only when the tool is selected. | S8-DEFER-012 | `tools/toolsearch.py :: search(query) -> list[ToolSummary]` with `ToolSummary(name, server, description_excerpt)`; `load(name) -> ToolSpec` returning the full schema. | `test_tool_search.py::test_search_returns_summaries_not_full_schemas` and `::test_load_returns_full_spec`. | Returning full definitions from a search would defeat the deferral. Mitigation: the summary type is deliberately narrower than the spec type, and the difference is tested. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S8-CONF-027 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-3; area6-tests-errors.md F9-3 | A server configuration supplied on the command line (`--mcp-config`) is merged with the configured servers, and the command-line servers take precedence over a configured server of the same name. | S8-SCOPE-004, S1-CLI-001 | `mcp/config.py :: merge(configured, cli_supplied) -> list[ServerSpec]` with name-keyed precedence. | `test_mcp_merge.py::test_cli_overrides_configured_by_name`. | A CLI-supplied server that silently loses to a configured one is a confusing failure. Mitigation: the merge is name-keyed and tested. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S8-CONF-028 | ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION (the reference's MCP connection-shutdown ordering was not directly characterised in agent-evidence.md; only the connection lifecycle in agent-reference-shots/mcp-debug-log.txt was observed) | On session end, MCP connections are closed in a deterministic order — session-scoped first, then project, then user — and a server that fails to close is reported without blocking shutdown. | S8-SCOPE-004, S1-SESSION-023 | `mcp/manager.py :: shutdown() -> list[CloseFailure]` closing in a fixed scope order and collecting failures. | `test_mcp_shutdown.py::test_close_order_deterministic` and `::test_failing_close_does_not_block_shutdown`. | A hanging server can keep the process alive at exit. Mitigation: each close has a timeout and failures are collected, not raised. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |

## S9 — Skills, reusable instructions, slash commands

**Priority band:** P0 parity-core.

**Evidence base.** `agent-evidence.md` §4 Area 10 (F10-1 … F10-26), §4 Area 14 (slash commands), §4 Area 3 F10-15/F10-16; `agent-reference-shots/areas/area10-skills.md` (per-finding detail); `agent-reference-shots/area-commands/agent-evidence-area-commands.md` (observed local-command dispatch and the `/context` token accounting output); `agent-reference-shots/area-commands/probe-*.sanitized.jsonl` (captured local-command envelopes); `docs-cache/commands.md` (the command-menu matching algorithm); `area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md` §2 F-I5 (menu matching).

**Scope note.** S9 owns the *instruction and command* extension surfaces: skills, the command menu and its matching algorithm, output styles, and the plugin packaging that carries them. It does not own the permission engine, but it does own the discovery and invocation semantics of command-shaped extension points.
| ID | Evidence | Acceptance criterion (testable) | Depends on | Proposed module + interface shape | Automated verification test | Security / operational risk + mitigation | Priority / Phase |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| S9-SKILL-001 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-1; area10-skills.md F10-1 | Skill discovery is a three-scope resolver (managed, user, project) plus additional scopes contributed by `--add-dir`, a plugin, or a synced location, and the resolver **prints its own search paths** so the operator can see where skills came from. | S9-SKILL-002, S2-PROJ-001 | `skills/resolver.py :: resolve_scopes(ctx) -> list[Scope]` with `Scope(kind, roots: list[Path])`; `skills/resolver.py :: describe_paths() -> str` printed by the skills listing. | `test_skill_resolver.py::test_search_paths_printed` and `::test_scope_roots_correct`. | A resolver that silently searches unexpected places is a discovery risk. Mitigation: the searched paths are printed, so the operator can audit them. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S9-SKILL-002 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-2; area10-skills.md F10-2 | Scope precedence is applied at **name resolution**, not at load time: when two scopes define a skill with the same name, exactly one wins and the other is shadowed; the loading set and the resolution set are not the same thing. | S9-SKILL-001 | `skills/resolver.py :: resolve(name) -> Optional[SkillSpec]` applying the scope precedence; `skills/loader.py :: load_all() -> list[SkillSpec]` returning every candidate. | `test_skill_resolution.py::test_highest_scope_wins` and `::test_loader_set_differs_from_resolution_set`. | Shadowing must be predictable, or a project can silently replace an operator's skill. Mitigation: precedence is a fixed order applied in one function. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S9-SKILL-003 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-3; area10-skills.md F10-3 | A nested `.claude/skills/` directory is a startup scope **only when the session starts inside it**; it is not discovered by walking downward. | S9-SKILL-001, S1-SESSION-014 | `skills/resolver.py :: scope_roots(ctx)` including the nested root only when `cwd` is inside it. | `test_skill_scopes.py::test_nested_scope_requires_cwd_inside`. | A downward walk would pick up skills from unrelated subtrees. Mitigation: the nested root is conditional on the launch directory. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S9-SKILL-004 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-4; area10-skills.md F10-4 | A skill with a `paths:` frontmatter glob is **conditional**: it is held in a separate store and becomes eligible only when a matching path is in play. | S9-SKILL-005, S3-FILE-001 | `skills/conditional.py :: ConditionalSkillStore` with `paths_glob` and `eligible(paths) -> bool`. | `test_skill_conditional.py::test_conditional_skill_not_listed_until_eligible`. | A conditional skill that is always listed dilutes the listing budget. Mitigation: conditional skills are held separately and only promoted when eligible. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S9-SKILL-005 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-5, §6 L27; area10-skills.md F10-5 | The two invocation-control flags are **not** opposites: `disable-model-invocation` hides the skill's *description* from the model (so the model will not call it), while `user-invocable: false` hides the *command* from the user's menu. Setting one does not imply the other. | S9-SKILL-006, S9-SKILL-007 | `skills/model.py :: SkillSpec(disable_model_invocation: bool, user_invocable: bool)` with the two applied to different surfaces: the description list and the command list respectively. | `test_skill_flags.py::test_disable_model_invocation_hides_description_only` and `::test_user_invocable_false_hides_command_only`. | Treating them as opposites would break a legitimate configuration (a skill the user invokes manually but the model must not). Mitigation: two independent booleans, two independent surfaces, four test assertions. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S9-SKILL-006 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-6; area10-skills.md F10-6 | `skillOverrides` has four states — `on`, `name-only`, `user-invocable-only`, `off` — and each state affects three different surfaces (description visibility, command visibility, and the command-menu entry), with a defined mapping. | S9-SKILL-005, S9-SKILL-007 | `skills/overrides.py :: SkillOverride` enum with the four values; `skills/overrides.py :: apply(spec, override) -> VisibleSurfaces(description, command, menu_entry)`. | `test_skill_overrides.py::test_four_states_map_to_documented_surfaces` — table-driven over the four states. | A mis-mapped state can hide a skill from the operator while leaving it callable by the model. Mitigation: the mapping is a pure function and is table-tested. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S9-SKILL-007 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-7; area10-skills.md F10-7 | The skill listing is a plain-text attachment with a hard character budget — 1% of the context window, or an 8,000-character fallback — where **names are always kept** and the least-used skills are truncated first; the listing is accompanied by a sidecar of names and digests. | S11-CONTEXT-010, S9-SKILL-006 | `skills/listing.py :: build_listing(skills, budget) -> Listing` with `Listing(text, names, digests)`; the budget is `min(context_window * 0.01, 8_000)` when the window is unknown. | `test_skill_listing.py::test_names_always_retained` and `::test_least_used_truncated_first` and `::test_digest_sidecar_emitted`. | A listing that lets a truncated-away skill's description persist would defeat the budget. Mitigation: truncation removes descriptions, not names, and the budget is a hard constant. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S9-SKILL-008 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-8, §6 L33; area10-skills.md F10-8 | `SKILL.md` frontmatter uses a fixed, hyphen-cased field set (the sole exception is `when_to_use`), and it has three distinct failure modes: an unknown field, a wrongly-cased field, and a malformed value — each with its own diagnostic. | S9-SKILL-007 | `skills/frontmatter.py :: parse(text) -> Frontmatter` with `KNOWN_FIELDS` (hyphen-case) and `parse_result.errors: list[FrontmatterError]` discriminated by kind. | `test_frontmatter.py::test_three_failure_modes_distinguished` — assert the error kind for an unknown field, a camelCase field and a bad value. | Silent acceptance of a mis-cased field would mean a setting that looks applied but is not. Mitigation: the three failure modes are distinct error kinds and are surfaced. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S9-SKILL-009 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-9; area10-skills.md F10-9 | The skill body is a template language: `$ARGUMENTS`, positional `$N`, declared `$name` variables and six `${CLAUDE_*}` variables (`${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}`, `${CLAUDE_EFFORT}`, `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}`, `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}`, `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`, `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}`) are substituted in the body **and** in the `allowed-tools` Bash rules, so a substituted command matches the permission rules as written. | S9-SKILL-010, S5-CMD-010 | `skills/template.py :: render(body, args, env) -> RenderedSkill` with `rendered.allowed_tools` also substituted; `skills/template.py :: VAR_PATTERN` recognising the documented forms. | `test_skill_template.py::test_substitution_in_body_and_allowed_tools` and `::test_declared_variable_replacement` and `::test_unknown_variable_left_intact`. | Substituting into `allowed-tools` changes which commands the skill may run; an error there is a permission change. Mitigation: the same renderer is used for both and both are tested. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S9-SKILL-010 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-10, §6 L26; area10-skills.md F10-10 | The skill loader **executes** shell substitutions of the form `` !`cmd` `` found inside a skill body, at render time, and does so silently. This is a code-execution surface created by merely loading a skill. | S9-SKILL-011, S6-HOOK-046 | `skills/exec.py :: execute_inline_commands(body, cwd) -> (rendered_body, exec_log)`; each executed command is recorded and reported. | `test_skill_exec.py::test_inline_command_executed_and_logged` and `::test_execution_requires_the_explicit_marker`. | This is the highest-risk row in S9: a repository-supplied skill executes code at load time with the operator's privileges. Mitigation: (a) the execution is a separate, explicit mechanism rather than an accident of templating; (b) the log is surfaced; (c) the `disableSkillShellExecution` setting (S9-SET-016) provides an off switch; (d) in the reference this is a documented limitation (L26), so the reimplementation should make the risk *visible* even where it matches the reference. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S9-SKILL-011 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-11, §6 L29; area10-skills.md F10-11 | `context: fork` runs a skill in an isolated subagent, and a forked skill is **not** counted in the spawned-subagent statistics, which the reference treats as a known inconsistency. | S10-AGENT-014 | `skills/fork.py :: run_forked(skill, ctx) -> ForkResult`; `agents/stats.py :: AgentStats` with the forked run excluded from `spawned`. | `test_skill_fork.py::test_fork_runs_in_isolated_context` and `::test_fork_not_counted_in_spawned`. | A mis-counted statistic misleads a monitoring dashboard. Mitigation: the exclusion is explicit and tested, and the reference's own inconsistency is documented rather than copied silently. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S9-SKILL-012 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-12, §6 L33; area10-skills.md F10-12 | Subagent definitions use a camelCase frontmatter schema, distinct from the hyphen-case skill schema, with a documented list of fields that are ignored; the two schemas differing in casing is a known reference inconsistency. | S10-AGENT-008 | `agents/frontmatter.py :: parse_agent(text) -> AgentDefinition` with camelCase keys and an `IGNORED_FIELDS` set. | `test_agent_frontmatter.py::test_camel_case_schema_accepted` and `::test_ignored_fields_ignored` and `::test_skill_casing_not_shared`. | A shared parser would mis-parse one of the two schemas. Mitigation: two separate parsers with separate tests, and the divergence is documented. | P0 parity-core / Phase 4 |
| S9-SKILL-013 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-13; area10-skills.md F10-13; **claude-code-evidence.md R12** | A subagent's `skills:` field preloads the skill's **body — not its frontmatter** — into the subagent's context. The earlier claim that the whole `SKILL.md` including frontmatter was preloaded was an artefact of the subagent reading the file with its Read tool and quoting it. | S9-SKILL-008, S10-AGENT-010 | `agents/runner.py :: preload_skills(skill_names) -> list[Attachment]` attaching the skill body **with the frontmatter stripped**, preceded by the base-directory line. | `test_agent_skills.py::test_preload_excludes_frontmatter` — assert the preloaded text contains the body and does not contain the `---` frontmatter block. | Preloaded instructions are untrusted content injected into a delegated context. Stripping the frontmatter is what keeps the loader's *metadata* out of the delegated prompt. Mitigation: the preloaded attachment is marked as instruction content and the subagent's report is scanned (S10-AGENT-004). | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S9-SKILL-014 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-14; area10-skills.md F10-14 | The delegation tool is named `Task` in the harness's own registry and `Agent` in the model's tool schema; both names refer to the same capability and the published schema uses the model's name. | S10-AGENT-005 | `tools/registry.py` with an internal name and a `model_name` on each tool spec; the init event's tool list uses the model name. | `test_tool_naming.py::test_task_and_agent_are_one_tool` — assert one spec with two names and that the published list uses the model name. | Two names for one tool invites duplicate registrations. Mitigation: a single spec with two name fields, asserted. | P0 parity-core / Phase 4 |
| S9-SKILL-015 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-15; area10-skills.md F10-15 | The agent registry the model sees is a structured, **incremental** attachment: new agents are appended to the existing registry attachment rather than replacing it. | S10-AGENT-008 | `agents/registry.py :: AgentRegistry` with `upsert(agent)` and `render()` producing an incremental attachment with a version marker. | `test_agent_registry.py::test_incremental_attachment_not_replaced` — assert the second attachment contains only the delta plus the version marker. | Replacing the registry each turn re-sends the whole catalogue and wastes context. Mitigation: the registry renders deltas and the test asserts it. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S9-INSTR-016 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-16, §4 Area 2 F2; area10-skills.md F10-16 | Project instructions are concatenated in a defined order, and `@path` imports become **separate entries** rather than being inlined into the referring file's text. | S2-INSTR-006 | `instructions/importer.py :: expand_imports` returning a list of entries, each with its own path and text; the referring file's own text is not mutated. | `test_imports.py::test_import_is_separate_entry`. | Inlining would make it impossible to tell which file said what, and would break the read-before-edit expectation for the imported file. Mitigation: entries stay separate. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S9-RULE-017 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-17; S2-RULES-007 | `.claude/rules/` provides a path-scoped instruction channel injected on file access, not at launch. | S2-RULES-007 | `instructions/rules.py :: rules_for(path)`; the file-access hook consults it. | `test_rules.py::test_injected_on_access_not_at_launch`. | A rule that applies to the wrong path is an instruction mis-delivery. Mitigation: the path glob is evaluated against the accessed path. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S9-SETTINGS-018 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-19; area10-skills.md F10-19 | `skillOverrides`, `disableSkillShellExecution`, `disableBundledSkills`, `disableWorkflows` and `pluginConfigs` form one settings surface, and `pluginConfigs` in project scope is silently ignored by the reference. | S9-SKILL-006, S9-SKILL-010, S2-SETTINGS-011 | `config/settings.py` with these keys; `plugins/registry.py` ignoring project-scope `pluginConfigs`. | `test_settings.py::test_plugin_configs_ignored_in_project_scope`. | Silently ignoring a project-scope setting is surprising; reproducing it exactly is a parity decision that must be visible. Mitigation: the behaviour is documented in this checklist and pinned by a test; the debug log records the ignored key. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S9-WORKFLOW-019 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-20; area10-skills.md F10-20 | Dynamic workflows are a sandboxed, deterministic script layer over subagents: a workflow is a script whose steps invoke agents, and the engine is deterministic for the same inputs. | S10-WORKFLOW-026, S9-SKILL-010 | `workflows/engine.py :: run(workflow, inputs) -> WorkflowRun` executing steps in a sandbox with a deterministic scheduler; `workflows/engine.py :: WorkflowRun.journal`. | `test_workflow.py::test_deterministic_for_same_inputs` and `::test_step_runs_in_sandbox`. | A workflow script executes logic of arbitrary complexity. Mitigation: the engine is sandboxed and the journal records every step for audit. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S9-OUTSTYLE-020 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-21; area10-skills.md F10-21 | Output styles are a named, switchable instruction bundle with an exact settings key, and the active style is reported in the init event's `output_style` field. | S1-CLI-006, S2-INSTR-003 | `styles/output.py :: OutputStyle` with `styles/output.py :: active_style(ctx) -> str`; `protocol/events.py :: InitEvent.output_style`. | `test_output_styles.py::test_active_style_published` and `::test_style_switches_bundle`. | An output style changes the instruction bundle, so an unnoticed switch changes the agent's behaviour. Mitigation: the active style is published in the init event and visible in the status surface. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S9-PLUGIN-021 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-22; area10-skills.md F10-22 | `claude plugin validate --json` is a real validator with a stable JSON envelope, and it validates plugin *directories* as well as manifests; it reports findings about directories without enforcing them. | S9-PLUGIN-026 | `plugins/validate.py :: validate(path) -> ValidationReport` with `ValidationReport(ok, findings=[Finding(rule, path, message, severity)])`; the CLI renders it as JSON. | `test_plugin_validate.py::test_envelope_stable` and `::test_directory_findings_reported_not_enforced`. | A validator that enforces would break plugin loading on a warning. Mitigation: validation is advisory and the severity field distinguishes it from an error. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S9-PLUGIN-022 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-23; area10-skills.md F10-23 | A skill folder that contains a `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` becomes a plugin whose id is `<name>@skills-dir` — a distinct identity form from an ordinary skill. | S9-PLUGIN-021 | `plugins/identity.py :: plugin_id(manifest, origin) -> str` producing the `name@origin` form. | `test_plugin_id.py::test_skill_dir_plugin_id_form`. | An id collision between a skill and a plugin would make one shadow the other. Mitigation: the id form is distinct and tested. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S9-AGENTS-023 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-24; area10-skills.md F10-24 | `claude agents` is a registry of **background sessions**, not of agent definitions, and `claude import` is unavailable in the reference build; the unavailable subcommand must fail clearly rather than silently. | S1-SESSION-023 | `cli/agents.py :: list_agent_sessions() -> list[SessionSummary]`; `cli/agents.py` raising `CommandUnavailable('import')` for the missing subcommand. | `test_agents_cli.py::test_lists_sessions_not_definitions` and `::test_import_reports_unavailable`. | A command that lists the wrong thing misleads the operator. Mitigation: the semantics are pinned by a test, and the unavailable subcommand exits non-zero with a message. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S9-LEGACY-024 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-26; area10-skills.md F10-26 | Legacy `.claude/commands/*.md` files still load, and they are counted **separately** from skills in the listing. | S9-SKILL-007 | `skills/legacy.py :: load_legacy_commands(root) -> list[LegacyCommand]`; the listing attributes them to a distinct source. | `test_legacy_commands.py::test_legacy_commands_load_and_are_counted_separately`. | Silently merging the two would break counting and precedence. Mitigation: a distinct type and a distinct counter. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S9-CMD-025 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I5; docs-cache/commands.md § "How the command menu matches what you type" | The command menu's **list** predicate and its **highlight** predicate are different. The list matches when the typed letters match a command name or alias from the start of the name or from the start of a word within it, ignoring the `:`, `_` and `-` separators; the highlight (which decides what `Enter` runs) is stricter — it fires only when the letters after the `/` match the name or an alias. | S9-CMD-026, S1-INPUT-026 | `commands/matching.py :: list_matches(typed, command) -> bool` and `:: highlight_matches(typed, command) -> bool`, plus `:: match_offsets(typed, command) -> list[int]` implementing the word-boundary rule with `:`, `_`, `-` as separators. | `test_command_matching.py::test_adddir_highlights_add_dir` and `::test_new_highlights_clear_via_alias` and `::test_list_is_broader_than_highlight`. | A `startsWith` implementation would break `/adddir` → `/add-dir` and `/new` → `/clear`, and would make `Enter` run the wrong command. Mitigation: two separate predicates, each tested, with the word-boundary rule implemented once. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S9-CMD-026 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I5; docs-cache/commands.md | After a typo, nothing is highlighted: the close matches stay listed and can be picked, but `Enter` submits the text as typed and reports `Unknown command`. A hidden command is never brought in by a partial name; it appears only once its full name is typed. | S9-CMD-025, S9-CMD-027 | `commands/matching.py :: highlight_matches` returning false for a non-matching prefix; `commands/registry.py :: is_hidden(name) -> bool`. | `test_command_matching.py::test_typo_leaves_nothing_highlighted` and `::test_hidden_command_requires_full_name`. | Highlighting a near-miss makes `Enter` run the wrong command. Mitigation: the highlight predicate is exactly the documented rule and is tested with the documented examples. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S9-CMD-027 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I5; agent-evidence.md §6 L24 | An unrecognised slash command is reported to the user as unknown, and a command whose name matches but which is unavailable for the current plan or platform is reported with its own availability message rather than as unknown; an unmatched slash command typed inside a message is sent to the model as ordinary text. | S9-CMD-025 | `commands/dispatch.py :: dispatch(name, ctx) -> CommandOutcome` with `CommandOutcome(kind=Literal['ran','unknown','unavailable','sent_to_model'])`. | `test_command_dispatch.py::test_unknown_reported` and `::test_unavailable_has_own_message` and `::test_inline_unmatched_sent_to_model`. | Silently sending an unknown command to the model wastes a turn and can be a prompt-injection vector. Mitigation: the four outcomes are distinguished and each is tested. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S9-CMD-028 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I5 | The command namespace is a merge of four sources — built-in commands, bundled and user-authored skills, plugin contributions and MCP prompts — and the display form is namespaced (`/myplugin:deploy-app`) while the match form is bare (`/deploy`). | S9-SKILL-001, S8-PROMPT-011, S9-PLUGIN-021 | `commands/registry.py :: CommandRegistry` merging four providers; `commands/matching.py` matching against the bare name while rendering the namespaced name. | `test_command_registry.py::test_four_sources_merged` and `::test_bare_match_namespaced_display`. | Namespace confusion causes the wrong command to run. Mitigation: the registry keeps the bare and namespaced names as separate fields and the test asserts both. | P0 parity-core / Phase 3 |
| S9-LOCAL-029 | agent-reference-shots/area-commands/probe-*.sanitized.jsonl | A local (client-side) command produces a result envelope with `num_turns: 0`, `duration_api_ms: 0` and `total_cost_usd: 0`, and reports the command's own local handling in a dedicated field — it does not consume a model turn. | S1-RESULT-011, S9-CMD-027 | `commands/dispatch.py :: run_local(command, ctx) -> CommandResult` with `CommandResult(local_command=..., turns=0, cost=0)`. | `test_local_command.py::test_local_command_costs_nothing` — assert the envelope's `num_turns == 0` and `total_cost_usd == 0` and that the local field names the handler. | A local command that silently consumed a model turn would be both slow and expensive. Mitigation: the local path is structurally separate and the cost fields are asserted. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S9-CMD-030 | agent-reference-shots/area-commands/probe-*.sanitized.jsonl; docs-cache/commands.md | The command menu lists only commands available to the current user; when nothing matches, the message is `No commands match "/name"`, and a few unavailable commands answer with their own availability message. | S9-CMD-026, S9-CMD-027 | `commands/menu.py :: render_no_match(typed) -> str` with the exact message; `commands/registry.py :: availability(name) -> Availability`. | `test_command_menu.py::test_no_match_message_exact` and `::test_unavailable_commands_excluded_from_menu`. | Listing an unavailable command invites a failed invocation. Mitigation: availability is checked before listing and the empty-state message is exact. | P1 extensibility / Phase 2 |


---

## S10 — Delegated work & subagent orchestration

**Priority band:** P0 parity-core.

**Evidence base.** `agent-evidence.md` §4 Area 3 F3-7 … F3-10, F3-13, F3-14 and §4 Area 6b (the parallel-agent area: file-backed job registry, worktree isolation, shared task list with file locks, agent-to-agent mailbox, scriptable fan-out); `agent-reference-shots/areas/area3-planning.md` F3-7 … F3-10, F3-13, F3-14; `agent-reference-shots/areas/area6-tests-errors.md` §"6.1 Findings" (the parallel-agent area, F1–F17) and §6.6 (the six clean-room layers); `agent-evidence.md` §6 L29 (forked skill not counted); `agent-reference-shots/area-commands/` probe captures.

**Scope note.** S10 owns the *fan-out* surfaces: the agent tool, agent definitions, the concurrency and nesting bounds, agent teams, dynamic workflows, worktree isolation, the mailbox, and the background job registry. The file-level details of the parallel-agent area (the file-backed stores and their lock protocol) are specified here because they are the coordination substrate.
| ID | Evidence | Acceptance criterion (testable) | Depends on | Proposed module + interface shape | Automated verification test | Security / operational risk + mitigation | Priority / Phase |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| S10-AGENT-001 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 3 F3-7; area3-planning.md F3-7 | The agent tool spawns a subagent in a **separate context window** with an enforced tool allowlist; the allowlist is applied by the dispatcher, not merely stated in the prompt. | S10-AGENT-008, S1-TOOLSET-022 | `agents/tool.py :: AgentTool` with `spawn(spec: AgentSpec) -> AgentHandle`; `agents/dispatch.py :: scoped_dispatch(handle, request)` filtering by the spec's tool allowlist. | `test_agent_tool.py::test_subagent_has_separate_context` and `::test_allowlist_is_enforced_at_dispatch` — attempt a tool outside the allowlist and assert refusal. | An advisory allowlist is defeated by a mis-routed call. Mitigation: enforcement at the dispatch boundary, asserted by a test that bypasses the prompt. | P0 parity-core / Phase 4 |
| S10-AGENT-002 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 3 F3-7, F3-10 | The agent tool accepts a mode selection: a fresh-context subagent, or a **fork** that inherits the parent conversation. Fork mode is the default. | S10-AGENT-006, S10-AGENT-007 | `agents/tool.py :: spawn(spec)` with `spec.mode: Literal['fresh','fork']`; `agents/fork.py :: fork(parent_context) -> Context`. | `test_agent_tool.py::test_fork_inherits_parent_context` and `::test_fresh_context_does_not`. | A fork that silently does not inherit wastes a delegation. Mitigation: the mode is explicit and its effect is tested both ways. | P0 parity-core / Phase 4 |
