| S10-AGENT-003 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 3 F3-10, F3-13 | A forked subagent inherits the parent conversation *and* the parent's tool access, so the fork can act with the parent's authority; a forked run therefore requires the same permission checks as the parent. | S10-AGENT-002, S6-RULE-010 | `agents/fork.py :: fork(parent_context)` copying the tool allowlist and the permission context; the forked dispatch path reuses the parent's permission engine. | `test_agent_tool.py::test_fork_requires_permission_checks` — attempt a gated action in a fork and assert it is gated. | A fork with more authority than the parent is a privilege-escalation path. Mitigation: the fork inherits the parent's permission context and the test asserts gating. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S10-AGENT-004 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 3 F3-13, §4 Area 6 F19 | A delegated agent's result returns to the parent as a **summary**, not as the raw transcript, and the summary is framed as model output that carries no user authority. | S4-EXEC-017, S7-AGENT-021 | `agents/result.py :: summarise(run) -> Summary`; the parent-side insertion wraps the summary in a framing that marks it as delegated output. | `test_agent_result.py::test_result_is_a_summary` and `::test_framing_marks_non_authority` — assert the inserted text contains the non-authority marker. | The framing is a security boundary that must be in the transcript the model reads, not in documentation. Mitigation: the framing is inserted by the harness. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S10-AGENT-005 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-14; S9-SKILL-014 | The delegation capability is registered once; the harness name and the model-facing name differ but refer to one tool. | S9-SKILL-014 | `tools/registry.py` with one spec carrying both names. | `test_tool_naming.py::test_single_registration` — assert exactly one delegation tool spec exists. | Two registrations would double-count spawns. Mitigation: one spec, asserted. | P0 parity-core / Phase 4 |
| S10-AGENT-006 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 3 F3-8, §4 Area 10 F10-12; area3-planning.md F3-8 | The built-in agent types are a fixed, documented set, and the read-only ones (`Explore`, `Plan`) are read-only and one-shot — they cannot continue into a second turn. | S4-EXEC-018, S9-SKILL-012 | `agents/types.py :: BUILTIN_AGENT_TYPES` with `AgentTypeSpec(name, tools, read_only, one_shot)`. | `test_agent_types.py::test_readonly_types_never_mutate` and `::test_one_shot_cannot_continue`. | A read-only type that can mutate would breach its contract. Mitigation: the tool filter is derived from the spec, not from a prompt. | P0 parity-core / Phase 4 |
| S10-AGENT-007 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 3 F3-9, F3-10 | A subagent's own context is separate: it does not see the parent's conversation unless it is a fork, and it returns only its result. | S10-AGENT-002 | `agents/context.py :: SubagentContext` with a distinct message list; `agents/runner.py` constructing it from the spec. | `test_agent_context.py::test_fresh_subagent_cannot_see_parent_history` — assert the subagent's first prompt contains no parent turns. | Context bleed between agents can leak the user's content into a lower-privilege agent. Mitigation: the context objects are separate instances with no shared list. | P0 parity-core / Phase 4 |
| S10-AGENT-008 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-12, F10-15; S9-SKILL-012 | User-authored agent definitions are parsed from a frontmatter schema and registered; the registry the model sees is incremental. | S9-SKILL-012, S9-SKILL-015 | `agents/registry.py :: AgentRegistry` with `load_from(dirs)` and `upsert()`; `agents/registry.py :: render_incremental()`. | `test_agent_registry.py::test_definitions_registered` and `::test_incremental_render`. | An agent definition is executable configuration. Mitigation: definitions are parsed strictly, and a malformed definition is rejected rather than partially applied. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-AGENT-009 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-13; S9-SKILL-013; **claude-code-evidence.md R12** | A subagent may be given a set of skills to preload; the preloaded content is the skill's **body**, with the frontmatter **stripped** — not the entire `SKILL.md`. | S9-SKILL-013 | `agents/runner.py :: preload_skills(names) -> list[Attachment]`, frontmatter stripped. | `test_agent_skills.py::test_preload_excludes_frontmatter`. | Preloaded instructions are untrusted content injected into a delegated context. Mitigation: the preloaded attachment is marked as instruction content and the subagent's report is scanned (S10-AGENT-004). | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-AGENT-010 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F19; area6-tests-errors.md F19 | A delegated run reports its per-run usage and duration to the parent, so the parent can decide whether to continue the agent or restart it. | S10-AGENT-004 | `agents/runner.py :: AgentRun` with `usage`, `duration_ms`, `agent_id`; the continuation handle is exposed in the hand-back block. | `test_agent_result.py::test_usage_and_duration_reported` and `::test_continuation_handle_present`. | Without usage data the parent cannot budget a long delegation. Mitigation: usage is always reported with the result. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-AGENT-011 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 3 F3-13; area3-planning.md F3-13 | A delegated agent can be continued after its report, addressed by its identifier with a short summary, and the continuation preserves the agent's context. | S10-AGENT-010 | `agents/mailbox.py :: send(agent_id, summary, message) -> None`; `agents/runner.py :: continue_agent(agent_id)` restoring the agent's context. | `test_agent_continue.py::test_continuation_preserves_context` — assert the continued agent's prompt includes the prior exchange. | A continuation that resets the context forces redundant work and re-execution of side effects. Mitigation: the agent's context is retained by identifier. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-AGENT-012 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 3 F3-14, F3-15; area6-tests-errors.md §6.1 (parallel-agent area) | Beyond one-shot delegation there are several distinct orchestration surfaces — an agent **view** (a panel listing running agents), **agent teams** (a set of named agents sharing a task list and a mailbox), **dynamic workflows** (a script layer), and **projects** (a long-lived multi-session grouping) — each with its own state store. | S10-TEAM-024, S10-WORKFLOW-026, S10-PROJECT-030 | `agents/views.py :: AgentView`; `teams/team.py :: Team`; `workflows/engine.py`; `projects/project.py :: Project`; each with its own state root under the config directory. | `test_orchestration.py::test_four_surfaces_have_distinct_state_roots` — assert the state paths differ and that no surface reads another's files. | Sharing a state store between surfaces couples their lifecycles and their trust models. Mitigation: distinct state roots, asserted. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-LIMIT-014 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 3 F3-9; S4-EXEC-019 | Concurrency and nesting are bounded with documented failure signals for each bound (depth, concurrency, budget). | S4-EXEC-019 | `agents/limits.py :: AgentLimits(max_concurrent, max_depth, budget_usd)`; `agents/manager.py` raising `AgentLimitExceeded(kind)`. | `test_agent_limits.py::test_each_bound_signalled_distinctly` — table-driven over the three kinds. | Unbounded fan-out exhausts budget and rate limits. Mitigation: three named bounds with distinct signals. | P0 parity-core / Phase 4 |
| S10-STATS-015 | agent-reference-shots/print-mode-json-result.json (`subagent_stats`) | Subagent statistics are reported on the result envelope with the documented shape: `spawned`, `requested{background, foreground, unset}`, `started_in_background`, `max_depth`, `spawned_by_subagents`, `completed`, `failed`, `killed{parent, user, system}`, `refused{depth_limit, concurrency_limit, budget}`, `by_type`. | S1-RESULT-011, S10-AGENT-014 | `agents/stats.py :: AgentStats` with the exact field set; `protocol/envelope.py :: ResultEnvelope.subagent_stats`. | `test_subagent_stats.py::test_field_set_matches_contract` and `::test_refused_buckets_populated` — assert each refusal reason increments its bucket. | Statistics drive the concurrency decisions; an inaccurate count causes a runaway. Mitigation: the counters are incremented at the point of the decision, and the field set is pinned. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-STATS-016 | agent-evidence.md §6 L29; S9-SKILL-011 | A skill run with `context: fork` is not counted as a spawned subagent in the statistics — a reference inconsistency that is reproduced deliberately and documented. | S9-SKILL-011, S10-STATS-015 | `skills/fork.py` marking the run as `not_counted`; `agents/stats.py :: spawned` excluding it. | `test_subagent_stats.py::test_forked_skill_not_counted` — assert `spawned` is unchanged by a forked skill run. | A divergence in either direction is a parity question. Mitigation: the behaviour is explicit, tested, and flagged in this checklist. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S10-JOB-017 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6b (parallel-agent area, F2); area6-tests-errors.md §6.1 | Background work is tracked in a file-backed job registry: a job has a state file and a timeline file under a jobs directory, and the job's state is machine-readable JSON with a stable listing command. | S5-BG-016, S1-SESSION-023 | `jobs/registry.py :: JobRegistry` rooted at `jobs_root()` with `state.json` and `timeline.jsonl` per job; `jobs/registry.py :: list_jobs() -> list[JobState]`. | `test_job_registry.py::test_state_and_timeline_files_created` and `::test_listing_is_stable_json`. | A job directory in a shared location could be pre-created by another user. Mitigation: the registry root is created with restrictive permissions and validated before use. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-JOB-018 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6b (parallel-agent area, F13); area12-dependencies.md F13 | Background agents require a local supervisor daemon, not just the CLI: the daemon owns the roster with a protocol version, and a client connects over a local socket. | S1-SESSION-023, S1-CLI-006 | `runtime/daemon.py :: Supervisor` with `roster.json` carrying a `proto` version; `runtime/socket.py :: connect(path) -> Client`. | `test_daemon.py::test_roster_has_proto_version` and `::test_client_rejects_unknown_proto`. | A local socket is an access-control surface. Mitigation: the socket path is created with restrictive permissions and the protocol version gates compatibility. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-WORKTREE-020 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6b (parallel-agent area, F3); docs-cache/worktrees.md § "How Claude Code enforces isolation" | Worktree-based edit isolation enforces **four** non-disableable checks: (1) file edits into the main checkout, (2) a command whose working directory resolves into the main checkout, (3) git redirects (`git -C`, `--git-dir`, `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `cd`), and (4) command shapes whose target cannot be verified (computed command names, `${!name}`, `${ command; }`). | S5-CMD-012, S6-CRIT-021 | `worktree/guard.py :: WorktreeGuard` with `check_file_edit(path)`, `check_command_cwd(command, cwd)`, `check_git_redirect(command)`, `check_command_shape(command)`; each raising `IsolationViolation(kind)`. | `test_worktree_isolation.py::test_four_checks_enforced` — table-driven over one violating command per check. | These are the checks that make parallel edits safe. Mitigation: each check is a separate function, none of them is user-disableable, and all four are tested. | P0 parity-core / Phase 4 |
| S10-WORKTREE-021 | docs-cache/worktrees.md § "How Claude Code enforces isolation" | On Windows, the PowerShell tool receives **only** the working-directory check — the other three checks do not apply to it, and this asymmetry is a documented, deliberate limitation. | S10-WORKTREE-020, S5-PS-019 | `worktree/guard.py :: checks_for(tool) -> list[Check]` with the PowerShell-specific set. | `test_worktree_isolation.py::test_powershell_gets_only_cwd_check`. | An asymmetry that is not documented is a hidden hole. Mitigation: the check set is a per-tool function and the asymmetry is asserted. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S10-WORKTREE-022 | docs-cache/worktrees.md (sweep rules, `git worktree lock` behaviour, `worktree.baseRef`) | Worktrees are swept on a configured period, a worktree can be locked, and the base reference for a new worktree is configurable. | S10-WORKTREE-020 | `worktree/manager.py :: WorktreeManager` with `create(base_ref)`, `lock(path)`, `sweep(period_days)`; `config/settings.py :: worktree.baseRef` and `cleanupPeriodDays`. | `test_worktree.py::test_sweep_removes_stale` and `::test_locked_worktree_not_swept` and `::test_base_ref_configurable`. | A sweep that removes a locked worktree destroys work in progress. Mitigation: the lock is checked before removal, and the test asserts it. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-WORKTREE-023 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6b (parallel-agent area, F3) | The worktree lifecycle's failure behaviour — what happens when a worktree cannot be created or a branch is already checked out — is not fully characterised in the evidence, so the reimplementation must define it explicitly. | S10-WORKTREE-020 | `worktree/manager.py :: create(base_ref) -> Worktree` raising `WorktreeCreationError(reason)` with a distinct reason per failure mode. | `test_worktree.py::test_creation_failures_have_distinct_reasons`. | An undefined failure path usually degrades into a partial worktree and a confused agent. Mitigation: the failure reasons are enumerated and each is tested. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-TASK-024 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6b (parallel-agent area, F4); area6-tests-errors.md §6.6.4 | A shared task list used by a team is file-backed with a dependency graph and **file-lock claiming**: a task is claimed by taking a lock, and a lock conflict is reported rather than silently overwritten. | S4-TASK-005, S10-TEAM-026 | `teams/tasklist.py :: SharedTaskList` rooted at `tasks_root() / team` with per-task `n.json` files and a `.lock`; `teams/tasklist.py :: claim(task_id, owner) -> ClaimResult`. | `test_team_tasklist.py::test_claim_excludes_claimed_task` and `::test_lock_conflict_reported`. | Two agents claiming the same task duplicate work or corrupt state. Mitigation: the lock is an atomic file operation and conflicts are reported. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-MAIL-026 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6b (parallel-agent area, F5); area6-tests-errors.md §6.6.5 | An agent-to-agent mailbox is file-backed with a three-state delivery protocol: undelivered, delivered and acknowledged; a message that is never acknowledged remains visible. | S10-AGENT-011 | `teams/mailbox.py :: Mailbox` rooted at `teams_root() / team / inboxes / <agent>.json`; `teams/mailbox.py :: send(to, message)`, `:: inbox(agent) -> list[Message]`, `:: acknowledge(message_id)` with `DeliveryState` enum. | `test_mailbox.py::test_three_delivery_states` and `::test_unacknowledged_message_remains_visible`. | A lost message means a delegation silently does nothing. Mitigation: the state machine is explicit and the unacknowledged state is visible. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-FANOUT-027 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6b (parallel-agent area, F6); area6-tests-errors.md §6.6.6 | Scriptable fan-out over a set of targets has a deterministic replay rule: the same script and the same inputs produce the same sequence of delegations, and a partial run can be replayed without redoing completed steps. | S10-JOB-017, S10-WORKFLOW-019 | `agents/fanout.py :: run(script, targets) -> FanoutRun` with a deterministic ordering of targets and a per-target completion record used to skip already-finished work on replay. | `test_fanout.py::test_same_inputs_same_order` and `::test_replay_skips_completed`. | A non-deterministic fan-out makes a partial failure irreproducible. Mitigation: target ordering is a stable sort on a stable key and progress is recorded per target. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-MONITOR-028 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6b (parallel-agent area, F7); area6-tests-errors.md §6.6.7 | Human-facing 'watch the workers' surfaces exist: a panel listing running agents and their status, and a view of the shared task list, both readable without a model turn. | S12-UX-012, S10-TASK-024 | `ui/agentspanel.py :: AgentsPanel` rendering `JobState` records; `ui/tasklist.py` (S4-TASK-007) rendering the shared list. | `test_agents_panel.py::test_panel_lists_running_agents` and `::test_panel_reads_no_model`. | The panel reads shared files that another process may be writing. Mitigation: reads tolerate a partially-written file and re-read rather than error. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-TEAM-029 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6b (parallel-agent area, F4, F5); agent-evidence.md §4 Area 3 F3-14 | An agent team is a named set of agents that share a task list and a mailbox, and the team has its own durable state directory. | S10-TASK-024, S10-MAIL-026 | `teams/team.py :: Team(name, members)` with `state_root` under the teams directory; `teams/team.py :: create(name, members)`. | `test_team.py::test_team_state_root_created` and `::test_members_share_tasklist`. | A shared team directory is a collaboration surface with a wider blast radius. Mitigation: the team root is created with restrictive permissions and validated before use. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-TEAM-030 | ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION (the reference's team lifecycle and shutdown semantics were not directly characterised in agent-evidence.md; only the shared task list, the mailbox and the file-backed state layout were observed) | A team shuts down deterministically: members are asked to finish, then stopped, then the team state is marked closed; a member that does not stop within the grace period is recorded as abandoned rather than blocking the shutdown. | S10-TEAM-029, S5-BG-018 | `teams/team.py :: shutdown(grace_s) -> ShutdownReport` with `ShutdownReport(stopped, abandoned)`. | `test_team.py::test_shutdown_records_abandoned_member` and `::test_shutdown_completes_within_grace`. | A shutdown that hangs leaves a team writing into a shared task list. Mitigation: a bounded grace period with an explicit abandoned record. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S10-PROJECT-031 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6b (parallel-agent area, F5, F7) and the 'projects' parallelism mechanism | A project is a long-lived grouping of sessions with its own durable state, distinct from a team's shared-task model and from a background session. | S10-TEAM-029, S1-SESSION-023 | `projects/project.py :: Project(name, state_root)` with `add_session(session_id)` and `sessions() -> list[SessionSummary]`. | `test_project.py::test_project_sessions_listed` and `::test_project_state_distinct_from_team_state`. | Grouping sessions into a project widens what a later session can see. Mitigation: the project state is explicit about which sessions it references and nothing else. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-WORKFLOW-032 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 10 F10-20; S9-WORKFLOW-019 | A dynamic workflow is a sandboxed, deterministic script over subagents whose journal records each step, so a run can be inspected and resumed. | S9-WORKFLOW-019, S4-PLAN-013 | `workflows/engine.py :: WorkflowRun` with `journal: list[StepRecord]` persisted; `workflows/engine.py :: resume(run_id)`. | `test_workflow.py::test_journal_records_each_step` and `::test_run_is_resumable`. | A workflow can perform many actions; the journal is the audit trail. Mitigation: every step is journalled before and after execution. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-AGENT-033 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 3 F3-14 (the `/goal` continuation surface); area3-planning.md F3-14 | A goal-driven continuation is a distinct surface from one-shot delegation: it lets a long-running objective continue across turns with a stated completion criterion. | S10-AGENT-002, S4-LOOP-010 | `agents/goal.py :: GoalRunner` with `Goal(objective, done_predicate)` and `step()` advancing one turn. | `test_goal.py::test_goal_continues_across_turns` and `::test_completion_criterion_stops_the_loop`. | An unbounded goal loop can burn budget indefinitely. Mitigation: the goal carries a bound from the same limits mechanism as delegation. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |
| S10-DEP-034 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 12 F6; area12-dependencies.md F6 | A subagent may run on a **different model** from its parent session, and the model is chosen by the same resolution chain as the session's own model. | S13-MODEL-001 | `agents/runner.py :: resolve_model(spec, ctx) -> str` delegating to the session's model resolver with the spec's override. | `test_agent_model.py::test_override_model_used` and `::test_inherits_parent_when_unspecified`. | A model override can select a model that lacks a capability the subagent needs. Mitigation: the capability matrix is applied to the delegated model as well. | P1 extensibility / Phase 4 |


---

## S11 — Context management, persistent sessions, resumption

**Priority band:** P0 parity-core.

**Evidence base.** `agent-evidence.md` §4 Area 1 F4, F5, F11, F12, F13, F16, F18 and §4 X2 (settings); `agent-reference-shots/areas/area7-model-context-sessions.md` (F1–F23 per-finding detail); `agent-evidence.md` §6 L11/L16 (compaction pause; unfinished turns); `agent-reference-shots/area-commands/probe-*.sanitized.jsonl` (the observed `/context` token-accounting output); `agent-reference-shots/print-mode-json-result.json` (`usage`, `modelUsage`, `contextWindow`, `maxOutputTokens`); `docs-cache/context-window.md` and `docs-cache/costs.md`.

**Scope note.** S11 owns the *budget*: what is in the model's context, how the budget is measured and reported, when it is compacted, and how a session persists and resumes.
| ID | Evidence | Acceptance criterion (testable) | Depends on | Proposed module + interface shape | Automated verification test | Security / operational risk + mitigation | Priority / Phase |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| S11-CONTEXT-001 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 7 (context-window area); area7-model-context-sessions.md F1 | The context window has a known size per model (200,000 tokens in the observed envelope) and a known maximum output size (32,000 tokens); both are published per model in the usage accounting. | S13-MODEL-001, S1-RESULT-011 | `context/window.py :: ContextWindow(total_tokens, max_output_tokens)`; `protocol/envelope.py :: modelUsage[<model>].contextWindow` and `.maxOutputTokens`. | `test_context_window.py::test_published_sizes_match_model` — assert the envelope's `contextWindow` and `maxOutputTokens` for the configured model. | A wrong window size mis-sizes the compaction trigger. Mitigation: the size comes from the model registry, not from a constant in the loop. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S11-CONTEXT-002 | agent-reference-shots/area-commands/probe-*.sanitized.jsonl (observed `/context` output) | The context report breaks the budget down into named segments with token counts and percentages: `System prompt`, `System tools`, `System tools (deferred)`, `Skills`, `Free space`, `Autocompact buffer` — and a per-skill token table with a `Source` (User / Built-in) and a `Tokens` column. | S2-SELF-016, S9-SKILL-007 | `context/report.py :: build_report(ctx) -> ContextReport` with `ContextReport(segments=[Segment(name, tokens, percent)], skills=[SkillUsage(name, source, tokens)])`. | `test_context_report.py::test_segment_set_present` and `::test_per_skill_table_with_source`. | The report is the operator's only view of where the budget goes. Mitigation: the segment list is fixed and asserted so a new segment cannot be added silently. | P1 extensibility / Phase 2 |
| S11-CONTEXT-003 | agent-reference-shots/area-commands/probe-*.sanitized.jsonl | The context report shows a `Free space` segment equal to the remaining budget and an `Autocompact buffer` segment equal to the reserved compaction allowance, and the two are derived from the window size rather than hard-coded. | S11-CONTEXT-001, S11-COMPACT-004 | `context/report.py :: free_space(window, used) -> int` and `:: autocompact_buffer(window) -> int`. | `test_context_report.py::test_free_space_is_window_minus_used` and `::test_buffer_derived_from_window`. | A hard-coded buffer would be wrong for a different window size. Mitigation: both are computed from the model registry's window. | P1 extensibility / Phase 2 |
| S11-CONTEXT-004 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 7; agent-reference-shots/print-mode-json-result.json (`usage`) | Token usage is accounted per model with input, output, cache-read, cache-creation, web-search request and thinking-token counts, and the totals are reported on the envelope's `usage` and `modelUsage` structures. | S11-CONTEXT-001 | `context/usage.py :: Usage` with the documented sub-counts; `context/usage.py :: accumulate(delta) -> Usage`; `protocol/envelope.py` populating `usage` and `modelUsage`. | `test_usage.py::test_counts_accumulate_per_model` and `::test_cache_read_and_creation_tracked_separately`. | Cost attribution errors lead to wrong pricing decisions. Mitigation: the accumulator is the single source for both the envelope and the statusline. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S11-COMPACT-005 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 7, §6 L11 | Compaction emits a `compact_boundary` event carrying `preTokens`, `postTokens`, a monotonic `cumulativeDroppedTokens`, `durationMs`, `preCompactDiscoveredTools`, the preserved segment (`headUuid`, `anchorUuid`, `tailUuid`), the preserved message ids (`preservedMessages.uuids`) and a `logicalParentUuid`. | S7-COMPACT-024, S11-TRANSCRIPT-008 | `protocol/events.py :: CompactBoundaryEvent` with the exact field set; `context/compaction.py :: compact(context) -> CompactionResult` producing it. | `test_compaction.py::test_compact_boundary_field_set` and `::test_cumulative_dropped_is_monotonic`. | The boundary event is the only reliable way to reason about what the model can still see. Mitigation: the field set is pinned and the monotonic counter is asserted. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S11-COMPACT-006 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 7, §6 L11 | Compaction preserves a segment of the conversation (a head, an anchor and a tail) rather than keeping everything, and the preserved message ids are enumerated in the boundary event so a client can reconstruct what survived. | S11-COMPACT-005 | `context/compaction.py :: select_preserved(messages, budget) -> PreservedSegment`; `PreservedSegment(head_uuid, anchor_uuid, tail_uuid, message_uuids)`. | `test_compaction.py::test_preserved_segment_shape` and `::test_preserved_ids_match_retained_messages`. | Preserving the wrong slice loses the task's context. Mitigation: the preserved set is computed from the budget and cross-checked against the retained messages by a test. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S11-COMPACT-007 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 7 | Tools discovered before a compaction are recorded in the boundary event, so a tool that was loaded via a deferred search is not silently forgotten after compaction. | S8-DEFER-012, S11-COMPACT-005 | `context/compaction.py :: discovered_tools(ctx) -> list[str]` captured before compaction and reported in the event. | `test_compaction.py::test_pre_compact_discovered_tools_reported`. | A forgotten tool forces a re-discovery turn. Mitigation: the discovered-tool set is captured and reported. | P1 extensibility / Phase 2 |
| S11-COMPACT-008 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 7 | The compaction heuristics — what is summarised, what is dropped, and what the anchor selection favours — are not fully characterised in the evidence; the reimplementation must define them explicitly and version them. | S11-COMPACT-006 | `context/compaction.py :: CompactionPolicy(version, summarise_fn, anchor_selector)`; the policy version is recorded in the boundary event. | `test_compaction.py::test_policy_version_recorded` — assert the boundary event carries the policy version, and that two versions produce different retained sets under a fixture. | An unversioned heuristic makes a compaction difference impossible to explain after the fact. Mitigation: the policy is versioned and the version is emitted. | P1 extensibility / Phase 2 |
| S11-COMPACT-009 | agent-evidence.md §6 L16 | A turn that was abandoned before completion leaves the session in an explicitly unfinished state, and compaction does not convert an unfinished turn into a completed one. | S6-CANCEL-056, S11-COMPACT-005 | `context/compaction.py` preserving the unfinished-turn marker; `transcript/entries.py :: TurnStatus` with `UNFINISHED`. | `test_compaction.py::test_unfinished_turn_survives_compaction`. | Losing the unfinished marker makes a resumed session believe the work was done. Mitigation: the status is stored in the transcript and preserved. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S11-AUTOCOMPACT-010 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 7 and §4 Area 11 (model config); area-model-config.md §2.8 | Automatic compaction has a configurable trigger window; an invalid `--autocompact` value is a **hard error** (not a warning-and-ignore, unlike an invalid effort level). | S11-COMPACT-005, S13-EFFORT-016 | `config/settings.py :: autocompact_window` validated by a strict parser raising `ConfigError`; `context/compaction.py :: should_autocompact(used, window) -> bool`. | `test_autocompact.py::test_invalid_value_is_hard_error` and `::test_trigger_honours_window`. | Silently ignoring an invalid threshold changes when the model loses context. Mitigation: strict validation with a non-zero exit. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S11-CACHE-011 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F19, §4 Area 7; area7-model-context-sessions.md | Prompt caching is a first-class concern: the context is assembled so that a stable prefix is cacheable, and the cache-read and cache-creation token counts are reported separately from plain input tokens. | S2-ENV-016A, S11-CONTEXT-004 | `context/cacheplan.py :: plan(ctx) -> CachePlan`; `context/usage.py :: Usage.cache_read_input_tokens` and `.cache_creation_input_tokens`. | `test_cache.py::test_stable_prefix_precedes_volatile_content` and `::test_cache_counts_reported_separately`. | A cache-breaking prefix multiplies the cost of every turn. Mitigation: the cache plan is computed once per session and asserted stable. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S11-THRESHOLD-012 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 7 | A `contextWindow` of 200,000 is assumed for a model id the product does not recognise, and the assumption is stated in a diagnostic rather than applied silently. | S13-MODEL-030, S11-CONTEXT-001 | `context/window.py :: window_for(model_id) -> ContextWindow` falling back to 200,000 with a diagnostic on stderr. | `test_context_window.py::test_unknown_model_assumes_200k_and_warns`. | A wrong assumption mis-sizes the compaction trigger and the cost estimate. Mitigation: the fallback is a named constant and the warning is emitted. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S11-TRANSCRIPT-013 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F4; S1-SESSION-016 | The transcript is append-only JSONL under the config root, and every entry is one of the documented typed kinds (`mode`, `permission-mode`, `last-prompt`, `ai-title`, `cost-state`, `queue-operation`, `attachment`, `user`, `assistant`, `system`). | S1-SESSION-016 | `transcript/entries.py :: EntryType` enum with the documented members; `transcript/writer.py` writing one JSON object per line. | `test_transcript.py::test_entry_types_closed_set` and `::test_one_entry_per_line`. | An open entry set makes a transcript parser fragile. Mitigation: the type set is a closed enum and is asserted. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S11-TRANSCRIPT-014 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F5 | The transcript is a **message tree**, not a list: entries carry a parent reference and a `logicalParentUuid`, so a fork produces a branch rather than a linear sequence. | S1-SESSION-024 | `transcript/entries.py :: MessageEntry(parent_uuid, logical_parent_uuid)`; `transcript/tree.py :: build_tree(entries) -> Node`. | `test_transcript.py::test_tree_structure_reconstructed` — assert a fork yields two branches with a shared ancestor. | A linear reconstruction loses a branch and can make history look like a single conversation. Mitigation: the tree is rebuilt from the recorded parent links and tested. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S11-TRANSCRIPT-015 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F4, §4 Area 2 F18 | A file is re-read fresh on every read call, and the transcript records the read so a later edit can detect staleness; the read-before-edit precondition is evaluated against the tracker, not the transcript text. | S3-EDIT-016, S2-ENV-024 | `files/readtracker.py` (see S3) as the authority; `transcript/entries.py` recording read events for audit only. | `test_readtracker.py::test_transcript_is_not_the_read_authority` — assert a transcript entry alone does not satisfy the precondition. | Deriving the precondition from prose in the transcript is fragile. Mitigation: a dedicated tracker is the authority. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S11-RESUME-016 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F11, §4 Area 6b; area7-model-context-sessions.md | A session can be resumed by id, continued by taking the most recent session for the project, and both restore the context from the transcript rather than from an in-memory snapshot. | S1-SESSION-024, S11-TRANSCRIPT-013 | `session/history.py :: restore(session_id) -> SessionContext` reading the transcript; `session/history.py :: latest_for(project_key) -> Optional[SessionSummary]`. | `test_resume.py::test_restore_from_transcript` — delete any in-memory state and assert the restored context matches the recorded conversation. | Restoring from a snapshot would drift from the transcript. Mitigation: the transcript is the single source of truth for restoration. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S11-RESUME-017 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F11, §7/§11 (the `--system-prompt-snapshot` resume interaction is an open question) | Whether a resumed session reuses the original system-prompt snapshot or rebuilds it is not fully characterised in the evidence; the reimplementation must define the behaviour and record which was used. | S1-SESSION-025, S11-RESUME-016 | `session/history.py :: restore(...)` recording `prompt_snapshot: Literal['reused','rebuilt']` on the restored context; `runtime/session.py` writing it to the transcript. | `test_resume.py::test_snapshot_provenance_recorded`. | A silent rebuild changes the model's instructions between sessions. Mitigation: the provenance is recorded explicitly and is visible in the transcript. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S11-SESSION-018 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F3, F12; area7-model-context-sessions.md | The session has two distinct handles — the session id and the project key — and the session id is a UUIDv4 that a caller can pin via `--session-id` so that an external system can address the session. | S1-SESSION-014 | `session/identity.py :: SessionIdentity(session_id, project_key)`; `session/identity.py :: from_cwd_and_id(cwd, pinned_id)`. | `test_identity.py::test_two_handles_present_and_distinct`. | A single handle conflates 'which project' with 'which conversation'. Mitigation: the two fields are separate and asserted. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S11-SESSION-019 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F12, §4 Area 6b; area6-tests-errors.md §6.1 | A background session is addressable, its state is inspectable, and it can be messaged while no terminal is attached; the session's transcript continues to be written while backgrounded. | S1-SESSION-023, S10-JOB-017 | `session/background.py :: BackgroundSession` with `status()`, `send()`, `transcript_path`; the writer continues to append while detached. | `test_background.py::test_transcript_continues_while_detached`. | A background session that stops writing loses the record of work done. Mitigation: the writer is owned by the session, not by the terminal. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S11-SESSION-020 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F9, §4 Area 6 F14; area7-model-context-sessions.md | A session interrupted mid-turn can be resumed and continued, and the recovery step is explicit (`continue`) rather than automatic. | S6-RECOVER-044, S6-RECOVER-055 | `session/history.py :: resume_and_continue(session_id)`; `runtime/loop.py` treating the `continue` message as a new turn against the restored context. | `test_resume.py::test_continue_after_mid_response_failure`. | Automatic continuation would risk re-executing a tool. Mitigation: continuation is always user-initiated. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S11-SESSION-021 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F16, §4 Area 3 F10-25; area7-model-context-sessions.md | Auto memory persists across sessions for the same repository, is shared across worktrees, and is bounded by an index cap, so the model can recall earlier notes without the notes consuming the context budget. | S2-MEM-010, S2-INSTR-021 | `memory/store.py` (see S2) with the index cap; `memory/store.py :: project_scope(repo_id)`. | `test_memory.py::test_memory_survives_session_restart`. | Memory is a persistent channel between sessions. Mitigation: it is repository-scoped and bounded, and reads are auditable. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S11-CONTEXT-022 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 2 F17, §4 Area 7; area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I18 | The context report and the session recap are two distinct surfaces: the context report is an explicit command with token arithmetic, and the recap is a background-generated one-liner capped at 400 characters. | S11-CONTEXT-002, S12-UX-022 | `context/report.py :: build_report(ctx)`; `sessions/recap.py :: generate(ctx) -> str` with `MAX_RECAP_CHARS = 400`. | `test_recap.py::test_recap_capped_at_400` and `::test_context_report_not_capped`. | Conflating a one-line recap with the token report would hide the arithmetic the operator needs. Mitigation: separate surfaces with separate caps. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S11-BUDGET-023 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F10, §4 Area 6b; area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H10 | A session can be given a maximum turn count and a maximum budget; the two produce **distinct** termination subtypes on the envelope, so an automation can tell a turn-limit stop from a budget-limit stop. | S4-LOOP-011, S1-RESULT-011 | `runtime/limits.py :: Limits(max_turns, max_budget_usd)`; `protocol/envelope.py :: ResultEnvelope.terminal_reason` with the distinct values. | `test_limits.py::test_distinct_terminal_reasons` — assert the envelope's `terminal_reason` differs for the two limits. | Conflating the two hides the cause of an early stop. Mitigation: distinct reasons, asserted. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S11-COST-024 | agent-reference-shots/print-mode-json-result.json (`total_cost_usd`, `modelUsage[*].costUSD`, `costBasis`) | Cost accounting is client-side and per model: the envelope reports a total cost and a per-model cost, and the per-model entry carries a `costBasis` field so a consumer knows how the figure was derived. | S13-PROVIDER-020, S11-CONTEXT-004 | `context/cost.py :: CostModel` with `price_for(model, tier) -> Price`; `context/cost.py :: compute(usage) -> CostBreakdown` producing `total_cost_usd` and per-model `costUSD` with `costBasis`. | `test_cost.py::test_per_model_cost_sums_to_total` and `::test_cost_basis_present`. | An undocumented price basis makes a cost figure untrustworthy. Mitigation: the basis is a field, not a convention. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S11-QUEUE-025 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I2; S1-QUEUE-030 | The prompt queue is durable in the session state: queued entries survive a restart of the interactive session and are delivered when the session resumes, and queue operations are recorded in the transcript as a `queue-operation` entry type. | S1-QUEUE-030, S11-TRANSCRIPT-013 | `ui/queue.py :: PromptQueue` with `persist()`/`restore()` against the session state; `transcript/entries.py :: QueueOperationEntry`. | `test_queue.py::test_queue_survives_restart` and `::test_queue_operation_journalled`. | A lost queue loses the user's typed messages. Mitigation: queue entries are journalled at enqueue time. | P1 extensibility / Phase 2 |
| S11-TITLE-026 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 (the `ai-title` transcript entry type) | A session acquires an AI-generated title, recorded as its own transcript entry type so the title is versioned separately from the conversation. | S11-TRANSCRIPT-013 | `sessions/title.py :: generate(context) -> str`; `transcript/entries.py :: AiTitleEntry`. | `test_title.py::test_title_recorded_as_own_entry`. | A title derived from the conversation can leak sensitive content into a session list. Mitigation: the title is generated from a bounded summary and is a distinct entry type so it can be omitted. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |


---

## S12 — Progress reporting, error handling, UX

**Priority band:** P1 extensibility (several rows are P2 safety-tier because they are injection defences or operator-visibility controls).

**Evidence base.** `area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md` §2 F-I6 … F-I18 (the footer, the status area, the diff panel, the transcript viewer, the recap, invisible-character stripping, emoji and spellcheck); `docs-cache/statusline.md` (the status-line script contract and the JSON field vocabulary); `docs-cache/interactive-mode.md` § "General controls"; `agent-evidence.md` §6 L12/L18/L20; `agent-reference-shots/tui-background-session-raw.txt` and `tui-startup-banner.txt` (observed footer strings).

**Scope note.** S12 owns what the operator *sees*: the footer, the status line, the panels, the transcript rendering, and the small input conveniences. It does not own the events (S1) or the content (S3–S11).
| ID | Evidence | Acceptance criterion (testable) | Depends on | Proposed module + interface shape | Automated verification test | Security / operational risk + mitigation | Priority / Phase |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| S12-UX-001 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I7; agent-reference-shots/tui-background-session-raw.txt | The built-in footer is a **priority-ordered hint surface**: it always shows the permission-mode indicator, then appends contextual hints in order of what the operator most likely needs now (an interrupt affordance while busy; an agent-view and shortcut-help affordance while idle). The observed strings include `manual mode on`, `esc to interrupt` and `? for shortcuts`. | S6-MODE-001, S1-INPUT-026 | `ui/footer.py :: Footer` with `render(state: FooterState) -> Frame`; `FooterState` carrying `mode_label`, `busy`, `has_agents`, `pr_status`. | `test_footer.py::test_three_observed_variants` — assert the exact strings for busy-idle and idle-idle states; `::test_mode_label_is_manual_for_default`. | The footer is the operator's constant orientation cue. Mitigation: the hint set is a priority list, and the observed strings are pinned. | P1 extensibility / Phase 2 |
| S12-UX-002 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I7; docs-cache/statusline.md | The user-configurable status line is a **separate surface** from the built-in footer: it renders in its own row *above* the footer badges and does not replace them, and when a custom status line is configured most of the footer's keyboard hints are suppressed. | S12-UX-001 | `ui/statusline.py :: StatusLine` with `run(script, json_stdin) -> str`; the footer computes `suppress_hints = status_line_configured`. | `test_statusline.py::test_status_line_renders_above_footer` and `::test_custom_status_line_suppresses_hints`. | Merging the two surfaces would get the documented suppression behaviour wrong. Mitigation: they are separate components with an explicit interaction rule. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S12-UX-003 | docs-cache/statusline.md § "Available data" | The status line script receives a JSON session document on stdin with a documented field vocabulary (model id and display name, cwd, workspace fields including added dirs and git worktree, cost fields, context-window fields including used percentage, `exceeds_200k_tokens`, `fast_mode`, `effort.level`, `thinking.enabled`, rate-limit fields, `prompt_cache`, `session_id`, `session_name`, `prompt_id`). | S12-UX-002, S11-CONTEXT-004 | `ui/statusline.py :: build_payload(ctx) -> dict` with the documented key set. | `test_statusline.py::test_payload_field_set` — assert the key set matches the documented vocabulary. | The payload is a data disclosure surface to a user-supplied script. Mitigation: the payload contains no credential material, and the key set is pinned by a test. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S12-UX-004 | docs-cache/statusline.md | The status line is **debounced at 300 ms**, a change to the script's command itself skips the debounce and runs immediately, and a new update arriving while the script runs cancels the in-flight run. | S12-UX-002 | `ui/statusline.py :: StatusLineRunner` with `DEBOUNCE_MS = 300`, `update(payload)`, `set_command(cmd)`. | `test_statusline.py::test_updates_batched_within_debounce` and `::test_command_change_runs_immediately` and `::test_inflight_cancelled`. | A script that runs on every keystroke can spawn a process per event. Mitigation: the debounce and the cancellation are both tested. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S12-UX-005 | docs-cache/statusline.md | The status line's output is captured rather than connected to the terminal, so the script cannot read the terminal size from `tput`; the `COLUMNS` and `LINES` environment variables are set to the current dimensions instead. | S12-UX-004 | `ui/statusline.py :: run_script` capturing stdout with a pipe and setting `COLUMNS`/`LINES` in the child environment. | `test_statusline.py::test_columns_and_lines_set` and `::test_output_captured_not_inherited`. | A script that assumes a TTY can mis-render. Mitigation: the environment carries the dimensions and stdout is captured. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S12-UX-006 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I7 | The footer can carry a clickable pull-request or merge-request badge with a colour that encodes the review state (green approved, yellow pending, red changes requested, gray draft), and the badge disappears once the request is merged or closed. | S12-UX-001 | `ui/footer.py :: pull_request_badge(repo_state) -> Optional[Badge]` with `Badge(text, url, state)` and `state` in the documented set. | `test_footer.py::test_badge_states_mapped` and `::test_badge_absent_when_closed`. | A badge opens a URL in a browser; a wrong URL is a phishing vector. Mitigation: the URL is derived from the repository's own remote, not from model output. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S12-UX-007 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I7; docs-cache/statusline.md § "Available data" (`footerLinksRegexes`) | Clickable link badges can be added to the footer without writing a script, by configuring regular expressions that match identifiers appearing in the conversation. | S12-UX-006 | `ui/footer.py :: link_badges(text, patterns) -> list[Badge]` applying the configured regexes to the conversation text. | `test_footer.py::test_regex_links_extracted` and `::test_no_match_no_badge`. | A user-configured regex over conversation text could surface a secret in a clickable link. Mitigation: the badge text is the matched group and the URL scheme is restricted to http/https. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S12-UX-008 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I11 | The transcript viewer has its own keymap and the product has **two renderers** ('classic' and 'fullscreen') with different key availability; the active renderer is inspectable at runtime via a command with no argument. | S1-INPUT-026, S12-UX-009 | `ui/transcript.py :: TranscriptViewer` with `renderer: Literal['classic','fullscreen']` and a per-renderer keymap; `commands/` exposing a renderer-report command. | `test_transcript.py::test_keymaps_differ_by_renderer` and `::test_renderer_reported`. | Assuming a single renderer would mis-implement several documented keys. Mitigation: the renderer is an explicit field and the keymap is per-renderer. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S12-UX-009 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I11 | The transcript viewer expands content that is collapsed by default — such as repeated MCP calls shown as a single summary line and cross-session messages shown as a one-line preview — and can write the full conversation to the terminal's native scrollback or to a file for an external editor. | S12-UX-008 | `ui/transcript.py :: expand_all() -> RenderedTranscript`; `ui/transcript.py :: write_to_scrollback(text)` and `:: write_to_file(path)`. | `test_transcript.py::test_expand_all_reveals_repeated_calls` and `::test_scrollback_write`. | Writing conversation content to the native scrollback makes it searchable by other terminal tools and can expose it to screen sharing. Mitigation: the action is an explicit user command, not automatic. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S12-UX-010 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 5 F5, F6, F7; S5-BG-015 | Long-running work is surfaced to the operator as it proceeds: a backgrounded command shows real-time progress, and the operator can watch the running agents. | S5-BG-016, S10-MONITOR-028 | `ui/progress.py :: ProgressIndicator` with `attach(job)`, `detach(job)`; `exec/background.py` emitting progress events. | `test_progress.py::test_progress_shown_while_running` and `::test_detached_on_completion`. | Progress output that leaks command output to the screen may disclose secrets. Mitigation: the progress line shows status, not full output, unless the operator asks for the output. | P1 extensibility / Phase 2 |
| S12-UX-011 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I14 | A side-question channel exists: a question asked while the main turn is running is answered independently, without tool access, is not added to the conversation history, and replays at most the newest 20 earlier exchanges. | S4-LOOP-010, S12-UX-020 | `sessions/btw.py :: ask(question, ctx) -> SideAnswer` with `REPLAY_LIMIT = 20`; the answer is written to a dismissible overlay and excluded from the transcript history. | `test_btw.py::test_no_tool_calls_possible` and `::test_replay_bounded_at_20` and `::test_not_in_history`. | A side channel with tool access would be a permission bypass. Mitigation: the side-question context has no tools, asserted by a test. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S12-UX-012 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I14 | A side question can be forked into a background subagent that inherits the parent conversation plus the question and answer, so the operator can escalate a read-only question into a full-capability run; the fork is available only in local sessions. | S12-UX-011, S10-AGENT-002 | `sessions/btw.py :: fork_to_agent(answer) -> AgentHandle`; the fork inherits the parent's context and tool access. | `test_btw.py::test_fork_creates_agent_with_parent_context` and `::test_fork_inherits_permission_context`. | The escalation path must not bypass the parent's permissions. Mitigation: the fork inherits the parent's permission context and the test asserts gating. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S12-UX-013 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I12 | Shell mode is a distinct input mode with its own completion sources (the history of `!` commands in the current project, and live filesystem paths triggered by a forward slash), its own exit keys, and its own paste semantics. | S12-UX-014, S1-INPUT-026 | `ui/shellmode.py :: ShellMode` with `completions(prefix) -> list[str]` merging the two sources and `exit_keys`. | `test_shellmode.py::test_history_completion` and `::test_path_completion_on_slash` and `::test_paste_entering_shell_mode`. | Shell mode bypasses the model and therefore bypasses the permission prompt; the sandbox exemption is a documented consequence that must be surfaced (S12-UX-015). Mitigation: the exemption is shown to the operator when sandboxing is enabled. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S12-UX-014 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I12 | Shell-mode commands run **outside** the sandbox even when sandboxing is enabled, because the sandbox applies to the commands the model runs; the operator is told this. | S6-SANDBOX-030, S12-UX-013 | `sandbox/runner.py :: exempt_for_shell_mode() -> bool` with a documented exemption reason surfaced in the UI. | `test_sandbox.py::test_shell_mode_exemption_reported`. | A silent sandbox exemption would be a false sense of containment. Mitigation: the exemption is surfaced in the UI and is asserted. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 2 |
| S12-UX-015 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I16; agent-evidence.md §6 L16 | Invisible characters in a submitted prompt are removed **before** anything is sent: if anything was removed, the Enter sends nothing and the cleaned prompt returns to the input box with a notice giving the count and asking for a second Enter; on the command line the cleaned prompt is sent in one step, except when it would begin with `/`. | S12-UX-020, S1-CLI-007 | `text/invisible.py :: strip_invisible(text) -> StripResult` with `StripResult(text, removed_count)` and an explicit keep-list for Persian/Indic joiners and emoji variation selectors; `ui/editor.py :: submit()` implementing the two-step confirm. | `test_invisible.py::test_removal_requires_second_enter` and `::test_command_line_single_step` and `::test_joiner_and_selector_preserved`. | This is a prompt-injection defence at the submit boundary: pasted text can carry characters the terminal does not draw. Mitigation: an explicit keep-list (not a naive strip), the two-step confirm in the TUI, and a test for each exception. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S12-UX-016 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I15 | Emoji shortcode completion is available (`:` plus a shortcode), requires the shortcode to start the input or follow a space, and can be turned off with a setting that disables both the suggestion popup and the inline replacement. | S12-UX-015 | `ui/emoji.py :: complete(text) -> Optional[str]`; `config/settings.py :: emojiCompletionEnabled`. | `test_emoji.py::test_completion_only_after_space` and `::test_setting_disables_both_surfaces`. | A completion triggered inside a URL or a word would corrupt the prompt. Mitigation: the trigger position is checked and the off-switch covers both surfaces. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S12-UX-017 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I15 | Spell check is an optional external dependency with a fixed probe order (`aspell`, then `hunspell`, then `ispell`), a named checker that does **not** fall back, a bounded failure policy (two failures stop checking; three 15-second timeouts stop checking), and no word list of the product's own. | S12-UX-016 | `text/spellcheck.py :: SpellChecker` with `PROBE_ORDER = ('aspell','hunspell','ispell')`, `MAX_FAILURES = 2`, `MAX_TIMEOUTS = 3`, `TIMEOUT_S = 15`. | `test_spellcheck.py::test_probe_order` and `::test_two_failures_stop_checking` and `::test_named_checker_does_not_fall_back`. | An unbounded checker can hang the input path. Mitigation: the failure policy is bounded and tested at each boundary. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S12-UX-018 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I15 | Spell check runs only on the input box — never on the model's replies, never on the user's files — and is disabled in shell mode, in history search and in voice dictation; the checker's configuration (`checker`, `language`, `color`) is taken from exactly one settings scope, managed first, then `--settings`, then user. | S12-UX-017 | `text/spellcheck.py :: should_check(input_state) -> bool`; `config/loader.py` resolving the spellcheck block from a single scope. | `test_spellcheck.py::test_scope_selection_uses_one_source` and `::test_shell_mode_not_checked`. | Checking file contents would send them to an external process. Mitigation: the checker only ever sees the input buffer, asserted by the arrangement. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S12-UX-019 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I18 | The automatic session recap is generated in the background only when at least three minutes have passed since the last completed turn, the terminal is unfocused, the session has at least three turns, and it has not already appeared immediately before; the output is capped at 400 characters; the automatic recap never appears in non-interactive mode. | S11-CONTEXT-022 | `sessions/recap.py :: should_generate(ctx, idle_ms, focused, turn_count, last_shown) -> bool` with `MIN_IDLE_MS = 180_000`, `MIN_TURNS = 3`; `MAX_RECAP_CHARS = 400`. | `test_recap.py::test_trigger_predicate` — table-driven over the four conditions; `::test_not_in_print_mode`. | An automatic recap that fires too often is noise; one that leaks content into an idle screen is a disclosure. Mitigation: the trigger predicate is explicit and the output is capped. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S12-UX-020 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I18 | An on-demand recap is distinct from the automatic one: it runs only when the operator asks, it is never run unattended (an unattended request returns a notice instead), and it is not produced in a relayed message from a chat integration or a scheduled task. | S12-UX-019 | `sessions/recap.py :: on_demand(ctx, origin) -> RecapOutcome` with `origin` in `Literal['operator','relayed','scheduled']` and the relayed/scheduled origins returning a notice. | `test_recap.py::test_relayed_request_returns_notice` and `::test_operator_request_runs`. | An unattended recap could summarise a session into a third-party chat. Mitigation: the origin is explicit and the unattended paths are refused. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S12-UX-021 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 5 F13; area5-bash-permissions.md F13 | The permission prompt's layout, options and key bindings are specified, including the ability to attach a comment to a chosen option — a comment that is carried into the decision and shown to the model. | S6-APPROVE-036 | `ui/confirm.py :: PermissionPrompt` with `Option(id, label, action, comment_capable)`; the chosen option's comment is included in the `Decision`. | `test_prompt.py::test_comment_attached_to_option` and `::test_comment_reaches_the_decision`. | A comment is a human instruction attached to an approval; dropping it would lose the operator's intent. Mitigation: the comment is a field on the decision and is tested end to end. | P1 extensibility / Phase 2 |
| S12-UX-022 | docs-cache/statusline.md; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 8 F-7 | The product's error presentation to the operator distinguishes three audiences: the model (an actionable reason), the operator (a readable notice in the transcript, including hook error notices), and automation (structured metadata on the envelope). | S7-DENIAL-010, S7-HOOK-018 | `ui/errors.py :: OperatorNotice` rendered into the transcript; `protocol/envelope.py` carrying the machine form. | `test_errors.py::test_three_audiences_get_distinct_forms`. | Collapsing the audiences loses either the operator's clarity or automation's parseability. Mitigation: three distinct renderings, one test. | P1 extensibility / Phase 2 |
| S12-UX-023 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I10 | Vim editing mode is a full modal editor with navigation, editing, text objects and visual mode, and it survives transitions to the transcript viewer and to panels, preserving the mode and the cursor position. | S1-INPUT-028 | `ui/vim.py :: VimState(mode, cursor, pending_sequence)` with `handle_key(key) -> Action`; the state is preserved across panel transitions. | `test_vim.py::test_state_preserved_across_panel_transitions` and `::test_insert_mode_remap_two_key_sequence`. | The insert-mode remap is a prefix-timer mechanism with a one-second window and the specific behaviour that a mismatched second key leaves both characters as literal text. Mitigation: the timer is implemented and the mismatched case is tested. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S12-UX-024 | area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I10 | The insert-mode remap setting is read only from the user settings file, the `--settings` flag and managed settings; entries in a project's settings files are ignored, so a checked-out repository cannot remap the operator's keystrokes. | S12-UX-023, S2-SETTINGS-011 | `ui/vim.py :: load_remaps(scopes) -> dict[str,str]` restricted to the three allowed scopes; the project scope is excluded. | `test_vim.py::test_project_scope_remap_ignored` and `::test_user_scope_remap_applied`. | A repository that could remap the operator's keystrokes could redirect a dangerous shortcut. Mitigation: the scope restriction is enforced at load time and is tested. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |

## S13 — Model selection, inference config, provider abstraction

**Priority band:** P0 parity-core.

**Evidence base.** `agent-evidence.md` §4 Area 11 (model configuration) and §4 Area 12 F1–F6, F19 (dependencies and the capability matrix); `agent-reference-shots/areas/area-model-config.md` §2.1–§2.10 (aliases, precedence chain, policy layer, overrides, unrecognised models, effort, fallback chains, autocompact window, diagnostics); `agent-reference-shots/areas/area12-dependencies.md` F1–F6, F19, F20; `agent-reference-shots/print-mode-json-result.json` (`modelUsage[*].canonicalModel`, `.provider`, `.contextWindow`).

**Scope note.** S13 owns *which* model runs, *with what* effort and window, and how a provider boundary is expressed. It does not own the transport retry policy (S7) or the capability gating of individual features (which consumes S13's matrix).
| ID | Evidence | Acceptance criterion (testable) | Depends on | Proposed module + interface shape | Automated verification test | Security / operational risk + mitigation | Priority / Phase |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| S13-MODEL-001 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 11; area-model-config.md §2.2 | The model is resolved by a **five-level chain**: the CLI `--model` flag, then per-role model overrides, then the `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` environment variable, then the settings `model` key, then the built-in default. The first level that yields a value wins. | none | `models/resolve.py :: resolve_model(layers: ModelLayers) -> ResolvedModel` with `ModelLayers(cli, role_overrides, env, settings, default)`; `models/resolve.py :: ResolvedModel(model_id, source)`. | `test_model_resolution.py::test_five_level_precedence` — table-driven over the levels, asserting the winner and the recorded source. | The order determines whether an operator's explicit choice can be overridden by ambient state. Mitigation: the chain is one function, table-tested, and the source is recorded so the choice is auditable. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S13-MODEL-002 | area-model-config.md §2.1 | Model aliases exist and map to concrete versions through an alias-to-version table; an alias is resolved to a concrete model id before the request is issued. | S13-MODEL-001 | `models/aliases.py :: ALIAS_TABLE: dict[str, str]` and `:: resolve_alias(name) -> str`; the resolved concrete id is what appears in `modelUsage[*].canonicalModel`. | `test_model_alias.py::test_alias_resolves_to_version` and `::test_canonical_model_reported`. | An alias table that drifts from the actual model set produces request failures. Mitigation: the table is a data file validated on load. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S13-MODEL-003 | area-model-config.md §2.3 | An organisation-level policy layer can restrict the available models; the effective model set is the intersection of the policy and the catalogue, and a model excluded by policy cannot be selected by any lower-precedence layer. | S13-MODEL-001, S6-RULESET-053 | `models/policy.py :: PolicyLayer` with `available_models() -> set[str]`; `models/resolve.py` filtering the resolved model against the policy and failing closed if it is excluded. | `test_model_policy.py::test_policy_excludes_model` and `::test_excluded_model_cannot_be_selected_by_cli`. | A policy that a flag can override is not a policy. Mitigation: the filter is applied after resolution and the test asserts the flag cannot win. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 2 |
| S13-MODEL-004 | area-model-config.md §2.4; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 12 F4 | Per-role model overrides allow different roles (for example a background helper or a safety classifier) to run on different models, and the override participates in the same precedence chain rather than bypassing it. | S13-MODEL-001, S13-PROVIDER-016 | `models/resolve.py :: resolve_for_role(role, layers)` selecting the role's override layer; `models/roles.py :: ROLE_NAMES`. | `test_model_resolution.py::test_role_override_used_for_that_role_only`. | A role running on an unapproved model can bypass a policy intended for it. Mitigation: the role's resolved model is filtered by the same policy layer. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S13-MODEL-005 | area-model-config.md §2.5; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 11 | An unrecognised model id is not an error: the product assumes a 200,000-token context window, records the model as unrecognised, and writes a `[claude-code:unrecognized_model]` diagnostic to stderr. | S11-THRESHOLD-012, S7-DIAG-023 | `models/registry.py :: window_for(model_id)` falling back to 200,000; `models/registry.py :: is_recognised(model_id) -> bool`; the diagnostic written on first use. | `test_model_unknown.py::test_unknown_model_assumes_200k_and_diagnoses`. | Silently accepting an unknown model can produce a request that will fail or a mis-sized context. Mitigation: a fallback that is stated in a diagnostic, plus a way to correct it. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S13-EFFORT-006 | area-model-config.md §2.6; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 11 | Five effort levels are supported: `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` and `max`; the level is published per turn in the init event and the envelope's `per_turn_effort_active` field. | S13-MODEL-001, S1-CLI-006 | `models/effort.py :: Effort` enum with the five levels; `models/effort.py :: parse(value) -> Effort`; `protocol/events.py :: InitEvent.per_turn_effort_active`. | `test_effort.py::test_five_levels` and `::test_level_published_in_init_and_envelope`. | An unknown level silently mapped to a default misrepresents the requested compute. Mitigation: the enum is closed and the published value is asserted. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S13-EFFORT-007 | area-model-config.md §2.6 | An invalid effort value produces a **warning and is ignored** (the default is used), which is deliberately different from an invalid `--autocompact` value, which is a hard error. | S13-EFFORT-006, S11-AUTOCOMPACT-010 | `models/effort.py :: parse_lenient(value) -> ParseOutcome(value, warning)`; the CLI writes the warning to stderr and continues. | `test_effort.py::test_invalid_effort_warns_and_uses_default` and `::test_invalid_autocompact_exits_nonzero`. | The asymmetry must be deliberate: a typo in an effort level should not block a run, but a typo in a threshold should not silently change it. Mitigation: both behaviours are tested. | P1 extensibility / Phase 2 |
| S13-FALLBACK-008 | area-model-config.md §2.7 | A fallback chain allows a model to fall back to another on certain failures, and the chain is configured rather than hard-coded; the model actually used is reported in the usage accounting. | S13-MODEL-001, S7-API-014 | `models/fallback.py :: FallbackChain(models: list[str], on: set[FailureClass])`; `models/fallback.py :: next_for(failure) -> Optional[str]`. | `test_fallback.py::test_chain_applied_on_matching_failure` and `::test_actual_model_reported`. | An automatic fallback can silently change cost and capability. Mitigation: the model actually used is always reported per model in the envelope. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S13-FALLBACK-009 | area-model-config.md §2.7; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 11 | A fallback must not cross a capability boundary: falling back to a model that lacks a capability the session depends on must be prevented or must degrade the affected feature explicitly. | S13-CAP-011, S13-FALLBACK-008 | `models/fallback.py :: filter_by_capability(chain, required) -> list[str]` using the capability matrix. | `test_fallback.py::test_fallback_respects_required_capabilities`. | A silent capability downgrade produces a session that appears to work but is missing a tool. Mitigation: the chain is filtered by the required capability set before use. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S13-CAP-010 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 12 F3, F4; area12-dependencies.md F3, F4 | Capability availability is a **compile-time (provider, model, plan) matrix**, and a Tier 2 feature fails **by subtraction** — the tool is simply absent from the registry — rather than by raising an error when used. | S1-TOOLSET-022, S13-PROVIDER-015 | `capabilities/matrix.py :: CapabilityMatrix` as a data table of `(provider, model, plan) -> set[Feature]`; `tools/registry.py` filtering by it. | `test_capabilities.py::test_tier2_feature_absent_by_subtraction` — assert the tool is not in the published list rather than failing at call time. | Failing by subtraction is a fail-safe: the model cannot attempt what it cannot do. Mitigation: the matrix is a data table and the absence is asserted. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S13-CAP-011 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 12 F4, F19; area12-dependencies.md F4 | The provider × feature matrix is genuinely non-uniform and must be modelled as data rather than as conditionals scattered through the code. | S13-CAP-010 | `capabilities/matrix.py` as a declarative table; a loader that validates every (provider, model, plan) row against the known feature vocabulary. | `test_capabilities.py::test_matrix_rows_validate_against_vocabulary`. | Scattered conditionals drift out of sync with the data. Mitigation: the matrix is the only source and a test validates it against the vocabulary. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S13-PROVIDER-012 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 12 F1; area12-dependencies.md F1 | The core loop has exactly one hard dependency: a reachable model endpoint. Everything else degrades; the endpoint is not optional. | S13-PROVIDER-015 | `transport/provider.py :: Provider` interface with `send(request) -> Response`; the runtime refuses to start without a configured provider. | `test_provider.py::test_missing_provider_is_a_hard_startup_error`. | A missing endpoint must fail loudly at start-up, not after the operator has typed a prompt. Mitigation: the provider is resolved before the session starts. | P0 parity-core / Phase 1 |
| S13-PROVIDER-013 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 12 F2, F17; S2-ENV-015 | `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` acts as a **capability gate**: setting it disables four features outright, so the product is not merely redirected but functionally reduced. | S2-ENV-015 | `capabilities/gate.py :: apply_base_url_gate(features, env) -> set[Feature]`; the gate is applied to the registry filter. | `test_capability_gate.py::test_four_features_disabled` — assert the exact set is removed when the variable is set. | A half-working feature against a non-vendor endpoint is a correctness hazard. Mitigation: the gate removes the feature entirely. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S13-PROVIDER-014 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 12 F7, F8, F17, F19; area12-dependencies.md F7, F8, F17 | The network allowlist is large, enumerated and **per-feature**: each feature declares which hosts it may contact, and the traffic opt-outs disable specific classes of traffic rather than everything. | S13-PROVIDER-015 | `net/allowlist.py :: FeatureNetworkPolicy` as a table of `feature -> set[host]`; `net/proxy.py :: check(feature, url) -> Decision`. | `test_allowlist.py::test_per_feature_policy_enforced` and `::test_opt_out_disables_only_its_class`. | A single global allowlist is either too broad (leaks data) or too narrow (breaks features). Mitigation: per-feature policy data, tested. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S13-PROVIDER-015 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 12 F9, F10; area12-dependencies.md F9, F10 | Telemetry is a separate, per-provider, per-service dependency with four independent opt-outs; the opt-outs are honoured independently and the current state is reported. | S13-PROVIDER-012, S12-UX-003 | `telemetry/state.py :: TelemetryState` with four independent flags and `state()` reported in the init event's `analytics_disabled` and `product_feedback_disabled` fields and elsewhere. | `test_telemetry.py::test_four_independent_optouts` and `::test_state_published`. | A partially-honoured opt-out is a privacy breach. Mitigation: each opt-out is an independent flag with its own test. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S13-PROVIDER-016 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 12 F5; area12-dependencies.md F5 | A second, independent model provider exists for the safety classifier and the advisor, separate from the main model provider, and its absence disables the features that depend on it. | S6-CLS-026, S13-PROVIDER-012 | `transport/classifier_provider.py :: ClassifierProvider` resolved separately from the main provider; the capability matrix marks the classifier features as absent when it is unavailable. | `test_provider.py::test_classifier_features_absent_without_provider`. | A classifier that silently falls back to the main model loses its independence. Mitigation: the fallback is by absence, not by substitution, and the absence is asserted. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S13-PROVIDER-017 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 12 F20; area12-dependencies.md F20 | There is no offline *mode*: the product is a continuum of per-feature degradation, and each feature declares its own dependency so that a missing dependency degrades only that feature. | S13-CAP-010, S13-PROVIDER-012 | `capabilities/dependencies.py :: feature_dependencies() -> dict[Feature, set[Dependency]]`; the matrix is computed as the closure of available dependencies. | `test_dependencies.py::test_missing_dependency_degrades_only_its_feature`. | A global offline switch would be a different product. Mitigation: per-feature dependency declarations and a test that proves the isolation. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
| S13-AUTO-018 | area-model-config.md §2.8; S11-AUTOCOMPACT-010 | The auto-compaction window is derived from the model's context window, so a larger model compacts later; the value is configurable within bounds. | S11-AUTOCOMPACT-010, S11-CONTEXT-001 | `context/compaction.py :: default_window(model) -> int` derived from `ContextWindow.total_tokens`. | `test_autocompact.py::test_window_scales_with_context_size`. | A hard-coded window mis-sizes the trigger for every model except one. Mitigation: the window is derived from the registry. | P0 parity-core / Phase 2 |
| S13-DIAG-019 | area-model-config.md §2.9 | Model configuration errors have their own diagnostic surface, separate from the general diagnostics, and they are written to stderr so stdout stays parseable. | S7-DIAG-023, S13-MODEL-005 | `output/writer.py :: write_diagnostic('model', message)`; the diagnostic surface enumerates the model-configuration conditions. | `test_model_diag.py::test_diagnostic_on_stderr` and `::test_diagnostic_conditions_covered`. | A configuration mistake that only shows up at request time wastes a round trip. Mitigation: the diagnostic is emitted at resolution time. | P1 extensibility / Phase 2 |
| S13-ACCT-020 | area-model-config.md §2.10; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 12 F12 | Account- and organisation-level model policy is separate from the local settings and can restrict the model set, the features or the available endpoints; the local settings cannot widen it. | S13-MODEL-003, S6-RULESET-053 | `models/policy.py :: AccountPolicy` loaded from the account configuration; the local policy is intersected with it. | `test_model_policy.py::test_account_policy_narrows_local_policy`. | A local setting that widens an account restriction breaks the account's guarantee. Mitigation: the intersection is computed at resolution time and tested. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S13-ENFORCE-021 | agent-evidence.md §7/§11 (the `enforceAvailableModels` binding is listed as an open question) | The binding between the `availableModels` policy key and the enforced model set is not fully characterised in the evidence; the reimplementation must define it explicitly and make the enforced set inspectable. | S13-MODEL-003, S2-SELF-016 | `models/policy.py :: enforced_models() -> set[str]` reading the `availableModels` key; the enforced set is reported by the self-inspection surface. | `test_model_policy.py::test_enforced_set_inspectable` and `::test_available_models_binding_documented`. | An unenforced policy key is a false assurance. Mitigation: the binding is defined in one place and the resulting set is inspectable. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S13-CRED-022 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 11 (the credential source is reported as `apiKeySource` in the init event); agent-evidence.md §2 (credential mechanics were deliberately not investigated) | The credential source is reported as a named category in the init event (never the credential itself), so a host can tell whether the session is using an environment variable, a helper, or an interactive login. | S1-CLI-006, S14-REDACT-004 | `auth/source.py :: CredentialSource` enum; `protocol/events.py :: InitEvent.apiKeySource` carrying the category only. | `test_credential_source.py::test_source_category_published_without_value` — assert the field is a category and that no credential material appears anywhere in the init event. | Publishing a credential, or a field whose name suggests one, invites accidental capture by a logging pipeline. Mitigation: the field is a closed enum of categories and the init event is scanned for secret-looking content in tests. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 2 |
| S13-THINK-023 | area-model-config.md §2.6; agent-reference-shots/print-mode-json-result.json (`thinkingTokens`) | Extended thinking is a toggleable capability and its token consumption is accounted separately (`thinkingTokens` in the per-model usage) so that cost attribution stays correct. | S13-EFFORT-006, S11-CONTEXT-004 | `models/thinking.py :: ThinkingConfig(enabled, budget_tokens)`; `context/usage.py :: Usage.thinking_tokens` counted separately from output tokens. | `test_thinking.py::test_thinking_tokens_accounted_separately`. | Folding thinking tokens into output tokens misprices a request. Mitigation: a separate counter and a test. | P1 extensibility / Phase 2 |
| S13-THRESH-024 | agent-reference-shots/print-mode-json-result.json (`exceeds_200k_tokens` appears in the statusline vocabulary); docs-cache/statusline.md | The 200,000-token threshold is a reported signal (the status-line payload exposes `exceeds_200k_tokens`), so an operator can see when a session has crossed the standard context boundary. | S11-THRESHOLD-012, S12-UX-003 | `context/window.py :: exceeds_standard_window(used) -> bool` at 200,000; `ui/statusline.py` exposing it in the payload. | `test_context_window.py::test_threshold_reported`. | The threshold is a billing boundary as well as a technical one. Mitigation: it is computed from the constant and reported, not hard-coded into the payload builder. | P1 extensibility / Phase 3 |
