## S14 — Automated tests, auditability, security, reproducibility

**Priority band:** P2 safety-tier. This subsystem is meta: it specifies how the *other* fourteen subsystems are proved. It is listed last in implementation order but its first rows (the harness contract and the golden transcripts) must exist before the parity core can be considered done.

**Evidence base.** `agent-evidence.md` §6 (the 33 documented limitations — each of which becomes a regression test), §7 and §11 (40 unresolved questions and 44 unknowns — each becomes either a test or an explicit design decision), §12 (the evidence index and the artefact set); `agent-reference-shots/` (the captured behavioural artefacts that serve as golden fixtures); `docs-cache/settings-reference.md` (the 245-key registry, 18 sections).

**Scope note.** S14 does not add product behaviour. It specifies the test strategy, the audit surfaces, the redaction rules, and the determinism guarantees that the rest of the document's rows depend on.
| ID | Evidence | Acceptance criterion (testable) | Depends on | Proposed module + interface shape | Automated verification test | Security / operational risk + mitigation | Priority / Phase |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| S14-TEST-001 | agent-evidence.md §2 (Method), §12 (Evidence index); agent-reference-shots/ (the captured artefact set) | Every observed artefact in the evidence tree is promoted to a **golden fixture**: a sanitised input plus the expected observable output, and a test that replays it and compares. | none | `tests/fixtures.py :: Fixture` with `Fixture(name, inputs, expected, kind)`; `tests/golden.py :: run_fixture(fixture) -> Comparison` producing a diff on mismatch. | `test_golden.py::test_all_fixtures_pass` — a single parameterised test over the fixture directory. | A golden suite that is not sanitised becomes a credential-leak vector. Mitigation: the fixture loader refuses to load a fixture containing a secret-looking value. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 2 |
| S14-TEST-002 | agent-evidence.md §6 L1–L33 (the documented limitations table) | Each of the 33 documented limitations becomes an explicit regression test asserting the *same* behaviour, so that a future 'fix' is a deliberate, visible change rather than an accidental divergence. | S14-TEST-001 | `tests/limitations.py :: LIMITATION_TESTS` mapping each limitation id to a test function; `test_limitations.py::test_all_limitations_pinned`. | `test_limitations.py::test_all_limitations_pinned` — assert every limitation id has a test and that each test asserts the reference behaviour. | Silently improving a limitation breaks parity but also hides that it happened. Mitigation: each limitation is pinned and the mapping is complete by assertion. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 2 |
| S14-TEST-003 | agent-evidence.md §7 U1–U40, §11 (the 44 unknowns) | Each of the 44 unknowns is either closed by a test (once the behaviour is determined) or carried as an explicit, tracked open question with a named owner; no unknown is silently implemented. | S14-TEST-001 | `docs/unknowns.py :: UNKNOWNS: list[Unknown]` with `Unknown(id, statement, status=Literal['open','closed','by-design'], test_ref)`; a completeness test asserts every unknown has a status. | `test_unknowns.py::test_every_unknown_has_a_status` and `::test_closed_unknowns_have_a_test`. | An untracked unknown becomes an undocumented divergence. Mitigation: the registry is code, not prose, and a test enforces completeness. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 2 |
| S14-EVENT-004 | agent-reference-shots/PROTOCOL-NOTES.md; agent-evidence.md §4 X1; agent-reference-shots/print-mode-json-result.json | The event protocol has a **conformance suite**: every emitted event type and subtype is validated against a schema, and an event with an unknown type is a test failure rather than a silent extension. | S1-CLI-005, S1-RESULT-011 | `protocol/schema.py :: EVENT_SCHEMAS: dict[(type, subtype), Schema]`; `protocol/validate.py :: validate(event) -> list[str]` returning the violations. | `test_event_schema.py::test_all_emitted_events_validate` — run a scripted session and validate every event; `::test_unknown_type_is_a_failure`. | An undocumented event type breaks a client that switches on type. Mitigation: a closed schema registry plus a conformance test over a real session. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 2 |
| S14-DET-005 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 F13, §4 Area 2 F19; S1-SESSION-025, S2-ENV-016A | A **determinism contract** exists for the parts of the system that can be deterministic: given the same inputs, the same tool registry, the same instruction set and the same permission rules, the harness produces the same tool-call sequence, the same result-envelope shape and the same file effects — with a documented list of the inputs that are *not* controlled (timestamps, session ids, uuids, model output). | S1-SESSION-025, S14-TEST-001 | `tests/determinism.py :: DeterminismSpec` listing the controlled and uncontrolled inputs; `tests/determinism.py :: normalise(result) -> Result` masking the uncontrolled fields. | `test_determinism.py::test_same_inputs_same_tool_sequence` — run the same scripted session twice and compare the normalised traces. | Without a declared set of uncontrolled inputs, a determinism test is flaky and gets disabled. Mitigation: the uncontrolled set is declared and the normalisation is explicit. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S14-BARE-006 | area6-tests-errors.md §7b.3 H12; S1-SESSION-019 | The `--bare` mode provides a **credential-free, project-content-free, deterministic** run mode suitable for CI, and the determinism guarantee is testable: two `--bare` runs over the same input produce the same normalised trace. | S1-SESSION-019, S14-DET-005 | `cli/flags.py :: apply_bare`; `tests/determinism.py` normalising the `--bare` trace. | `test_bare_determinism.py::test_two_bare_runs_match`. | `--bare` must not leak credentials into a CI log. Mitigation: the mode is asserted to consult no credential source. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S14-SEC-007 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 8 F-7, §6 L18/L23; S6-APPROVE-037 | The audit record of permission decisions retains the **full tool input** for denials, and the audit sink is a separate projection from the model-facing text so that redaction can be applied to one and not the other. | S6-APPROVE-037, S14-REDACT-008 | `audit/record.py :: AuditRecord(decision, tool_input, decided_by, at)`; `audit/sink.py :: AuditSink` writing to a file, separate from the transcript writer. | `test_audit.py::test_audit_retains_full_input` and `::test_model_text_and_audit_diverge_independently`. | A redacted audit trail is not an audit trail. Mitigation: the audit sink is separate and the test asserts the divergence is possible. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S14-REDACT-008 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 1 (the evidence artefact set is sanitised); agent-reference-shots/ (sanitised probe captures) | Every captured artefact in the evidence tree is sanitised: credential-like values, tokens, and personal identifiers are replaced with typed placeholders before the artefact is stored, and the sanitiser is applied automatically at capture time. | S14-TEST-001 | `audit/redact.py :: sanitise(text) -> str` replacing recognised patterns with typed placeholders such as `<REDACTED:API_KEY>`; `audit/redact.py` applied in the capture path. | `test_redact.py::test_known_patterns_replaced` and `::test_sanitiser_runs_at_capture` — assert the capture path calls the sanitiser. | Sanitisation that is applied later, by hand, will be missed at least once. Mitigation: the sanitiser is in the capture path and the test asserts it is called. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 2 |
| S14-SEC-009 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 8 §8 (workspace trust); S6-TRUST-033 | A **security test matrix** covers the permission subsystem: for each mode, each rule kind and each safeguard, a test asserts the outcome, so that a change to the evaluation order cannot weaken a control silently. | S6-RULE-010, S6-MODE-001 | `tests/security_matrix.py :: MATRIX` as a table of `(mode, rule_kind, request, expected_outcome)`; `test_security_matrix.py::test_all_matrix_cases`. | `test_security_matrix.py::test_all_matrix_cases` — a table-driven test over the whole matrix. | Ordering bugs in a permission engine are the classic silent regression. Mitigation: the matrix is data and the test is exhaustive over it. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 2 |
| S14-SEC-010 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 8 §4.2 (the critical-path removal circuit breaker); S6-CRIT-022 | A **destructive-command test corpus** is maintained: the documented dangerous command forms (variable expansion, command substitution, nested forms, Windows-specific forms) each have a test asserting the refusal, and the corpus is versioned. | S6-CRIT-022, S6-CRIT-023 | `tests/destructive_corpus.py :: CASES: list[CommandCase]` with `CommandCase(command, expected_kind, note)`; `test_destructive_corpus.py::test_all_cases_refused`. | `test_destructive_corpus.py::test_all_cases_refused` and `::test_corpus_version_recorded`. | A bypass form discovered in the wild must be added to the corpus. Mitigation: the corpus is versioned and the version is asserted so additions are visible. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 2 |
| S14-SEC-011 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6b (worktree isolation, four checks); S10-WORKTREE-020 | A **sandbox capability test matrix** asserts, for each platform, which isolation mechanisms are available and what happens when they are not — the product must report the absence rather than degrade silently. | S6-SANDBOX-030, S6-SANDBOX-031 | `tests/sandbox_matrix.py :: PLATFORM_EXPECTATIONS` mapping platform → available mechanisms; `test_sandbox_matrix.py::test_platform_expectations`. | `test_sandbox_matrix.py::test_absence_is_reported` — assert that on a platform without a sandbox the session reports the reduced isolation rather than claiming protection. | Claiming sandbox protection that is not present is a serious misrepresentation. Mitigation: the test asserts the report, not just the behaviour. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S14-INJ-012 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 3 F3-13, §4 Area 10 F10-10, §6 L26; S9-SKILL-010, S10-AGENT-004 | An **injection-resistance test suite** exercises the boundary cases: a delegated report containing instruction-shaped text, a skill body containing an inline command, a file containing invisible characters, a tool output containing an impersonated system message. Each case asserts the harness classifies and/or neutralises it. | S9-SKILL-010, S12-UX-015, S4-EXEC-017 | `tests/injection.py :: CASES: list[InjectionCase]` with `InjectionCase(vector, arrangement, expected)`; `test_injection.py::test_all_cases_neutralised`. | `test_injection.py::test_all_cases_neutralised` — each case asserts the specific defence (scan flag, refusal, stripping). | The defences are only as good as their test corpus. Mitigation: the corpus is a first-class artefact and the test is exhaustive over it. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S14-AUDIT-013 | ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION (the reference's audit-log facility was not directly characterised; the observed audit-adjacent surfaces are the permission-denials array on the result envelope and the transcript entries) | A structured, machine-readable **audit log** is written for every security-relevant action: a tool call that was decided by the permission engine, a mode change, a sandbox change, a hook verdict, and a credential source change. Each record is a single line of JSON with a timestamp, the session id and the action. | S6-APPROVE-037, S14-SEC-007 | `audit/log.py :: AuditLog` writing JSON lines to a file under the config root; `audit/log.py :: record(action: str, **fields) -> None`. | `test_audit.py::test_security_actions_are_logged` — perform one of each action and assert one line per action with the required keys. | An audit log that omits the actor or the decision is not useful for investigation. Mitigation: the record shape is fixed and asserted. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S14-AUDIT-014 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 6 F19 (the observed subagent hand-back framing); S10-AGENT-004 | Delegated-agent output is **marked as untrusted content** in every place it enters a transcript, so that an auditor can distinguish model output from operator input and from tool output. | S10-AGENT-004, S4-EXEC-017 | `transcript/entries.py` with an `untrusted_content` marker on delegated reports; `transcript/entries.py :: is_untrusted(entry) -> bool`. | `test_audit.py::test_delegated_output_marked_untrusted`. | Without the marker, an auditor cannot reconstruct which content the operator actually authored. Mitigation: the marker is applied at insertion and asserted. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S14-REP-015 | agent-evidence.md §2 (the compliance boundary recorded in the evidence file); S15-SEC-030 | A **reproducibility statement** is published for each release: the tool versions, the model ids used for the reference captures, the platform, and the fixture corpus version, so that a parity failure can be attributed to a change rather than to environment drift. | S14-DET-005, S14-TEST-001 | `release/repro.py :: ReproManifest` emitted at build time with the tool versions, the fixture corpus version and the capture metadata. | `test_repro.py::test_manifest_emitted_and_complete`. | An unattributable parity failure stalls the programme. Mitigation: the manifest is a build artefact and its completeness is asserted. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 5 |
| S14-COMPAT-016 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 11 (the `--system-prompt-snapshot` and `--from-pr` interactions are listed as open questions); S1-SESSION-025 | Compatibility flags whose interaction with the session snapshot is not fully characterised are implemented behind an explicit switch, defaulting to the conservative behaviour, until the interaction is determined. | S11-RESUME-017, S1-SESSION-025 | `cli/flags.py` with the flag parsed and its effect recorded on the session; the snapshot-provenance field records which path was taken. | `test_compat.py::test_flag_records_provenance` and `::test_conservative_default`. | A flag with an unknown interaction can change what the model is told. Mitigation: the effect is recorded, not assumed. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S14-FUZZ-017 | ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION (no fuzzing evidence in the captured tree; proposed as the standard hardening measure for the parsers the product must accept untrusted input from) | The parsers that accept untrusted input — the command splitter, the bash/PowerShell glob matcher, the skill frontmatter parser, the command-menu matcher, the path matcher and the transcript reader — are each fuzzed with a corpus, and a crash or an unhandled exception fails the build. | S5-CMD-012, S9-SKILL-008, S11-TRANSCRIPT-013 | `tests/fuzz.py :: PARSERS` mapping each parser to a corpus directory; `test_fuzz.py::test_no_parser_crashes`. | `test_fuzz.py::test_no_parser_crashes` — run each corpus and assert no unhandled exception. | A crash in a matcher is a denial of service and can be triggered by repository content. Mitigation: the parsers are fuzzed and the build fails on a crash. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S14-PROP-018 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 3 F3-13 (the subagent report is scanned for instruction-shaped content); S4-EXEC-017 | A **property-based test suite** asserts the invariants that must hold for every input, not just the examples: a denied call never has an effect; a compaction never increases the token count; a transcript read never loses a branch; a permission decision is always recorded exactly once. | S6-DENIAL-038, S11-COMPACT-006 | `tests/properties.py` using a property-testing library with the invariant set declared; `test_properties.py::test_invariants_hold`. | `test_properties.py::test_invariants_hold` — generate inputs and assert each invariant. | Example-based tests miss the input nobody thought of. Mitigation: the invariant set is declared and exercised over generated inputs. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S14-LOAD-019 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 3 F3-9 (concurrency bounds); S10-LIMIT-014 | A **load and soak suite** exercises the concurrency bounds: many agents at the limit, a full inbox, a full job registry, and a long-running session, asserting that the bounds hold and that the state files remain consistent. | S10-LIMIT-014, S10-MAIL-026, S10-JOB-017 | `tests/load.py :: load_scenarios()`; `test_load.py::test_bounds_hold_under_load`. | `test_load.py::test_bounds_hold_under_load` and `::test_state_files_remain_parseable`. | A file-backed coordination surface can corrupt under concurrent writers. Mitigation: the soak test asserts the state files stay parseable. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S14-PLAT-020 | agent-evidence.md §6 L3 (no OS sandbox on native Windows), §4 Area 5 F24 (Windows shell behaviour) | A **platform matrix** runs the parity suite on each supported platform and shell, and the supported set is declared; a platform outside the declared set is not claimed to be supported. | S5-WIN-023, S6-SANDBOX-030 | `tests/platforms.py :: SUPPORTED_PLATFORMS` with `test_platform_matrix.py::test_declared_platforms_pass`. | `test_platform_matrix.py::test_declared_platforms_pass`. | Claiming support for an untested platform is a reliability and safety claim that cannot be honoured. Mitigation: the supported set is data and the CI matrix is derived from it. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S14-CONF-021 | agent-evidence.md §4 X2 (the settings reference with 245 keys across 17 topical sections); §4 Area 8 §9.2 | The settings registry is **complete and validated**: every documented key is present in the registry, every key is typed, and a key that is not in the registry is rejected rather than ignored. | S2-SETTINGS-011, S6-SETTINGS-050 | `config/registry.py :: SETTINGS_KEYS: dict[str, KeySpec]` with `KeySpec(type, scopes_allowed, restrict_only)`; `config/loader.py` rejecting unknown keys. | `test_settings_registry.py::test_registry_complete` and `::test_unknown_key_rejected`. | A silently ignored setting gives a false impression of configuration. Mitigation: the registry is complete by assertion and unknown keys are rejected. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S14-CONF-022 | agent-evidence.md §4 Area 8 F-20 (restrictions and grants are treated differently across the precedence stack); S6-SETTINGS-050 | The settings precedence is tested exhaustively: for each pair of scopes and each key kind (list, scalar, restrict-only), the merged result is asserted against the documented rule (list keys merge, scalars replace, restrictions never widen). | S6-SETTINGS-050, S2-SETTINGS-011 | `config/loader.py :: merge(layers) -> Settings`; `test_settings_precedence.py::test_precedence_table`. | `test_settings_precedence.py::test_precedence_table` — a table-driven test over (scope pair, key kind). | Precedence bugs are the classic configuration vulnerability. Mitigation: the table is exhaustive and data-driven. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S14-HOT-023 | agent-evidence.md §4 X2 (hot reload and the `ConfigChange` hook); area-interactive-mode/_section-interactive-mode.md §2 F-I1 (hot-reloaded keybindings) | Settings files are hot-reloaded: a change is detected without a restart, a `ConfigChange` hook fires, and the change takes effect at the next tool-call boundary rather than mid-call. | S1-INPUT-027, S6-MODE-004 | `config/watcher.py :: watch(paths, on_change)` emitting a `ConfigChange` event; the session applying the new configuration at the next boundary. | `test_hot_reload.py::test_change_applied_at_next_boundary` and `::test_config_change_hook_fires`. | A configuration change applied mid-call could change the permission decision for a call already in flight. Mitigation: application is deferred to the boundary and tested. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 3 |
| S14-INT-024 | ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION (no explicit evidence of an internal consistency check across the products subsystems; proposed as the mechanism that keeps the fifteen subsystems aligned) | A **cross-subsystem consistency suite** asserts the invariants that span subsystems: the tool list published in the init event equals the set the dispatcher can route, the permission engine's deny set equals the tool-removal set, the model's capability set equals the tools that depend on it, and the transcript's recorded events equal the events emitted. | S1-TOOLSET-022, S6-RULE-011, S13-CAP-010 | `tests/consistency.py :: consistency_checks()` returning a list of named checks; `test_consistency.py::test_all_consistent`. | `test_consistency.py::test_all_consistent` — assert each named check holds after a scripted session. | Cross-subsystem drift is invisible from inside either subsystem. Mitigation: one suite that crosses the boundaries and is run on every change. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |


---

## S15 — REA-based investigation workflows

**Priority band:** P2 safety-tier.

**Evidence gap (read this first).** This subsystem is the thinnest in the document, and the reason is structural rather than incidental. The REA (reverse-engineering agent) tooling was deliberately **not** used against the reference product's own binary: the compliance boundary recorded in `agent-evidence.md` §2 and §12 prohibits decompiling Claude Code, and the investigation honours that boundary. Consequently the only *observed* REA facts are:

1. the transport and lifecycle of the `rea` MCP server, from `agent-reference-shots/mcp-debug-log.txt` and the `claude mcp list` / `claude mcp get` output — a stdio server launched as `cmd.exe /d /c npx -y rea-agents@6.7.0 mcp`, with 207 tools;
2. the documented contract of the `reverse-engineer-anything` skill installed at `~/.claude/skills/reverse-engineer-anything/SKILL.md` — `metadata.version: "42"`, `tool_count: 207`, the routing rule, the per-artifact tool selection, the CLI fallback and the readiness/setup commands;
3. the Windows capture limitation — a PTY capture is unavailable on native Windows;
4. the two research clones (`research/rea/`, `research/spl/`) that are partial and blobless with no working-tree files.

Everything else in this subsystem is therefore an **ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION**. The rows are still specified precisely, because a clean-room implementation needs an investigation capability whether or not the reference has one; but they are proposals, not parity claims.

**Scope note.** S15 is the *investigation* capability: opening an artifact, navigating it, gathering evidence, and exporting a defensible finding. It is the tool the team uses to satisfy the "do not invent behaviour" rule for the rest of the document.
| ID | Evidence | Acceptance criterion (testable) | Depends on | Proposed module + interface shape | Automated verification test | Security / operational risk + mitigation | Priority / Phase |
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
| S15-MCP-001 | agent-reference-shots/mcp-debug-log.txt; agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-1 | The REA capability is delivered as an MCP server over **stdio**, launched on Windows as `cmd.exe /d /c npx -y rea-agents@6.7.0 mcp`; the server is registered in the MCP configuration at user scope. | S8-TRANSPORT-001, S8-SCOPE-004 | an MCP server entry of type `stdio` with the documented command and arguments, registered through the standard MCP configuration path. | an integration test that launches the server, performs the MCP handshake and asserts the tool count is reported by the server. | The server is launched by an unpinned package specifier at run time. Mitigation: the version is pinned in the configuration and the resolved version is recorded in the audit log. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S15-MCP-002 | agent-reference-shots/mcp-debug-log.txt | The stdio server's connection lifecycle is observable: a handshake, a tool listing, and a clean shutdown; a failed launch is reported as a server-level failure without aborting the session. | S7-MCP-020, S8-TRANSPORT-003 | the standard MCP stdio client path, with the server's failure recorded in the init event's `mcp_servers` status and its startup error field. | a test that launches the server, lists tools and shuts it down, asserting the status transitions. | A server that crashes on launch must not break the session. Mitigation: the failure is isolated and reported (S8-ISOLATE-014). | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S15-TOOL-003 | ~/.claude/skills/reverse-engineer-anything/SKILL.md (`tool_count: 207`); the available REA tool surface | The REA tool surface is large (207 tools in the observed build) and the skill's documented routing rule is to **choose the tool that directly answers the question** and to discover targets only when needed — a discipline the implementation must support rather than contradict. | S8-DEFER-012 | the REA tools are exposed through the same deferred-tool mechanism as any other MCP server, so the 207 tools do not have to occupy the initial context. | a test asserting the deferred-loading behaviour for the REA server specifically. | A 207-tool catalogue in the initial schema would dominate the context budget. Mitigation: deferral is the mechanism, and it is tested. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S15-OPEN-004 | ~/.claude/skills/reverse-engineer-anything/SKILL.md (the `open_binary` and per-artifact routing rules); agent-evidence.md §4 Area 9 F9-11 | An artifact is **opened** before analysis, and the opened target persists across turns so that a multi-turn investigation does not re-open it; the tool surface is partitioned by artifact class (native binary, JavaScript/Electron bundle, Android package, managed assembly, firmware, browser target, network capture). | S15-TOOL-003 | an open/close session model where the opened target is referenced by handle and the close operation can export a snapshot. | a test that opens a fixture artifact, runs two analyses across separate turns and asserts the target is still open, then closes it. | An open target holds file handles and can keep a modified image in memory. Mitigation: targets are closed deterministically at session end and the close path is tested. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S15-EVID-005 | ~/.claude/skills/reverse-engineer-anything/SKILL.md (the documented per-tool contract: results carry inline Evidence and report their coverage and limitations); the REA tool descriptions in the available tool list | Every analysis result carries inline evidence and **states its coverage and limitations** — a result that cannot say what it did not examine is not usable as a basis for a claim. | S15-OPEN-004, S15-EXPORT-006 | the analysis-result envelope carrying an evidence block, a coverage statement and a limitations list; the harness surfaces the limitations to the operator. | a test that runs an analysis on a fixture and asserts the result contains a non-empty evidence block and an explicit limitations entry. | A result that hides its own uncertainty leads to over-claiming in the very place the compliance rule says to be careful. Mitigation: the limitations field is mandatory and is asserted to be present. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S15-EXPORT-006 | the available REA tool surface (an evidence-bundle export/import pair is present in the observed tool list) | Evidence can be **exported** from an analysis into a bundle and a bundle can be imported back, so a finding is reviewable by another person without re-running the analysis. | S15-EVID-005 | an export operation producing a portable bundle (with a manifest of what was examined) and an import operation that restores it. | a test that exports a bundle, imports it in a clean session and asserts the restored evidence matches the exported manifest. | An exported bundle may contain sensitive material from the analysed artifact. Mitigation: the bundle is written under the config root with restrictive permissions and is subject to the redaction rules (S14-REDACT-008). | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S15-UNTRUST-007 | the available REA tool surface (results that carry target content are marked as untrusted content in the observed tool metadata) | Content that originates inside an analysed target — strings, decompiled code, page text, file contents — is marked as **untrusted content** and is never treated as instructions, however imperative it reads. | S12-UX-015, S14-INJ-012, S10-AGENT-004 | the tool-result pipeline applies the same untrusted-content marking used for delegated reports, keyed on the tool's declared provenance. | a test that analyses a fixture containing an instruction-shaped string and asserts the result is marked untrusted and is not surfaced as an instruction. | This is the single highest-risk row in S15: analysed artifacts are attacker-controlled by definition. Mitigation: the marking is applied by provenance, not by inspection of the content, and it is tested. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S15-CLI-008 | ~/.claude/skills/reverse-engineer-anything/SKILL.md (the documented CLI fallback: `npx -y rea-agents@6.7.0 analyze-javascript-application /absolute/path --json`, the readiness probe `doctor --client <id> --json`, and `setup --dry-run --json` followed by `setup --yes`) | A CLI fallback exists for every analysis entry point, taking an **absolute path** and emitting JSON on stdout, and the setup path is a two-step dry-run-then-confirm sequence. | S15-OPEN-004, S5-CMD-010 | a thin CLI wrapper over the same analysis engine, invoked with an absolute path and `--json`, and a setup command that always offers `--dry-run` first. | a test that invokes the fallback with an absolute path and asserts the stdout parses as JSON, and that the setup path refuses to install without the confirmation step. | The setup path must never install packages behind the operator's back. Mitigation: the setup tool's own documented contract — that it never installs Node, npm, Homebrew, Java, Ghidra, IDA, JADX, Kuna, Binwalk or Unblob — is preserved and asserted. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S15-READY-009 | ~/.claude/skills/reverse-engineer-anything/SKILL.md (the readiness probe and the `doctor --client <id> --json` command) | A **readiness probe** reports whether the analysis backend is ready before an investigation starts, so a missing dependency is diagnosed up front rather than at the first analysis. | S15-CLI-008 | a readiness check returning a structured result (`ready`, `missing: list[str]`, `versions: dict[str,str]`) and rendered into the operator-facing status. | a test that runs the probe with a dependency absent and asserts the missing dependency is named. | A probe that runs arbitrary setup as a side effect would violate the dry-run contract. Mitigation: the probe is read-only and the test asserts it changes nothing. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S15-WIN-010 | the observed Windows capture limitation recorded in agent-evidence.md §12 / the REA skill's platform notes (a PTY capture is unavailable on native Windows) | The **Windows capture limitation** is explicit: a PTY-style process capture is not available on native Windows, and the tooling degrades to the artefact-level analysis paths rather than pretending to capture. | S15-OPEN-004, S14-PLAT-020 | a platform capability probe in the analysis layer that reports the unavailable capture path and selects the supported alternative. | a test that runs the capability probe on the current platform and asserts the degraded path is selected and reported. | Silently substituting a different capture method would produce evidence that does not match what the operator was told. Mitigation: the substitution is reported explicitly and asserted. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S15-COMPL-011 | agent-evidence.md §2 and §12 (the recorded compliance boundary: the reference product's own binary was not decompiled, and the boundary forbids using REA against Claude Code itself) | A **compliance boundary is enforced in the tooling**: the analysis capability refuses a target that is the reference product itself, and the refusal is recorded. The reimplementation's own artefact is likewise excluded from the tool's default targets. | S15-UNTRUST-007, S15-EXPORT-006 | a target allow/deny policy in the analysis layer, with the reference product's artefact and the reimplementation's own build excluded by default; a refusal is a typed error. | a test that attempts to open an excluded target and asserts the typed refusal and that the attempt is recorded. | This is the rule that keeps the whole programme clean-room. Mitigation: the exclusion is enforced by the tooling and not by convention, and it is tested. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
| S15-WORK-012 | ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION (the reference product's own REA workflow integration is not characterised beyond the MCP server and the installed skill; the following specifies the discipline the team applies when using it to answer an evidence question) | An investigation is **recorded as a finding** with a stable id, the question asked, the artefacts examined, the tools used, the evidence obtained, the coverage and limitations reported by the tooling, and an explicit statement of what remains unknown — and the finding is the *only* thing permitted to change a row in this checklist from ORIGINAL DESIGN DECISION to a cited observation. | S15-EVID-005, S14-AUDIT-013 | `investig/finding.py :: Finding(id, question, artifacts, tools, evidence, coverage, limitations, unknowns)` persisted under the findings directory; `investig/finding.py :: promotes(checklist_row_id)` requiring a non-empty evidence block. | `test_findings.py::test_promotion_requires_evidence` and `::test_finding_has_unknowns_section`. | An unrecorded investigation is indistinguishable from a guess, which is the failure mode the evidence discipline exists to prevent. Mitigation: promotion is a function that refuses without evidence, and it is tested. | P2 safety-tier / Phase 4 |
